# user_stories.md — Cartograph

**Schema (fixed):**
`ID · Title · Actor · Narrative(As a / I want / So that) · Priority(MoSCoW) · Status · Acceptance Criteria(AC-XXXX, Given/When/Then) · Security · Performance · Trace(Milestone, Crate, Flow?, Test IDs)`

**Status enum:** `Draft · Ready · In-Progress · In-Review · Blocked · Done · Deferred`

> Convention: Security and Performance facts are mapped onto the owning US/AC. Trace binds each story to a milestone, the owning crate(s), and test IDs (see `US-TM.md`).

---

### US-0001 — Add a repo set and define system topology
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** to add 1..N GitHub repos and declare which repos form one system **so that** the engine treats them as a single analyzable whole.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0001** Given valid GitHub App/PAT auth, when I add a repo URL, then it is cloned read-only and listed with commit SHA. The token comes from the auth ladder (ADR-0009): `GH_TOKEN`, then `GITHUB_TOKEN`, then `gh auth token`. `gh` is found on `PATH` or, for a desktop app launched from Finder or a launcher with a minimal `PATH`, at the standard Homebrew and Linuxbrew install paths. No token means anonymous, so only public repos can be cloned.
- **AC-0002** Given a multi-repo set, when I edit `cartograph.system.toml`, then declared layer hints and channel identities are applied at ingest. Guided new-manifest creation (#1671, in progress) preserves exact repository/configuration identities and explicit service/gateway declarations, previews validated TOML and saves only on request. Service base URL lines are trimmed and blank lines omitted for preview and save while the editable draft is retained. Duplicate configuration keys and invalid bindings are refused. Saving never overwrites an existing manifest or starts recovery; cancellation and failure retain the draft.
- **AC-0003** Given an unauthorized repo, when I add it, then I get a clear auth-failure with remediation and no partial clone. The remediation says whether a credential was found and refused (check its repo access) or none was found (sign in with `gh auth login` or set `GH_TOKEN`).
- **AC-0049** Given any completed ingest, when its summary is shown, then TypeScript and Terraform each report source-file, node, and edge counts including explicit zeros.
- **AC-0050** Given a non-empty graph, when I request a clear and confirm the warning, then all graph facts are removed while durable job history remains intact.
- **AC-0076** Given accumulated terminal jobs, when I clear finished jobs and confirm the warning, then done/failed/cancelled rows are removed while queued/running/resumable interrupted jobs and all graph facts remain intact (an ended investigation's interrupted row is cleared per AC-0202).
- **AC-0077** Given the production Jobs surface, when it renders, then it offers only lifecycle verbs on existing work (Cancel/Retry/Resume and Clear finished) — no job-creation control.
- **AC-0078** Given any recovery command (ingest, add-repo, add-system, or an ingest retry/resume), when it runs, then extraction executes on a blocking worker thread — the webview/main thread never blocks, the UI stays interactive, and job progress renders throughout.
- **AC-0085** Given one or more ingested targets, when the Workspace renders, then it states what the current system contains — each repo with its commit identity, derived from the graph's own facts (never from history logs, which survive a clear) — and that further ingests merge into the same system; the destructive action is labeled in system terms (Clear system) and its confirmation names exactly what is removed (every recovered fact) and what survives (job history and settings). The shell breadcrumb reflects evidence-backed system contents after startup or refresh even without an in-memory ingest summary; an empty, cleared, or evidence-less graph shows No system, regardless of raw node counts or retained job history.
- **AC-0094** Given a running recovery job (ingest, add-repo, or add-system), when I navigate away from the Recovering screen or run it in background, then it stays reachable from the status bar ("Ingesting…") and from a "View live" action on its row in the Jobs surface; while it runs, both surfaces show a best-effort, non-persisted live detail of the file and layer (application/infra/language) currently being read, throttled so it never floods the event bridge.
- **AC-0195** Given an extraction in which a node id or a (source, destination, label) relation occurs more than once — one relation cited from several call or import sites, or distinct declarations sharing one id — when it is ingested (direct, add-repo, or add-system), then the graph keeps exactly one fact per node id and per relation, the summary's node and edge totals are the distinct facts the whole operation published — the union across every repo it loaded (Repo nodes included) and every later whole-graph stage (observed BACKS stitching, found-ADR relinking), less any fact those stages retracted — so a first ingest into an empty graph quotes the same totals as the Workspace graph counts, each per-layer row counts distinct facts, and the collapsed occurrences, including a globally keyed fact published by more than one repo, are reported explicitly as merged nodes, merged edges, and node identity collisions (one id carrying differing facts) — never silently.
- **AC-0196** Given an ingest summary, when the IngestCard renders it, then merged occurrences and node identity collisions are stated beside the totals whenever either is non-zero, and no merge line appears when both are zero.
- **AC-0197** Given a local preflight, when it scans a repository, then the scan runs on a blocking worker thread rather than the invoking webview/main thread, and the Preflight surface shows live, throttled progress naming the file being read and its position in the walk — and, while the tree is still being listed, the directory being entered and how many files have been found so far.
- **AC-0198** Given a running preflight, when I cancel it (or start another preflight, which supersedes it, or switch the source away from a local tree, or change the local path, which abandons it), then it stops before reading the next file (or, while the tree is still being listed, before entering the next directory), persists no findings (AC-0215), reports that it was cancelled rather than a raw error (an abandoned scan reports nothing), and a superseded or abandoned result never replaces the current one.
- **AC-0215** Given a preflight whose register write is already in progress, when it is cancelled, superseded or abandoned, then exactly one of the two wins, decided atomically at the last step before the write commits and without Cancel ever waiting on the write: if the cancel lands first, the write is rolled back inside its transaction, the register keeps the rows it held before the write, and the scan reports that it was cancelled; if the write has already claimed its commit, the cancel loses, the findings are committed, and the scan completes and reports its result, never a cancellation (an explicit Cancel then shows that result; a superseded or abandoned scan's result is still ignored by run id).
- **AC-0202** Given an investigation ended by owner death, whose Job row is `interrupted` and offers no Retry/Resume, when I clear finished jobs and confirm, then that row is removed — the confirmation counts it — while its task keeps its interrupted or outcome-unknown status and journal in investigation history; the row is never relabelled done or failed, and resumable interrupted jobs are kept.
- **AC-0203** Given an extraction that cites one (source, destination, label) relation from several sites, when it is ingested, then the stored edge keeps the props, tier and confidence of the last occurrence in deterministic extraction order and cites the evidence spans of every occurrence with that same tier, confidence and extractor — sorted, de-duplicated and bounded, with any spans beyond the bound counted on the edge rather than dropped silently — and whenever more than one occurrence is unioned its content hash is derived from their distinct fact hashes together with the canonical span list (spans beyond the bound and their count included), so adding, removing or moving any cited site changes the hash, a single-site relation keeps its extracted hash; a collapsed relation publishes without a primary-source receipt binding, since no single receipt attested it; and re-ingesting the same commit yields an identical edge and graph hash.
- **AC-0224** Given the Connect step, when it opens with a target kept from an earlier visit, then the target field is focused with its whole value selected, so typing replaces rather than appends to it; pressing Enter in the field submits the preflight exactly when the Preflight button is enabled (never for a blank target or without a backend); and when the preflight then ends without a report (an error such as a missing path, or a cancel), the Preflight step says it did not complete and demotes Run full recovery from the primary action — it stays available, since recovery scans the target itself and fails closed if it cannot read it.
- **Security:** Tokens stored in OS keychain; never logged; least-privilege App scopes.
- **Performance:** Shallow/sparse clone; 1 GB repo clones within bounded progress feedback.
- **Trace:** M0–M3 · `ingest`, `core-graph`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0001..0003,T-0049..0050,T-0076..0078,T-0085,T-0094,T-0195..0198,T-0202..0203,T-0224

### US-0002 — Deterministic extraction of server-side facts (TS/JS/Python/Go/Java/Kotlin)
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** import/call graphs, endpoints, and data access extracted statically **so that** server facts are Confirmed without inference.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0004** Given a TS repo, when ingested, then endpoints (method/path/handler) are extracted via the framework adapter and marked Confirmed.
- **AC-0005** Given typed TS, when building call edges, then intra-procedural edges are complete and inter-procedural edges resolve where types permit.
- **AC-0006** Given any extracted fact, when inspected, then it carries provenance (file/span/commit, tier, extractor_id).
- **AC-0053** Given Python that import-proves FastAPI or Flask, when the repo is ingested, then Confirmed T0 File/Symbol/IMPORTS/CALLS facts plus literal Endpoint/HANDLES registrations are recovered with exact evidence, directory-proven imported calls are joined deterministically, lookalike framework calls are ignored, and the ingest summary reports Python separately.
- **AC-0054** Given Go that import-proves `net/http`, chi, or gin, when the repo is ingested, then Confirmed T0 File/Symbol/IMPORTS/CALLS facts plus literal Endpoint registrations are recovered with exact evidence, local-package calls and handlers are joined deterministically, unresolved handler expressions retain the Endpoint with an explicit HANDLES Gap, dynamic routes and lookalike registrations are ignored, files requiring an undeclared build target are excluded, application layer hints are honored (server, events or client; since AC-0434 Go also carries client calls), and the ingest summary reports Go separately.
- **AC-0079** Given a Java repository, when it is ingested, then classes/interfaces/enums/records and methods become Confirmed T0 Symbols with exact evidence spans, same-class and import-proven cross-file calls are joined deterministically repo-wide, a declared-package import whose target cannot be proven fails closed to an explicit Gap, calls on foreign-package imports assert nothing (the import itself closes over a proven external boundary, AC-0207), and the ingest summary reports Java separately.
- **AC-0080** Given Java that import-proves Spring Web annotations (named or wildcard `org.springframework.*` imports), when a `@RestController`/`@Controller` class is parsed, then `@{Get,Post,Put,Delete,Patch}Mapping` methods become Confirmed Endpoints with class-level `@RequestMapping` path composition and HANDLES edges to their handler methods; lookalike annotations without the proving import produce no endpoints.
- **AC-0095** Given a `.js`/`.jsx`/`.mjs`/`.cjs` repo, when ingested, then the same TypeScript-crate grammar recovers Confirmed T0 File/Symbol/IMPORTS/CALLS facts and Express/Next/React-Router endpoints and screens as it does for `.ts`/`.tsx` (no separate JS adapter, and Preflight/Settings report JavaScript as installed rather than a requestable planned adapter); an extensionless relative import resolves to the real file and symbol regardless of its actual source extension once the whole directory is known, rather than a phantom `.ts`-guessed placeholder.
- **AC-0098** Given a Kotlin repository (`.kt`/`.kts`), when it is ingested, then classes, interfaces, objects, data classes, enums, and functions (top-level, member, and extension) become Confirmed T0 Symbols with exact evidence spans; same-scope and import-proven cross-file calls (type/object receivers and imported top-level functions) are joined deterministically repo-wide, with a declared-package import whose target cannot be proven — or is declared ambiguously more than once — failing closed to an explicit Gap; an import of an extension function (overloads included), top-level property, type alias, annotation class (including the grammar's expression-shaped parse of an annotated one) or enum entry resolves to its declaring File when exactly one file declares it; a call constructing an imported class or data class that exactly one file declares, and that declares no `invoke`, is a constructor call, which (as for a same-file class) is not modeled and raises no Gap, while an ambiguous class, an object's or interface's `invoke` call (wherever the operator is declared), a class declaring `invoke`, or a member the source never declares (a generated `X.serializer()`), still gaps, and a same-named factory function still resolves; calls on foreign packages and unproven receivers asserting nothing (a foreign import itself closes over a proven external boundary, AC-0207); import-proven Spring Web annotations compose class-level `@RequestMapping` with `@{Get,Post,Put,Delete,Patch}Mapping` into Confirmed Endpoint/HANDLES facts (lookalikes without the proving import produce no endpoints, and a present-but-non-literal path — on the method or the class base — fails closed to an explicit route Gap instead of a Confirmed endpoint); Preflight/Settings report Kotlin as installed (`t0.adapter-kotlin`) rather than a requestable planned adapter; and the ingest summary reports Kotlin separately.
- **AC-0099** Given a TS/JS source with an `eval()` or `new Function()` whose code argument is compile-time-known — a string literal, a substitution-free template, or a same-file `const`/const-object member proven by binding (never a name coincidence; for `new Function` the last string argument is the body and earlier ones are parameter names), then the string content is parsed by the same extractor and its Symbols, CALLS, and event sites are emitted as Confirmed T0 facts carrying `via: "eval"` with evidence citing the argument's span at the eval site, the enclosing symbol gains a CALLS edge into the extracted code's entry symbol so flows cross the eval boundary, and recovery reconciles Preflight's `inline-eval` findings from that same extraction (adapter-supplied claims, so the textual scan and the AST proof never disagree; AC-0199/AC-0200): a fully proven site's finding closes, while a const-shaped-but-unproven argument downgrades to an explicit potential Gap and any other dynamic argument — including a shadowed local `eval`/`Function` binding, which is not the global and yields no facts — stays an Unsupported finding, never a guessed fact. Every eval-recovered fact's content hash is derived from its final namespaced form (a node's label and id, an edge's label and endpoints), so the same code at two eval sites never yields distinct facts sharing one hash, and re-ingest stays deterministic.
- **AC-0100** Given how Node/TS actually resolve modules, when the directory-wide reconciliation runs, then (a) an extensionless relative import matching no file resolves through the `index.<ext>` directory convention, (b) an explicit `.js`/`.jsx` relative specifier with no such file on disk resolves to its `.ts`/`.tsx` sibling (the NodeNext idiom), (c) a bare specifier matching a tsconfig/jsconfig `paths` alias or explicit `baseUrl` resolves to the real file with the deciding config cited as provenance evidence, and (d) a bare specifier naming a workspace package inside the ingested tree resolves through that package's `exports`/`module`/`main` map with its `package.json` cited; every resolution is a proof against files that exist — unmatched specifiers keep their explicit `mod:` node or placeholder, and external packages are never resolved into. The tsconfig parse is shared with the toolchain detector's stored facts (parse once, `adapters-fw::tsconfig`).
- **AC-0192** Given a proven Spring mapping in Java, when its path is a string literal or an array initializer of string literals (`@GetMapping({ "/vets" })`, `value = { "/a", "/b" }`), then each literal becomes its own Confirmed Endpoint composed with the class base, and an empty or absent path takes Spring's default; when the path on the method or the class-level `@RequestMapping` is present but not provably literal (a constant, a concatenation, any expression), then the mapping yields an explicit route Gap bound to its handler and no Endpoint — never a fabricated `/`.
- **AC-0199** Given a local preflight, when it scans TS/JS sources, then it performs no TS extraction (no AST parse, #243) and reports every textual `eval(`/`new Function(` line as an Unsupported `inline-eval` finding marked pending AST proof at recovery — none is claimed closed or downgraded, and none is dropped.
- **AC-0200** Given a completed local recovery, when its TS extraction has classified the eval sites, then the register's preflight findings for that repo are replaced by the textual scan of the captured bytes the claims were proven on (not the live tree), reconciled with those claims (AC-0099), so the register and the Preflight surface (which receives the reconciled report, potential-Gap lane included, with the recovery's summary) quote the post-recovery classification and no pending wording survives. The reconciliation scan runs only after the recovered facts are published, and its write goes through the per-repo fence only once the recovery's job has settled as completed, so a recovery that fails earlier, or is cancelled at any point before it completes (even during the reconciliation scan), leaves the pending findings in place; a preflight of that repo that began before the reconciliation cannot overwrite it afterwards, while a preflight started after recovery is newer and reports the lines as pending again until the next recovery (attested reuse of the recovery's proof is #438).
- **AC-0201** Given a TS/JS `eval()` or `new Function()` whose code argument is const-shaped (an identifier or member expression) but not provable to a literal, when the file is extracted, then the site becomes exactly one explicit `Gap` node — `attempted_tiers: ["T0"]`, a reason stating the argument could not be proven to a literal, and Gap-tier provenance citing the call site plus every unproven argument (inside proven eval code, the one outer-literal span instead, AC-0213) — owned through `DEPENDS_ON` by its enclosing symbol (or its File at top level), with an id keyed by repo, path and site offset so the same source yields the same Gap on every run and in the captured production lane; nothing about the site is asserted as Confirmed, and proven or dynamic sites emit no such Gap; a proven `eval()` whose code itself holds such a site is claimed const-unproven rather than covered, so Preflight keeps its potential-Gap finding, and a nested Gap's content hash derives from its final namespaced id.
- **AC-0209** Given a completed GitHub-backed (`add_repo`) or system-manifest (`add_system`) recovery, when its TS extraction has classified the eval sites, then each recovered repo's register preflight findings are replaced exactly as a local recovery's are (AC-0200): the textual scan of the captured bytes, reconciled with that repo's claims (AC-0099), scanned only after the recovered facts are published and written through the per-repo fence only once the recovery has completed. The recovery summary carries the reconciled report — a single report for a GitHub add, one per repo in manifest order for a system add. A recovery that fails or is cancelled, including a system recovery whose later repo fails after an earlier one recovered, writes no repo's findings.
- **AC-0214** Given a local, GitHub-backed or system-manifest recovery, when it publishes its job as completed, then its reconciliation's place in the per-repo fence (AC-0200, AC-0209) is already reserved and every source it wrote is ready and released, so a preflight of that repo started after the job shows done can read it and is always newer than the reconciliation: if that preflight persists first, the reconciliation writes nothing over it, and if it persists afterwards it is not refused. A preflight begun before the reservation still cannot overwrite the reconciled findings, and a recovery whose cancel wins the race still writes nothing.
- **AC-0216** Given preflight reports and recovery reports for the same repo that settle in any order — a preflight that overlapped a recovery, or a recovery re-run from Jobs → Retry — when they reach the Preflight surface, then the register order decides which is shown, not the order they arrive in: every register write carries its epoch; a preflight that a recovery reconciled past answers with the reconciled report the register holds instead of reporting a cancellation; a recovery's report replaces the shown report when no preflight began since the recovery started or when the shown report is the same repo's, and never when its reconciliation wrote nothing because a newer preflight's findings, or an overlapping recovery's newer reconciliation of that repo, were already there; the surface never shows an older report for a repo than the newest it has received. A retried recovery returns its reconciled report with the retried job row, applied under the same rule.
- **AC-0212** Given a proven TS/JS `eval()` or `new Function()` whose decoded code itself holds further eval sites, when the file is extracted, then the outer site's claim is the worst of its nested claims — dynamic over const-unproven over covered — so a nested dynamic site (for example `eval("eval(x + 1)")`) keeps the line's Unsupported `inline-eval` finding in Preflight even beside a const-unproven one, while the facts recovered from the proven outer code stay Confirmed T0.
- **AC-0220** Given a Vite or webpack config (`vite.config.*`, `webpack.config.*`) in the ingested tree, when bare JS/TS specifiers are resolved and classified, then the config is read as data and never executed: each literal `resolve.alias` key (object form, Vite's `{ find, replacement }` array form with a string `find`, and, in webpack configs only, the exact-match `key$` — a Vite key keeps a trailing `$` as spelled) whose replacement is provably a filesystem path — a relative string literal, `path.resolve`/`path.join` over `__dirname` and string literals, or `fileURLToPath(new URL('…', import.meta.url))` — governs files beneath the config's directory; a specifier it addresses (the key itself, or `key/…` unless exact) resolves to the real file only when the alias provably belongs to the exported config (the file's only `resolve.alias` object, inside the object given to `export default`, `module.exports =`, or `defineConfig(…)`), every same-directory config addressing it agrees on key and target, and the literal replacement proves a file — with the config's key span cited as provenance evidence — and otherwise stays an explicit Gap whose reason names the key and the config, never a Confirmed external package. Keys aliased to another package name, computed keys, regex `find`s, and non-literal replacements are skipped and keep their AC-0207 classification.
- **AC-0213** Given a fact recovered from proven `eval()` / `new Function()` code whose evidence cites several spans inside the decoded code (such as a nested const-unproven eval Gap and its `DEPENDS_ON` edge, which cite the inner call and each unproven argument), when the file is extracted, then — decoded offsets not being mapped back into the escaped literal — its provenance cites the outer string argument's span exactly once, never N identical copies, and the fact carries `unmapped_inner_spans` with the number of inner spans left unmapped; facts with a single evidence span carry no marker.
- **Security:** No code leaves device at T0.
- **Performance:** Incremental tree-sitter parse; re-parse only changed files by `content_hash`.

- **AC-0206** Given a proven Spring mapping in Java or Kotlin, when the class-level `@RequestMapping` base and the method mapping are composed, then segments meet at exactly one `/` (never `//`) and a trailing slash spelled in either literal is kept (a run of trailing slashes as one) — `"/api/"` with a bare `@GetMapping` is `/api/` and `"/api"` with `@GetMapping("/")` is `/api/` — so `/a` and `/a/` are distinct Confirmed Endpoints, matching Spring 6's default of no trailing-slash matching; a client fetch path that differs from a route only by a trailing slash never confirms against it.

- **AC-0557** Given a proven Spring controller in Kotlin, when a function carries a function-level `@RequestMapping` (#1605), then it becomes an Endpoint per proven verb and route. Its verbs are the union of the class-level and function-level `method =` restrictions, as Spring combines them (verb shorthands included). An empty union serves every method (`ALL`). Each proven `RequestMethod.X` (single, `[…]` or `arrayOf(…)`, qualified, imported or import-aliased) gives that verb, and a non-empty literal path array (`value = ["/a", "/b"]`) gives one route per element composed with the class base as AC-0206 does. An unprovable verb at either level (a constant, a spread, an imported enum entry, a lookalike `RequestMethod`) becomes a route Gap with method `?`, and a dynamic path becomes one route Gap naming its verbs, never a guessed method. A lookalike `RequestMapping` with no Spring import proves nothing.
- **Trace:** M1, M10 · `adapters-lang-kotlin` · — · T-0557

- **AC-0555** Given a proven Spring controller in Java, when a handler carries a method-level `@RequestMapping` (#1598), then it becomes an Endpoint per proven verb and route: its verbs are the union of the class-level and method-level `method =` restrictions (as Spring combines them, verb shorthands included), an empty union (`method` absent or `{}` at both levels) serves every method (`ALL`), each proven `RequestMethod.X` (single or array) gives that verb, and the path composes with the class base as AC-0206 does. An unprovable verb at either level (static import, constant, lookalike `RequestMethod`) or a dynamic path becomes one route Gap naming its verbs, never a guessed method, and a lookalike `RequestMapping` with no Spring import proves nothing. No proven handler is silently dropped.

- **AC-0597** Given source in any built-in language adapter (TypeScript, JavaScript, Python, Go, Java, Kotlin, Rust), when it is ingested, then each standard-library read of an environment variable with a literal name (`process.env.X`, `os.environ[...]`, `os.environ.get`, `os.getenv`, `os.Getenv`, `os.LookupEnv`, `System.getenv`, `std::env::var`, `std::env::var_os`) yields one `ConfigRead` node per file and key, T0 Confirmed, citing the span of every read, with a `READS_CONFIG` edge from each enclosing symbol or the file. A read whose key is not a recordable literal name yields one unnamed dynamic node per file. A look-alike that is not provably the standard-library read yields nothing. Only the key name is stored, never a value. Existing `env_key` resources are unchanged, the same tree yields the same facts, each adapter's coverage cell states what it does not read, and a `ConfigRead` node is never offered as a candidate target for a gap.

- **AC-0651** Given a graph with environment-file facts and `ConfigRead` facts, when a spec is exported, then `configuration.md` lists each key once per repository with the environment files that declare it and the files and symbols that read it, each fact row with tier and confidence, and a source span for every read (an environment-file fact stores no span, and the document says so); keys read and never declared and keys declared and never read are marked, and "never declared" is stated as meaning that no ingested environment file declares the key; files with reads whose key is not named are listed; the languages read and the reads not covered are stated; every row resolves in the fact index; `verified-only` leaves out InferredWeak rows and states how many, `best-effort` includes them marked; the same graph yields the same bytes; only names are shown. In the Atlas a `ConfigRead` is placed with the code that reads it and shows its key.

- **AC-0207** Given any T0 adapter (TS/JS, Python, Go, Java, Kotlin, Terraform) whose edges name an endpoint no parsed declaration defines, when it closes over that endpoint, then the placeholder node is never left without provenance: it cites the edge that named it (evidence span, the producing extractor), states a human-readable `reason`, and records its `boundary` (ADR-0031). It is Confirmed only when the adapter proves the boundary — an **external** import the repository provably cannot provide (a JVM package that no Java or Kotlin source declares, lies beneath, or encloses, with every header readable — multiline file annotations included; in Go module mode a standard-library path or a module outside every repository module and local `replace`; a Python top-level name no repository directory or module shares; a bare JS/TS specifier matching no tsconfig alias or `baseUrl` path, literal bundler `resolve.alias` key (AC-0220), workspace package, `#` subpath import, or repository source directory, citing the `package.json` that declares it when one does; under a tsconfig that `extends` another, whose inherited aliases are not loaded, only a manifest-declared dependency) or an **internal** repository-declared package (a JVM package, a Go package directory — beneath a `go.mod` or a local `replace` target resolved against its declaring `go.mod`, whose complete directory and proving Go file resolve inside the repository root (never lexically or through a symlink outside it), and never beneath a module path some version-qualified `replace` names, since the selected version is unknown — a workspace package cited by its manifest). Every other endpoint — an in-system import with no unique declaration, an unresolved relative import, symbol, or resource, or any import when the adapter cannot decide — stays an explicit Gap carrying that reason, so external dependencies never count as system gaps. a placeholder named by several references is Confirmed only when every reference proves the same boundary (one unresolved reference keeps it a Gap), including across adapters in a polyglot ingest (one adapter's Gap is never overwritten by another adapter's Confirmed placeholder for the same id); a JVM header scan reads Unicode annotation names and arguments without panicking, and fails closed to unknown on anything it cannot parse; JVM imports whose complete target (a type, a top-level function, or a member whose Symbol the type defines) the repository declares exactly once resolve to the declaring File, and a prefix alone never does; a Python absolute import resolves to the recovered File it names from the repository root, the root that directory-proven calls already assume (`a.b` is `a/b.py` or the package `a/b/__init__.py`), only when exactly one of the two exists and no parent is a plain module (`a.py` without `a/__init__.py`) that Python would load instead; a relative JS/TS import of a non-source asset (stylesheet, image, JSON) names that file rather than a `.ts`-guessed phantom and is internal when the file exists and the import does not climb above the repository root; an observed (T1) state resolution supersedes a Terraform placeholder's Gap provenance; and the Gap register classes placeholder gaps by their reason rather than an internal edge label.

- **AC-0340** Given a route handler whose route resolves to Flask (directly or through its Blueprint mounts), when the repo is ingested, then its Endpoint's `responses` records what the handler's own `return` statements prove, each read as `body` or `(body, status[, headers])`. - **AC-0479** Given a Kotlin product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each call `<client>.<verb>(<url>)`, optionally with a trailing request block, whose receiver is proven to be `io.ktor.client.HttpClient` (a named-imported or fully qualified typed or constructed local, parameter or property, or `this.x`) and whose function resolves to an `io.ktor.client.request` extension (a named import or an alias keeping the imported verb, when the file declares no function of that name; a wildcard import of the package proves nothing, since a same-package extension in another file wins over it) becomes a fetch site. The verbs `get`, `post`, `put`, `delete`, `patch`, `head` and `options` give their method and `request` gives `GET`; a block that names `method` gives `?`, and one that names `url` makes the URL computed. The URL is the single positional argument, a literal only as a pure string literal. A URL that is not an absolute literal joins a runtime base named by the client expression (a `defaultRequest` the file does not show), which the stitcher reports as an undeclared runtime base, resolved as an RFC 3986 reference. Block-only calls, named arguments, more than one argument, an unproven or foreign receiver, another package's function of a verb's name, a wildcard-only import and test sources yield nothing; a site with a request block cites the whole call including the block (#1416).
- **AC-0495** Given a Kotlin product source whose RestTemplate, WebClient, OkHttp, Ktor or Apache HttpClient request lies outside any function, in a property or top-level initializer (not an accessor, `init` block, constructor parameter default, property delegate or enum entry), when its application layers are ingested, then the request becomes a fetch site owned by its file (no symbol and no entry), with the same proof, method and URL reading as inside a function; a request inside a function stays owned by its innermost function (#1426).
- **AC-0442** Given a Kotlin product source whose Spring endpoint annotation (`@RestController`/`@Controller`, the class `@RequestMapping` base or the function mapping) is proven only by a star import, when the repository's product sources declare a same-package top-level type (class, interface, object, enum or annotation class) or type alias of that name, in Kotlin or, in a mixed JVM tree, in Java (Kotlin resolves explicit imports, then the package, then star imports), then that route becomes a route Gap whose reason names the shadowing, also when its path is dynamic. A controller stays proven while any proven stereotype is unshadowed, and a directly proven one dominates. A named import or the fully qualified spelling `{package}.{name}` proves directly, and any other qualified spelling proves nothing. Test source sets never obscure a product import, a `private` top-level Kotlin declaration obscures only within its own file, and default-package declarations obscure default-package files. Single-file extraction settles against the file's own declarations, and cached files keep their pending routes retargeted to the current commit (#1288).
- **AC-0443** Given a Kotlin source that uses `dynamic` or `suspend` as a name (a declared function, property or parameter, possibly after a comment, or a reference or call such as an expression body ending in `suspend`), or `dynamic` anywhere as a word, when it is parsed, then the soft keyword that the grammar lexes as a keyword no longer drops the declaration or, under stacked class annotations, the whole class. Its Symbols and endpoints are recovered with exact spans and source text, and a real `suspend` modifier and a `dynamic` type still parse as before (#1302).
- **AC-0470** Given a Kotlin source whose import list ends with a wildcard import (`import io.ktor.client.request.*`) directly followed by a `suspend` declaration, when it is parsed, then the soft-keyword pass keeps `suspend` as a modifier, because a `*` right after `.` ends a star import and opens no expression, so the declaration keeps its Symbol and facts; a `*` operator before `suspend` still marks a respelled name (#1417).
- **AC-0496** Given a Python repository that import-proves `aiohttp` as AC-0424 proves `requests`/`httpx` (no repository module or package named `aiohttp`, no patch that may mock the call), when its server layers are ingested, then `aiohttp.request(method, url)` becomes a fetch site read like `requests.request`, and a name bound once to `aiohttp.ClientSession(…)` under AC-0425's rules has its verb methods and `request(method, url)` read as fetch sites. Its `base_url`, the first positional argument or the `base_url=` keyword, read exactly as httpx's, becomes the site's base, and a URL is resolved against it as `ClientSession._build_url` does with `URL.join`, an RFC 3986 reference resolution (`https://h/api/` + `users` → `https://h/api/users`; `/users` → `https://h/users`); a URL with scheme and host stands alone. aiohttp has no module verb functions, so `aiohttp.get(…)` yields nothing, nor do splatted, rebound, patched or shadowed sessions (#1432).
- **AC-0444** Given a Kotlin source with a block comment (nested or not, possibly containing `//`) on the line before a declaration, including after an expression-bodied function, when it is parsed, then the declaration and its class are kept with exact spans: block comments are blanked to spaces in the same-length parse copy (line breaks kept), while string literals that look like comments are untouched (#1306).
- **AC-0456** Given a Kotlin source, when its application layers are ingested, then each call of a `RestTemplate` verb method on a receiver proven to be `org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate` becomes a fetch site owned by its innermost enclosing function, with the same verbs and methods as AC-0435, Spring's reified Kotlin extensions of those names included: a call with type arguments only when the file imports that extension from `org.springframework.web.client` by name, or by wildcard when no named import binds that name, and no call of a verb's name in a file that declares a function of that name (a same-package extension in another file is not seen). The receiver is proven by a typed local, parameter, constructor or class property, or `this.x`, whose stated type (or `RestTemplate(…)` initializer) is the fully qualified name or a name its named import binds to that type in a file that declares nothing of that name, or by an inline `RestTemplate()`. Star imports, another `RestTemplate`, an untyped or shadowing binding, an enclosing type parameter of the type's name, and a visible binding of a qualified constructor's first segment prove nothing. No Retrofit base joins these sites. `exchange`/`execute(url, method, …)` read an import-proven `HttpMethod.X` second argument; with four or more arguments an unproven method is `?`, and with two or three no site is made. A call with named arguments or a trailing lambda is not read, nor a one-argument `getForObject<T>(url)`, which the grammar parses as comparisons. The URL is the first argument, a literal only as a pure string literal (URI templates included, `$` templates not); any other URL is Computed.
- **AC-0457** Given a Kotlin source, when its application layers are ingested, then each `WebClient` request chain `<client>.<verb>().uri(<url>, …)` whose client is proven to be `org.springframework.web.reactive.function.client.WebClient` becomes a fetch site owned by its innermost enclosing function, with the entry `Type.function`. The client is proven as for AC-0456 by a typed local, parameter, constructor or class property, or `this.x` (an initializer alone proves nothing, since `WebClient` is an interface), or by an inline `WebClient.create(…)` or `WebClient.builder()…build()` whose qualifier no visible binding obscures. The verb is `get()`/`head()`/`post()`/`put()`/`patch()`/`delete()`/`options()`, or `method(HttpMethod.X)` with an import-proven constant, else `?`. The URL is the first `uri` argument, a literal only as a pure string literal, else Computed. The base is an inline `create("<literal>")` argument or the last `baseUrl("<literal>")` of an inline builder; a held client, or a builder with `uriBuilderFactory` or a lambda-configured link (`apply { … }`), has a runtime base named by the client expression; an absolute literal URL takes no base. No Retrofit base joins these sites. A builder chain proves a client only when every link is a `WebClient.Builder` member called in one of its shapes (argument count, a trailing lambda only for a functional parameter, and no non-string literal for a `String` parameter) and not declared or imported as a function by the file. A `uri(<url>, <function>)` call has a runtime URL. Named `uri` arguments, star imports, another `WebClient` and type parameters of its name prove nothing.
- **Trace:** M1,M10 · `adapters-lang-java`, `adapters-lang-kotlin`, `adapters-lang-rust`, `core-graph`, `core-prov`, `ingest`, `spec`, `app` · — · T-0004..0006,T-0053..0054,T-0079..0080,T-0095,T-0098..0100,T-0192,T-0199..0201,T-0230,T-0284,T-0310,T-0338,T-0360,T-0456,T-0457

### US-0003 — IaC resource graph + cloud capability resolution (Terraform/Pulumi/AWS)
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** a deterministic resource graph with cloud-capability edges **so that** infra/cloud topology is Confirmed.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0007** Given Terraform HCL, when parsed, then a resource DAG is built from interpolation references.
- **AC-0008** Given AWS resources, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then TRIGGERS/ROUTES/GRANTS edges are emitted deterministically.
- **AC-0009** Given Terraform state/plan JSON, when available, then T1 enrichment supersedes ambiguous T0 refs (observed provenance).
- **AC-0043** Given an API Gateway v1 integration with direct resource references, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then a Confirmed ROUTES edge links the REST API to its integration target.
- **AC-0044** Given a Lambda permission with direct resource references, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then a Confirmed TRIGGERS edge links its source ARN to the Lambda function.
- **AC-0045** Given Lambda@Edge associations nested under default or ordered CloudFront cache behaviors, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then Confirmed TRIGGERS edges link the distribution to every referenced Lambda function.
- **AC-0046** Given an EventBridge Pipe with direct resource references, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then a Confirmed TRIGGERS edge links its source to its target.
- **AC-0047** Given an IAM policy that references an `aws_iam_policy_document` defined in the same extraction, when its statement resources are resolvable, then Confirmed GRANTS edges target those resources with actions and evidence from the document; an absent or unresolved document target remains explicit.
- **AC-0048** Given a Terraform module with a literal local source confined to the ingest root, when extracted, then its resources and internal edges are instantiated under the `module.<name>.` address prefix recursively and deterministically; remote, escaping, symlink-escaping, or cyclic sources remain explicit leaf modules.
- **AC-0051** Given TypeScript that import-proves an AWS Pulumi constructor, when a resource with a literal logical name is parsed, then a Confirmed T0 Resource plus REFERENCES/DEPENDS_ON (including `parent`/`dependsOn`) and applicable shared Capability Registry edges are emitted; lookalike constructors without a Pulumi import produce no IaC facts.
- **AC-0052** Given a Pulumi stack export or preview JSON artifact declared as `pulumi_json`, when it is ingested, then matching T0 Pulumi resources gain separate Dynamic/Confirmed observed inputs, outputs, URN, and evidence while Pulumi secret wrappers are redacted and unmatched observations never fabricate T0 resources.
- **AC-0225** Given a Terraform module referenced by an explicit literal `source` (AC-0048) under a directory the ingest root's own `.gitignore` excludes, when it is extracted, then its files are still expanded (an explicit reference overrides `.gitignore`, ADR-0034) and the reported Terraform layer file count includes them — derived from the same incremental-cache file contexts extraction parsed or reused, so it matches file-for-file rather than undercounting from a separate walk; given a Go module root whose own `.gitignore` excludes `go.mod`, when the module path is resolved, then `go.mod` is treated as absent (an ambient direct read honors `.gitignore`, unlike an explicit reference) and internal-import proof that depends on it falls back to Gap exactly as it does with no `go.mod` at all.
- **AC-0316** Given TypeScript or JavaScript code that the repository has no IaC for, when it is ingested, then each proven client site becomes a `USES` edge from its enclosing symbol to an observed `Resource` (`props.observed = true`, `kind` = `browser-storage`, `sdk-client` or `auth-provider`). Proven sites are registry clients whose binding is import- or require-proven from the entry's module, and `indexedDB.open`, `caches.open` or Web Storage use in a file that does not bind that name. WebExtension storage (`<api>.storage.<area>` for the `local`, `sync`, `session` and `managed` areas) is one `webextension-storage` Resource per area when `<api>` is the unbound `chrome` or `browser` global or any name a default, namespace or `require` import of `webextension-polyfill` binds, and the file never writes `<api>.storage`; an area reached both ways keeps the polyfill `module`. There is one Resource per provider and instance identity. A literal identity names the instance, and a URL keeps only its scheme and authority (never credentials, paths or queries). An env reference records the variable name and never its value. A call without the declared identity names the provider alone. Bindings are proven lexically at each site: a parameter, local or block that rebinds the name, or a shadowed `window`/`self`/`globalThis` receiver, proves nothing. A runtime-computed identity becomes a Gap naming the site, with credentials and recognized secrets removed from its source text, and a literal that cannot be recorded safely is withheld whole. The client table is versioned registry data that extension files layer onto, and every edge it proves cites its registry file (#734).
- **AC-0317** Given TypeScript or JavaScript code that calls HTTP APIs, when it is ingested, then each call through the platform `fetch` (bare, or on an unbound `window`/`self`/`globalThis` that the file does not rewrite) or an import-proven registry HTTP client (axios, ky) whose URL starts with a proven absolute `http(s)` origin becomes a `USES` edge to one observed `Resource` per origin (`kind` = `http-api`), with the calling client on the edge. The URL head is the known leading text of a literal, literal `const`, template or `+` concatenation; text cut short by an unknown piece counts only once its authority is closed, so a host left open is never truncated to a wrong origin. A leading env reference records its name. Relative URLs name no other origin and make no resource. A computed registry-client URL is a Gap; a computed `fetch` URL is left to the client-call pass, which already records it (#734).
- **AC-0318** Given Python code that the repository has no IaC for, when it is ingested, then each proven call to a `pypi` registry client (boto3 `client`/`resource` for the service its literal first argument names, redis, the `limits.storage` Redis, Sentinel and Cluster rate-limit storages, smtplib, SQLAlchemy `create_engine`) becomes the same observed `Resource` and `USES` shape as AC-0316, with the same identity, credential and Gap rules; an identity declared as the first argument is also read from that parameter's keyword (`uri=`, `url=`, `host=`) when the registry entry names it. A client is proven only through a module-level import the file never rebinds; any other write of the bound name in the file (assignment, `def`/`class`, loop, `with`/`except` alias, parameter, walrus, `global`, a member write or `setattr` of the export the call reads, an import binding it to something else, or a wildcard import) proves nothing, and neither does a function-local or relative import alone. `os.environ[...]`, `os.environ.get(...)` and `os.getenv(...)` record the variable name, and a module-level name assigned once is followed one step (#734).
- **AC-0331** Given Python whose client identity names another repository module's constant (`config.X` through `from app import config`, `from app.config import X`, or `import app.config` then `app.config.X`), when it is ingested, then the constant is followed one step: the module must be exactly one repository file (`a/b.py` or `a/b/__init__.py`) that no package on the way rebinds, assigning the name once at module level to a literal or an env read and never otherwise writing, importing or star-importing it. No file may write that member or the reading module's binding through a proven binding or by dotted name (`setattr`, `monkeypatch.setattr`, `mock.patch`, `patch.object`, with positional or keyword operands; a splatted operand voids the follow), and no member write through any other root (an alias, a parameter) may use the constant's or the binding's name. A computed-name `setattr` through a root that may be any module (anything but a method's receiver or a name bound only by imports of one target, and anything beneath a member chain), or a computed-key write of `globals()`, `vars(x)` or `x.__dict__` reaching the module, voids the follow; a named member write through any chain voids it by name. When the repository makes a computed-name write on an undecorated or `@classmethod` method's receiver, the follow rests on that receiver convention: the resource and `USES` edge are InferredStrong and marked `identity_assumes_method_receivers`, never Confirmed. The `USES` edge then cites the defining assignment beside the call and records `identity_from`; a resource the follow creates cites it too, while one another site already proves keeps that proof unless it rested on the receiver assumption. Anything else stays the call site's Gap with its own expression (#734).
- **AC-0337** Given an AC-0318 call whose identity is a parameter of a module-level function (every parameter plain or typed, no default or splat, the parameter never rebound in the body), when it is ingested, then the parameter is followed to its one caller: the function's module must be one repository file that defines it once and names it nowhere else; across the repository, exactly one call through a proven import reaches it and no file reads it other than by calling it (a value, an attribute, a re-export), makes a relative, local or unproven import that could reach it, star-imports anything, or writes it as a member (named, computed, or through a namespace write). The call must have no splats and pass the parameter positionally or by keyword, not both. The argument is classified in the caller and a `config.X` argument is followed as AC-0331 does. Dynamic lookups are not seen, so the resource and `USES` edge are InferredStrong and marked `identity_assumes_static_callers` (also `identity_assumes_method_receivers` when the repository makes AC-0331's receiver writes), never Confirmed, citing the argument through `identity_from`. Anything else stays the call site's Gap (#734).
- **AC-0365** Given a JavaScript or TypeScript file that calls `self.addEventListener('install' | 'activate' | 'fetch', …)` with `self` the unshadowed global, or whose site-root path (`/sw.js`) is the literal argument of `navigator.serviceWorker.register(…)` in the same repository (resolved only when exactly one file fits; relative literals prove nothing), when it is ingested, then its File node and the Symbols defined in it carry `runtime: "service-worker"`, cite the proving call in their evidence and content hash, and the Atlas places them, and observed `browser-storage` Resources, in the Client layer instead of Server (the Server band, for browser storage too; browser storage also stays listed under the Infrastructure layer filter as observed infrastructure, #734). An anonymous function passed to `X.addEventListener('<event>', fn)` or to a method call `recv.method(fn)` keeps its `anon@<byte>` id and gains a `display_name` (`<file> · <event> listener`, `<file> · <callee> callback`) that the Atlas prefers; anything unproven stays as before (#1078).
- **AC-0376** Given an AC-0316 or AC-0318 client site that is not an HTTP API and whose instance identity is runtime-computed or withheld as unrecordable, when it is ingested, then the site keeps its Gap and its `USES` edge to that Gap, and it also gets a `USES` edge to the provider resource `observed:<provider>`. That edge is marked `instance_unresolved` and names the Gap. The resource is marked `instance_unresolved` unless an identity-less site also proves it, whatever the site order, and it keeps no raw identity text. An HTTP site with a computed URL still yields only its Gap (#1116).
- **AC-0689** Given Terraform where two different root-module directories (for example `envs/prod` and `envs/staging`) declare the same resource address, when it is extracted, then resource ids stay `res:{repo}@{address}` and a T0 Gap `gap:tf-address-collision:{repo}@{address}` names the address, each root module and every declaring file and byte span, instead of one declaration being lost silently; directories resolved as local module sources are child modules and their unprefixed reads never count, and a repository without such a collision gets no new fact (#1844).
- **Security:** IAM GRANTS feed the security view; secrets in state are redacted.
- **Performance:** Registry lookups O(1) per resource type.
- **Trace:** M2,M6 · `adapters-lang-ts`, `iac`, `adapters-lang-go`, `dynamic`, `spec`, `app` · — · T-0007..0009,T-0043..0048,T-0051..0052,T-0225,T-0316..0318,T-0331,T-0689

### US-0004 — Event graph with channel-identity stitching
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** producers and consumers linked by channel identity **so that** flows cross service and repo boundaries.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0010** Given a literal channel id on both sides, when matched, then a Confirmed PUBLISHES/SUBSCRIBES edge is created.
- **AC-0011** Given a channel id from a present config/env file, when resolved, then the edge is Confirmed via the config resolver.
- **AC-0012** Given a runtime-computed channel id, when unresolved at T0, then the hop escalates and, if still unresolved, emits a Gap with reason.
- **AC-0256** Given an EventBridge `PutEvents` site whose detail type is unresolved at T0, when an OTel trace is applied, then the Gap is filled only by observations attributed to that site that establish bus and detail type (with literal static components) as exactly one distinct scoped identity; a bus-only, contradicting, unattributed, multi-identity or batched case keeps the Gap with a reason naming the shortfall, and an observed bus is preserved as its own fact.
- **Security:** —
- **Performance:** Channel index keyed by identity for O(1) match.
- **Trace:** M3,M5,M6 · `events`, `dynamic`, `adapters-fw`, `adapters-lang-ts`, `flowtracer`, `app` · — · T-0010..0012, T-0256

### US-0005 — Client-side interaction graph (React first)
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** screens, components, and data-fetch call sites extracted **so that** flows are anchored at user actions.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0013** Given a React/Next repo, when ingested, then Screen/Component nodes and FETCHES edges to endpoints are created.
- **AC-0014** Given a data-fetch call site, when the endpoint is resolvable, then FETCHES is Confirmed; otherwise it escalates.
- **AC-0452** Given a fetch whose resolved path and method are known, when its repository's endpoints are stitched, then a method-less route serves every method, whether spelled `ANY` or Express-style `ALL` (Express `all`, an unguarded node-http route, a Next.js pages/api handler). A method route beside it on the same path keeps the match an ambiguity `Gap`. Prefix routes (`/x/*`) are not matched, because their match semantics differ by producer (#1327).
- **AC-0453** Given a prefix route whose producer declares its `prefix_kind` (#1329), when same-repo fetches or cross-repo binding joins are stitched, then it matches by that kind: `raw` (node-http `startsWith`) when the raw string before `*` prefixes the request path, `segments+` (Next `[...x]`) when the segments before `/*` match followed by one or more non-empty segments, and `segments*` (Next `[[...x]]`) for zero or more. A prefix route with a missing or unknown kind, or whose producers disagree on its kind (`conflict`, including a node-http exact route of the same id as a Next catch-all), matches nothing, even a request whose literal path ends in `*`. A request that matches an exact route and a prefix route, or several prefix routes, stays an ambiguity `Gap`, with no precedence assumed. The cross-repo pass also lets a method-less `ANY`/`ALL` route serve every method.
- **Security:** —
- **Performance:** —
- **Trace:** M4 · `adapters-lang-ts` (tsx), `adapters-fw`, `events` · — · T-0013..0014, T-0452..0453

### US-0006 — Deterministic end-to-end flow tracer
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** cross-layer flows traced T0-first with explicit gaps **so that** flows are trustworthy.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0015** Given a trigger, when traced, then each hop resolves at the lowest possible tier and records its tier.
- **AC-0016** Given any unresolved hop, when no tier resolves it, then a Gap node truncates that branch (no silent completion).
- **AC-0017** Given a completed trace, when scored, then flow_status ∈ {Verified, Partial, Inferred} per the scoring rule.
- **AC-0083** Given a recovered WebExtension graph, when flows are traced, then extension user-action anchors trigger their own flows — every ExtensionContext, and every manifest Command routed to its dispatching context (`_execute_*` to the toolbar action, all others to background, with an explicit Gap when that context is absent) — whose hops walk ENTRY into the entry file and DEFINED_IN in reverse from that file into the symbols it defines, deterministically under input reordering, while internally published channels participate as mid-flow hops rather than triggers.
- **AC-0219** Given a flow node that owns a Gap through `DEPENDS_ON`, when the flow is traced, then an execution Gap (code the owner runs that T0 could not recover, e.g. an unproven `eval()`) appears as a terminal Gap hop with its reason and attempted tiers and the flow is Partial, while a Gap flagged `rule_evidence_gap` (rule-evidence scope, including decoded eval code awaiting a span mapping) and any `DEPENDS_ON` between non-Gap facts leave the flow's status unchanged (ADR-0032).
- **AC-0257** Given a caller-admitted graph and explicit limits for flow count, cumulative hop count and compact UTF-8 JSON output bytes, when bounded tracing runs, then each owned flow and hop is admitted before construction; success is byte-identical to ordinary tracing with unchanged anchors, traversal and deduplication order, reversed-edge identity, depth status, provenance, producing methods and score summation, while a limit returns an explicit resource/maximum error without a partial result. The caller separately bounds graph admission and linear borrowed traversal workspace.
- **AC-0330** Given a repository that declares scheduled jobs in a yacron-format file (`jobs:` as a sequence of mappings with a `schedule`), when it is ingested, then each mapping that declares a `schedule` becomes a `Schedule` trigger carrying its name, verbatim schedule and command, and anchors its own flow through `ENTRY` into the file it runs. The entry is Confirmed only when the root `Dockerfile`'s final stage proves where the repository sits in the container (a single `COPY . <dir>`) and the script path, a relative one joined to that stage's final `WORKDIR`, lies under it, citing both files. A later `COPY`/`ADD` (`--from` included) or `RUN` in that stage that may reach the entry leaves it InferredStrong, marked `entry_may_be_replaced_in_image` and citing the instruction. Without the mapping, a relative path against the repository root or a unique repository suffix of an absolute path is InferredStrong and marked `entry_relative_to_repo_root` or `entry_path_suffix`. A shell-composed command, inline code (`node -e`, `python -c`), an interpreter option outside the modeled grammar, an unknown or ambiguous file, a command that runs no script, or a YAML alias, anchor, tag or merge key is a Gap naming the job. A GitHub Actions `jobs` mapping is not a schedule (#770).
- **AC-0332** Given a repository that declares scheduled jobs in a crontab(5) file (`crontab`, `*.crontab`, `*.cron`, or a file directly in a `cron.d` directory), when it is ingested, then each job line (five time fields, each within its bounds with names only in the month and weekday fields, or an `@` macro, then a user field in `cron.d` files, then the command) becomes a `Schedule` trigger with `format` `crontab`, its verbatim schedule and command, citing its own line, and resolves its `ENTRY` exactly as AC-0330 does. Comment, blank and environment (`NAME=value`, spaces around `=` allowed) lines are not jobs. In a file with at least one job, any other line, and a command using an unescaped `%`, is a Gap naming the line. A file with no parsable job declares nothing (#779).
- **AC-0333** Given a repository with a Kubernetes `CronJob` document (`apiVersion: batch/…`, in any `.yml`/`.yaml` file, multi-document streams included), when it is ingested, then each container of `spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.containers` becomes a `Schedule` trigger with `format` `kubernetes`, the job's `spec.schedule`, and its literal `command` then `args` as the command, named after the job (and the container when there are several), and resolves its `ENTRY` as AC-0330 does, except that an entry proven only through the root `Dockerfile` is `InferredStrong`, marked `entry_image_assumed_from_dockerfile`, since the image is not proven to be that build. A container without `command` or with an empty one (image entrypoint), one setting `workingDir`, an empty argument or one containing whitespace, a non-literal command list, a templated manifest (`{{`) or a YAML alias, anchor or tag is a Gap naming the job. Other kinds, and `CronJob` kinds outside `batch/`, declare nothing (#779).
- **AC-0334** Given a scheduled job whose command runs a Python entry with one option and value (`-j v`, `--job v`, `--job=v`), when it is ingested, then its `ENTRY` enters the function that value selects, `InferredStrong` (a static reading of the branch) and marked `entry_argument_dispatch`, carrying `dispatch` (option, value, function, file) and citing the branch, only when, on the straight-line path of the entry's `if __name__ == "__main__":` block (entering `with` bodies only) and in this order, `<parser> = argparse.ArgumentParser(...)` is the module's only binding of `<parser>` (with a top-level `import argparse` before the block the only binding of `argparse`), one `<parser>.add_argument` declares that option with only value-preserving keywords (`help`, `metavar`, `required`, `default`, `dest`, `type=str` with `str` never bound in the module, `action="store"`, `choices` listing the value), `<args> = <parser>.parse_args()` binds it, and one `if`/`elif` chain tests `<args>.<dest>` where every clause before the first one comparing it with the value compares it with another literal; the block reads the parser and the parsed arguments only as attributes after their bindings; and that branch makes exactly one bare call to a name bound once in module scope, by an undecorated top-level `def` before the block that the Python adapter emitted. Otherwise, including a converting `type`, `nargs` or other actions, a chain under another condition or loop, a write to or other use of the parsed arguments, a replaced, unproven or passed-on parser, a wildcard import, or a rebound, imported, decorated, conditional or later-defined function, the `ENTRY` stays the file as in AC-0330 and names why in `dispatch_unresolved`; a command passing no option makes no dispatch claim (#779).
- **AC-0335** Given a GitHub Actions workflow (a YAML file directly in `.github/workflows`) that declares `on.schedule`, when it is ingested, then each `cron` entry becomes a `Schedule` trigger with `format` `github-actions`, the workflow's `name` and its verbatim cron, citing the entry, whose `ENTRY` is the workflow file itself (Confirmed); the workflow's steps are not traced through. A cron that is not five schedule fields within their crontab(5) bounds (plus the start-value step `20/15` that GitHub documents), a non-literal one, or a YAML alias, anchor or tag on the entry, the `schedule` sequence or the `on` mapping is a Gap naming the entry. A workflow without `on.schedule`, and a YAML file outside that directory, declares no workflow schedule (#779).
- **AC-0336** Given an AC-0334 dispatch whose selected branch calls a function imported by a top-level absolute `from <module> import <name>` before the `__main__` block (the name's only module binding, not aliased), when it is ingested, then the `ENTRY` enters that function in the one repository file the module resolves to from the script's directory (`<module>.py` or `<module>/__init__.py`, with no package on the way shadowed by a same-named `.py` file), when that file binds the name once by a top-level `def` whose body the call runs, recording `dispatch.module` and the defining `file` and citing the import beside the branch. A branch whose one call is `asyncio.run(<bare call>)`, with `import asyncio` proven as AC-0334 proves `argparse`, dispatches as if it made that call. A call runs a function's body only as a plain call of a plain `def` or `asyncio.run` of an `async def`; a bare call of an `async def`, `asyncio.run` of a plain one, or either of a generator (a `yield` in its own body) proves no dispatch. The import must be the statement's only item binding the name (in `from m import f, g as f` the alias wins), and the same holds for `import argparse` / `import asyncio`. A repository `argparse` module, or for `asyncio.run` an `asyncio` module (`<name>.py` or `<name>/__init__.py` beside the script), shadows the standard library and proves no dispatch. A relative or aliased import, a module that is no repository file or is ambiguous, or a function the module does not define that way keeps the file and names why in `dispatch_unresolved` (#779).
- **Security:** —
- **Performance:** Path queries bounded; long flows stream incrementally to UI.
- **Trace:** M3–M5 · `flowtracer`, `ingest` · F-* · T-0015..0017, T-0083, T-0219, T-0257, T-0330, T-0332, T-0333, T-0334, T-0335, T-0336
