### US-0008 — Semantic tier with eval gating
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** semantic matching gated by paired evals **so that** inferred links meet a precision floor.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0021** Given real ingested unresolved channel or call hops, when T2 runs, then IaC-backed channel resources and repository symbols are eligible targets and proposals are marked InferredStrong with evidence.
- **AC-0022** Given a labeled eval set, when T2 is measured, then proposals below the precision floor are excluded from `best-effort` exports.
- **AC-0269** Given a local semantic preview with a verified model manifest and a bounded persistent vector cache, when the same redacted inputs are previewed after reopening the store without eviction, then candidates, queries and evaluation texts require zero embedding calls while current graph membership, evidence, labels, thresholds and proposal gating are recomputed. Changed text, model, recipe or store generation cannot reuse mismatched vectors; malformed or oversized rows are rejected before decoding; concurrent inserts reuse the first published vector. Graph clear, schema reset and retained-source forget delete cached vectors and invalidate in-flight generation leases before old results can return or republish. Unpinned model identity never permits persistent reuse. A writable corrupt or unsupported host-owned cache is recreated without blocking startup, graph clear or source forgetting; a fresh cache epoch prevents old-lease reuse. Namespace capacity evicts stale unprotected entries deterministically instead of permanently rejecting a new model.
- **AC-0285** Given an unresolved hop whose query is interface dispatch — a receiver named in the `IFoo` convention (a capital `I` followed by another capital letter and at least one further character) with a member access, such as `IChannel.listen` or `INodeProcess.cwd` — when T2 builds its ANN candidacy, then the hop is classified out of candidacy: it is never embedded and never proposed, and it stays an open Gap tagged with the exclusion reason `dynamic dispatch - needs type hierarchy` so the register can group it; conservative look-alikes (`Item.get`, `Icon.render`, `IO.read`, `ID.parse`, a bare `IFoo` with no member access, a lower-case receiver) stay in candidacy.
- **Security:** Embeddings computed locally by default. Cache keys cover only the exact redacted provider input bytes and a versioned redaction recipe. Cache rows contain vectors and digests, never raw source text or evaluation verdicts. Retained-source forgetting leaves graph facts in place, so later previews may re-embed them; logical deletion makes no physical disk-erasure claim.
- **Performance:** Bounded vector storage and deterministic sequence-based eviction; hit, miss, conflict and eviction counters disclose actual work. The ANN index is rebuilt from current candidates. Zero-call reuse applies only while all required entries remain resident; no end-to-end latency claim is established by cache counters.
- **Trace:** M7 · `semantic`, `llm` · — · T-0021..0022, T-0269, T-0285

### US-0009 — Bounded agentic tier with egress firewall
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** agents to propose resolutions only, with per-tier cloud opt-in **so that** privacy and integrity hold.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0023** Given a Local-only policy, when T3 needs a cloud provider, then it hard-fails closed (no silent egress).
- **AC-0024** Given cloud opt-in, when an agent runs, then the consent dialog shows the exact span-level payload leaving the device.
- **AC-0025** Given an agent proposal, when accepted/rejected, then the decision persists and re-applies on re-ingest.
- **AC-0055** Given a tier whose provider is switched to cloud in Settings, when standing consent has not been granted, then the full disclosure (provider, pinned model id, endpoint, per-token pricing, lane caveats) is shown before consent is recordable and no cloud call is possible; if no disclosure is available, no consent affordance is offered at all.
- **AC-0056** Given standing cloud consent, when it is revoked, the tier is disabled, or the tier's provider leaves cloud, then the derived egress policy and the status-bar egress summary immediately return to local-only.
- **AC-0087** Given the Settings surface, when it renders with a live core, then an Adapters section lists every installed adapter (language, extractor id, covered extensions, and what it extracts) from the same registry Preflight consults — inventory and coverage can never disagree — with the word "adapter" explained in place (per language/format, distinct from detected frameworks and toolchain versions), and known-but-uninstalled adapter types (JavaScript, C, C++, Kotlin, Swift, Objective-C) listed as recommendations wired to the request-adapter lane; a detected uncovered language with a planned adapter names that adapter type in its Preflight finding.
- **AC-0089** Given a gap cause class, when it is escalated as a batch, then every instance runs locally through the propose-only broker in one durable cancellable job (a failed instance records a failure, never an abort; a cancel stops at the next instance boundary), each success is an individually staged proposal accepted or rejected through the standard decision records — nothing joins the graph unaccepted — and cloud escalation remains per-instance because a consent grant binds to one exact payload hash; classes that truncate traced flows rank above larger classes that block nothing, deterministically, in both the cause lane and the by-tier tab.
- **AC-0063** Given an open gap, when strategies are requested, then the report derives from real provenance (attempted tiers, stop reason, required evidence citations, closed candidate set) with local and cloud options carrying exact redacted-payload egress estimates, the cloud option fails closed without standing T3 consent; and when an escalation runs, local executes immediately while cloud requires both standing consent and a per-payload grant whose hash matches the exact preview, the run is a durable job, egress bytes are recorded, and the result is a staged proposal that never joins the graph (accept/reject flows through the existing decision records).
- **AC-0231** Given the bounded T3 broker's edge-label allowlist (checked, never guessed, against a gap's own relation — an edge/hop's own label, or a Gap node's adjacent edge once the compiler can resolve one), when the Gaps register renders a gap row or cause class, then it offers escalation only for a relation the broker can currently propose and reads "not broker-executable" instead of a tier CTA for one it cannot (an unknown relation stays offered, optimistically, rather than guessed or withheld); and when the Resolution Strategy modal derives strategies for a gap whose relation the broker cannot propose, every strategy card reports unavailable with a user-level reason naming the relation, never the broker's internal validation error text ("invalid bounded task", "allowlist"). The modal still lists the gap's assembled evidence for such a gap, and escalating it reports the same user-level reason (#1635).
- **AC-0579** Given text bound for a model payload, an embedding input, a durable history field or recorded observed evidence, when it assigns a value to a name that ends in a credential word (`DB_PASSWORD`, `clientSecret`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `SECRET_KEY`, `passwd`) or to a quoted key (`"password": "…"`, `'api_key' => '…'`), then the shared `redact-text@2` policy replaces the value with `[REDACTED]`, keeps the name and separator byte for byte, and counts one replacement; a name that only contains a credential word (`passwordRequired`, `password_hash`), a comparison, and ordinary `token` or `key` vocabulary are left as written; redacting the result again changes nothing; and because the policy version is part of embedding cache keys, receipt source sizes and preview fingerprints, values bound to `redact-text@1` are recomputed and never reused.
- **AC-0580** Given a consented cloud call, when its payload is handed to the provider, then its bytes are added to the egress record before the request is sent, so a call that then fails, times out or returns something the host rejects is counted like one that succeeds; a call stopped before dispatch (no consent, policy denial, cancellation) records nothing, and when the record cannot be written the call is not made (#1767).
- **AC-0586** Given the desktop app, when the user opens **Settings → Provider keys**, then they can enter, replace and remove a provider API key. The key is written only to the operating system's credential store (Keychain on macOS, Credential Manager on Windows); it is never written to the settings database, a log, an export or a consent record, and no command returns it. The section shows whether a key is present and where the key in use comes from; the environment variable keeps working and takes precedence over a saved key. With no key and no variable the cloud lane stays closed and says where to add one. Holding a key grants nothing: each cloud step still asks for consent (ADR-0057, #1763).
- **AC-0590** Given a provider key is configured, when the user chooses **Check connection** in **Settings → Provider keys**, then the app sends one bodiless request for the provider's model list carrying the key and no source text, prompt or graph content; the request's size is written to the egress record under `<provider>:connection-check` before it is sent, and nothing is sent if that record cannot be written; no tier consent is asked; the result is one of key accepted, key refused or provider unreachable, states the bytes recorded, and never shows the key or the response body; with no key nothing is sent and the action is unavailable; the check stops after 10 seconds and a result that arrives after the user has left the page is dropped. And given the local lane, when the user opens **Settings → Local lane** or chooses **Check local lane**, then the app shows whether the local model server answers; when it does not, the command that starts it; when it does, each catalog model as installed or missing with the exact `ollama pull` command for each missing one. Cartograph does not pull a model (ADR-0057 amendment, #1795).
- **Security:** Default-deny cloud egress; secret redaction on payloads. The connection check carries no source content and is still recorded as egress.
- **Performance:** —
- **Trace:** M8 · `agents`, `llm`, `ingest`, `core-redact`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0057 · T-0023..0025,T-0055..0056,T-0063,T-0087,T-0089,T-0231,T-0579,T-0580,T-0586,T-0590

### US-0010 — Atlas graph canvas with confidence overlay
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** to explore the unified graph with layer filters and tier coloring **so that** I can see what is confirmed vs inferred vs gapped.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0026** Given the graph, when I filter by layer, then only that layer's nodes/edges render: a node renders when its kind is native to the layer, an edge renders when both endpoints are native to the layer or its relationship belongs to the layer and one endpoint is native (bringing in the other endpoint), so relations between unclassified facts (rule exits and rule gaps) never appear under a layer; a layer with nothing recovered shows what would populate it (for Infrastructure: infrastructure-as-code, deployment config or a stack plugin).
- **AC-0027** Given the confidence overlay, when active, then nodes/edges are colored by tier and Gaps are flagged.
- **AC-0028** Given a node, when selected, then the evidence panel shows file/span/commit with read-only jump-to-source. Screen titles in Atlas and evidence use the recorded route when no explicit display name or name is present; missing/blank routes retain the stable ID fallback. Display labels never change node identity or provenance.
- **AC-0062** Given the evidence drawer, when open, then it is resizable (320↔560px), its overlay never swallows clicks outside the panel, Esc closes it, the source view carries true file line numbers (windowed files report their real starting line) and the full span range including end line:col, non-Confirmed facts show a why-strip plus a collapsible why-this-tier explanation, the provenance table renders the complete 64-hex content hash with a copy affordance whose payload equals the displayed value, supporting evidence spans are navigable, a Gap offers the Resolution Strategy CTA, and the footer states the R-INT-1 integrity rule.
- **AC-0064** Given the Atlas canvas, when it renders, then node shape encodes kind independently of color (octagon with a dashed red border = Gap, diamond = gateway/channel, rounded rectangle = everything else), every edge carries a clickable mono tier+relation chip (`T0 HANDLES`, `GAP …`) that opens the evidence drawer for the edge exactly like a node, the layer filter drives the header scope chip (`Atlas · <layer>`), and a single click selects a node or edge even while the drawer is open.
- **AC-0081** Given a recovered graph, when the Atlas renders, then nodes are placed in labeled architecture bands (Infrastructure → Cloud → Server → Events → Client → Tools/Build, Gaps anchored to a neighbor's band) at deterministic positions — identical snapshots yield identical layouts regardless of input order — and past a size threshold the initial view renders collapsed per-band clusters (keyed by repo/module) with aggregated relation counts that expand on demand; reading the graph never requires manual arrangement. Config/build evidence (`Tool` nodes and the `File` nodes proving them) lands in exactly one band, Tools/Build, never split across Server and Unclassified under the same cluster name; a Confirmed external/internal boundary placeholder is excluded from every band entirely, while an unresolved placeholder — a genuine Gap — stays banded and visible; a collapsed endpoint cluster is keyed by HTTP method plus route segment (`GET /owners`), never the bare method with a trailing colon; and a collapsed cluster's name and member count render as the tile's own content, not a caption beneath an empty box.
- **AC-0086** Given a selected node, when I press Enter (or activate the focus control), then the view reflows deterministically to that node's ego graph — the root, its direct connections, and the edges among them, everything else removed — with each focus stacking one navigable level; Esc (or the breadcrumb) backs out exactly one level at a time, ending at the full graph, and the focus state is keyboard-operable and announced via a live region.
- **AC-0366** Given a band with more than 60 clusters, when the Atlas renders, then the clusters fold into groups (repository, then contiguous ranges of sorted cluster keys) so no band or group opens to more than 60 tiles, nesting at most two group levels. Tiles wrap into a grid, edges aggregate between the nearest visible groups with a count, every cluster is reachable in at most three clicks, a group opens from its tile or the Collapsed groups index as a drill-in (its siblings step aside so the level stays within 60 tiles; an aggregated edge opens one group endpoint) and folds back from the Open groups trail, the indexes list every collapsed group and cluster, and the code bands read "Server code" and "Client code". Groups add no facts (#1079).
- **AC-0558** Given the evidence drawer on a non-Confirmed fact, when the why-strip and why-this-tier text render, then they name the producer from the recorded producing tier (Deterministic T0, Dynamic T1 observed execution, Semantic T2, Agentic T3, or explicitly not recorded) and describe confidence separately, so a Semantic weak fact is never called agentic and a deterministic Inferred fact is never described as proposed by a higher tier; the cited span is scrolled into view even when the source window starts at line 1; and a fact with no recorded producing method reads "producer not recorded", so it is not mistaken for an HTTP method (#1601).
- **AC-0715** Given the loaded Atlas snapshot, when I type in the labeled Find entity field, then entities are matched by display name, kind or stable ID (trimmed, case-insensitive, ordered by display name then ID) across every layer filter, focus level and collapsed group, boundary placeholders excluded as on the canvas, and the field states that scope; matches list in place of the ordinary indexes with an explicit no-match state, and clearing the query (Clear or Esc) restores those indexes without changing the selection. Choosing a result opens the evidence drawer with the exact original node (AC-0028) and reveals it through the existing mechanisms — the layer widens to All layers only when the active layer does not hold it, focus backs out only the levels that hide it, and the groups and cluster around it open; tier and provenance are unchanged. The lookup reads only the loaded snapshot (no graph reads or model calls); semantic retrieval stays #260 (#1666).
- **Security:** Source view is read-only (NG1).
- **Performance:** Canvas remains interactive at 10k+ nodes (Cytoscape.js).
- **Trace:** M9 · `core-graph`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0026..0028,T-0062,T-0064,T-0081,T-0086,T-0558,T-0715

### US-0012 — Spec Workbench, curation, and export
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** to review compiled artifacts, accept/reject inferred items, and export **so that** I get an official, trustworthy spec.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0032** Given compiled artifacts, when viewed, then every assertion shows inline provenance; rendered artifact tables keep short labels readable and long values accessible through table-local scrolling without changing export content.
- **AC-0033** Given an inferred item, when I accept/reject/annotate, then the decision persists and survives re-ingest via content_hash.
- **AC-0034** Given export, when run, then it honors R-INT-5 (`verified-only` vs `best-effort`) and includes Gap + Drift registers.
- **AC-0035** Given the full set, when exported, then it produces user stories, US-TM, flow dossiers, resource topology as Markdown with fenced Mermaid plus provenance, a data model retaining READS/WRITES/MAPS_TO relations that terminate at DataEntity, ADRs, Gap + Drift registers, and mapped security findings.
- **AC-0057** Given a completed recovery, when the Workspace landing renders, then the outcome tally (open findings split into gaps, unsupported patterns, and no-evidence) and every provenance-health count derive from the single register summary and atlas projection all surfaces share — findings are listed explicitly, never guessed or double-counted — and the outcome badge states the weakest confidence tier actually present among recovered facts (InferredWeak before InferredStrong before Confirmed); open gaps mark the recovery partial via the outcome title and tally but never inflate the badge to an inferred tier no fact carries.
- **AC-0058** Given the artifacts grid, when an artifact was generated, then it carries two independent badges — generation ("Artifact generated") and recovery authority (authoritative/partial/inferred) — while the gap register carries exactly one completion-style badge ("N open findings") and a missing artifact is labeled "Not generated" rather than omitted.
- **AC-0067** Given the Spec Workbench, when a Confirmed (deterministic) assertion renders, then its Accept/Reject/Annotate controls are visible but truly disabled — `disabled` plus `aria-disabled="true"`, never hidden and never exposed as enabled in the a11y tree — with an inline "Confirmed T0 — locked, read-only" explanation and a document-level note stating the R-INT-1 rule; only proposed T2/T3 assertions are curatable, Gap assertions offer no curation at all, and each doc-list entry carries a per-document count chip naming its actual unit (US/links/hops/ADR and assertion rows for registers, alert tone for non-empty registers); the gap summary names unresolved findings separately from assertion rows, explains that supporting rows can refer to the same finding, and capped previews retain full row totals (#1663).
- **AC-0210** Given a gap register larger than the Gaps lane's grouping threshold (12 rows), when the spec is compiled, then `gap_register.md` groups the gaps into the lane's cause classes (stop reason × extractor, largest first, deterministic ties), details at most 50 classes with up to 5 representative instances each (inline provenance for those instances), and states every omission as a counted line ("N more instances", "N more classes") so its size stays bounded regardless of register size; a `gap_register.json` sidecar lists every instance id by class, every instance still carries its full provenance in the bundle's structured assertions, and nothing is silently dropped.
- **AC-0218** Given a spec bundle whose artifact content or assertions are large, when `export_spec` compiles it for IPC, then each artifact's `content` is capped to a fixed byte preview (never splitting a UTF-8 character) and its `assertions` to a fixed count, while the artifact's true `content_byte_len` and `assertions_total` are always reported; the Spec Workbench renders the capped `content` in its `<pre>` with a visible counted truncation note instead of the complete text, and the new `read_spec_artifact` command returns the artifact's complete, untruncated content and assertions on demand for Copy artifact and Export bundle. The Gaps & Drift register surface, which resolves directly against the gap-register and drift-register assertions, always sees every instance — it backfills the complete list via `read_spec_artifact` whenever the IPC preview truncated it, so no real gap or drift finding is ever silently hidden by the cap.
- **AC-0221** Given a recovered traceability matrix above 200 links or a source-rule inventory above 50 observations, when the spec is compiled, then `US-TM.md` groups links by relation × target and `rule-evidence.md` groups observations by exit-source file (largest first, deterministic ties), each details at most 50 groups with up to 5 representative links or 3 representative observations per group (inline provenance for those representatives only), and states every omission as a counted line ("N more links", "N more observations", "N more classes" / "N more files") so its size stays bounded regardless of input size; `US-TM.json` and `rule-evidence.json` sidecars list every link, observation and relationship id by group, every assertion still carries its full provenance in the bundle's structured assertions, and at or below the thresholds both artifacts render exactly as before.
- **AC-0258** Given caller-admitted graph/flow inputs and requested official artifact ids, when selected compilation runs with an output budget, then it renders only selected artifact producers, normalizes duplicate ids into official artifact order, and admits selected content UTF-8 bytes plus exact complete JSON assertion-array bytes cumulatively, including escaping and punctuation. Each success retains ordinary full-artifact content, assertions, schema and identity under the same mode and rejection policy; an output or shared-ADR-derivation limit returns an explicit per-artifact failure with no truncated artifact or consumed output budget for that failure, and an unknown id fails before projection.
- **AC-0297** Given Export bundle or a work-packet export in the desktop app, when the user exports, then a native save panel (sanitised suggested basename, destination and overwrite confirmation owned by the panel) receives the complete, untruncated content and the host writes it — no size cap below what the previous browser download accepted; confirming reports the file exported, cancelling reports nothing and writes nothing, a write failure surfaces an error that never echoes the content and leaves any existing file at the destination unchanged; replacing a file keeps its permissions; outside the desktop host the browser download fallback is used.
- **AC-0358** Given a non-diagram Markdown artifact in the Spec Workbench, when it is selected, then it renders by default as headings, GFM pipe tables (escaped pipes and pipes inside code spans stay in their cell), fenced code, lists, blockquotes and inline code/bold, built only from React elements: no HTML from the artifact is interpreted and link targets show as inert text, never anchors. A Rendered/Source toggle (`aria-pressed`) shows the unchanged plain-text source and keeps its choice across artifact selection. Diagram and JSON artifacts keep their existing views, and the truncation note and assertions section appear in both modes. Rendering is linear in the content, including unclosed inline openers.
- **AC-0359** Given a recovered register whose gaps include classes the interpretation seam fills, when the Workspace outcome and the Gaps & Drift register render, then those gaps (established interpretation-seam omissions, never unclassified fallbacks) are listed in a separate Interpretation backlog lane with their own count and the same class grouping, the System gaps lane leads with the remaining adapter/registry/input/requirement and unclassified gaps, the shared register summary reports `interpretation_backlog` as a subset still counted in gaps and open findings, and nothing is suppressed (ADR-0036).
- **AC-0551** Given a recovered graph, when `read_spec_artifact` fetches one official artifact in full, then only that artifact is compiled, through the selected compiler with unbounded limits, and its content, content id and assertions equal that artifact in the full bundle; an unknown artifact id is refused with the same error as before (#1582).

- **AC-0587** Given the topology map, when it is drawn in `topology.md` or returned by the standalone topology export, then every node and edge prints its tier and confidence (`T0 Confirmed`, `T1 Confirmed`, `T2 InferredStrong`, `T3 InferredWeak`), so a static, an observed and an inferred edge never read the same (R-INT-2). Nothing in them is filtered by export mode, and the same stored inventories give the same bytes. `coverage.md` keeps one row per repository and estate totals by producer and by reason, with per-repository detail up to 12 repositories; `coverage.json` carries every stored inventory body. A row carries the fact key and, per distinct provenance, tier, confidence, method, basis, extractor, evidence spans and the citing artifacts; differing provenance for one fact is listed in full and marked. The index holds cited facts only, in citation-id order, with identical bytes for the same graph. A `verified-only` index has no InferredWeak row and `best-effort` marks such rows (R-INT-5). An export fails if a cited id has no row, if two facts share an id, or if a `verified-only` export cites an InferredWeak fact. MCP reads neither advertise nor serve the two index artifacts.

### US-0013 — ADR recovery and drift detection
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** found and recovered ADRs linked to governed targets, with conflicts surfaced **so that** decisions and drift are visible.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0036** Given existing Markdown ADR/RFC files, when parsed or re-ingested, then explicit `Governs:` or exact backtick target ids link to existing full-system graph targets as Confirmed facts, including targets from another repo, while removed declarations and deleted ADR files remove their prior links and found nodes.
- **AC-0037** Given evidence-backed channel architecture, when recovered ADRs are drafted, then they are marked recovered/inferred, cite the producing graph evidence, remain curatable, and cannot survive rejection of their supporting facts.
- **AC-0038** Given a found ADR with an explicit `Forbids:` edge constraint, when governed code conflicts, then a confidence-preserving finding appears in the Drift register mapped to the offending edge and any containing flow, unless that supporting edge was rejected.
- **AC-0059** Given the Gaps & Drift register surface, when it renders, then System Gaps, unsupported patterns, and no-evidence findings appear in three distinct lanes that never conflate (an unsupported item is a tool limitation, never a Gap), the header tally quotes the same register summary Workspace quotes, gaps group by their next escalation tier, and drift findings list under their own tab — all wired from the spec compiler's registers, never re-derived in the UI.
- **AC-0088** Given the load-bearing vocabulary wherever it renders (System-gap, unsupported-pattern, and no-evidence lanes; the Workspace outcome and artifact-authority badges; the Atlas confidence legend; the Flow Inspector projection toggle), when a user reaches the term, then a keyboard-accessible in-place help affordance explains it within one interaction (one Tab and one Enter; Esc dismisses) with a Learn-more deep link, and every note renders from the single-sourced help-notes module — never a per-surface copy that could drift.
- **AC-0082** Given a register with more gaps than a screenful, when the System-gaps lane renders, then gaps group into cause classes (stop reason × extractor) ordered deterministically by instance count, each class expands on demand to paged instance rows that stay responsive at thousands of gaps, and every instance keeps its Resolution Strategy path — the lane reads as a ranked handful of causes, never a wall of rows.
- **AC-0259** Given caller-admitted graph/flow inputs and explicit derived-row and complete serialized-ADR byte limits, when bounded ADR derivation runs, then recovered ADR and drift occurrences are admitted before materialization, including cross-product and repeated-flow evidence; within limits the complete result preserves ordinary methods, provenance, identities and order, while overflow returns an explicit limit error without a partial result. Input admission and linear workspace remain separately bounded by the caller.
- **AC-0313** Given an author-declared API reference (`.cartograph/api-docs.toml` in the repository root and/or `api_docs` on the repository's `cartograph.system.toml` entry, unioned with the manifest entry winning on duplicate `path`, where a `.cartograph` that is a plain file is not an opt-in, validated fail-closed as explicit `Gap` facts for non-relative or `..` paths, symlinks anywhere in the path, non-regular files such as FIFOs (checked on the opened handle, with no check-then-open window), files over 1 MiB, more than 16 docs, prefixes not starting with `/`, malformed TOML (which drops only the repository file's declarations, never the manifest's), unsupported formats including YAML, or docs with zero parsable routes), when the repository is recovered and `drift_register.md` is compiled, then Markdown endpoint headings and OpenAPI 3 JSON `paths.<path>.<method>` entries for `GET`/`POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` become Confirmed T0 `DocumentedRoute` facts linked by `DOCUMENTED_IN` to their `File` node, canonical keys are compared against recovered `Endpoint` nodes (scoped by the declared `prefix` or the documented routes' first path segments), and every difference is emitted as a `Drift` node (`documented-not-served` at `InferredStrong` with reason `"route absence depends on recovery coverage"`, `served-undocumented`, or `method-mismatch` replacing disjoint method pairs on the same canonical path, both at the weakest confidence of the recovered endpoints they cite, so an `InferredStrong` endpoint never yields `Confirmed` drift) linked by `DOCUMENTED_AS` and `SERVED_AS` edges and rendered under `## API contract drift` when docs are declared (#739).
- **Security:** —
- **Performance:** —
- **Trace:** M9 · `ingest`, `spec`, `app` · — · T-0036..0038,T-0059,T-0082,T-0088,T-0259,T-0313

### US-0014 — Determinism and re-ingest idempotency
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** re-ingesting the same commit under the same source registration to yield an identical graph **so that** outputs are reproducible.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0039** Given the same commit and inputs, when re-ingested, then the ordered T0 node/edge identity plus content-hash snapshot is identical and unchanged graph facts are not rewritten; every ingest or evidence path is canonicalized through one shared helper that never stores a platform-specific verbatim prefix (the Windows `\\?\` extended-length form), so the stored path form for the same tree does not vary by platform.
- **AC-0223** Given a tree recovered through any lane (local, managed clone, or manifest), when its Terraform root, local-module ancestors, and module sources are resolved, or a cloned repo's root is reserved as the key for its plugin settings and evidence reads, then each path takes the same verbatim-prefix-free canonical form as the shared helper in AC-0039 — Terraform extraction canonicalizes the same way, and a managed clone root derives from the canonicalized app data directory even when that directory is reached through a symlink — so containment checks and settings keys compare one path form.
- **AC-0040** Given a changed or deleted TS/TSX/Terraform file, when re-ingested in the same process, then only new or byte-changed per-file extraction contexts are reparsed, unchanged contexts are reused by source hash, deterministic repository-wide joins are refreshed, and stale facts are removed.
- **AC-0060** Given any completed ingest, when it finishes, then a history record persists its tier tallies over the whole graph (counted with the register's own provenance definition, reconciling with the register summary), unsupported/no-evidence counts, per-extractor coverage (files in scope, distinct files with facts — a covering adapter with zero facts is a 0% row, never a missing row), and an order-independent content hash scoped to the facts whose evidence belongs to this ingest's own registration(s) — a single-repo ingest hashes only that repo's facts, a whole-system ingest hashes every repo it loaded — so re-ingesting the same commit under the same source registration shows identical hashes in queryable history even when an unrelated repo elsewhere in a multi-repo workspace changes between the two ingests (#466) — determinism observable as data, not only asserted in tests.
- **AC-0205** Given a tree whose own `.gitignore` files exclude paths (for example `build/`, `out/`, `vendor/`), when it is preflighted or recovered through any lane (ordinary, captured, or managed checkout), then every extractor, plugin routing, toolchain detection, and Preflight skip the ignored paths and read the same sorted file set as each other. Git's precedence applies: the innermost `.gitignore` decides and `!` re-includes. Non-ignored files are still read, and the fixed skip lists remain the fallback. Only `.gitignore` files inside the tree count, never ancestor, `.git/info/exclude`, or global excludes. Symlinks are never followed. Env-file indexing alone disregards `.gitignore` (ADR-0030).
- **AC-0208** Given any tree and any "Ingest parallelism" setting (Auto or a fixed 1…N workers in Settings, where N is this machine's available parallelism capped at 64; persisted across restarts, and a stored count above today's N is read back, shown, and applied as N), when TS/JS, Python, Go, Java, and Kotlin sources are extracted, then files are parsed on that many workers but merged in sorted walk order. The recovered facts, layer counts, and graph hash set are byte-identical to a serial run, and a failure reports the first failing file in walk order. Auto resolves to max(1, non-efficiency cores − 1), capped at one worker per 2 GiB of physical memory where the platform reports it (macOS, Linux) and at 4 workers where it does not (Windows). Workers run at most a small window ahead of the in-order merge, so peak memory does not grow with the worker count.
- **AC-0217** Given a normal (non-preflight) recovery whose primary source captured its TS/JS files, when they are extracted through the captured lane, then the same "Ingest parallelism" setting that governs the uncaptured path also governs it: files parse on that many workers but every fact and receipt merges on the calling thread strictly in manifest (sorted) order, so the recovered facts and receipts are byte-identical to a serial run for any worker count, and progress reporting still fires in that same order (#482).
- **AC-0423** Given the pinned repositories in `bench/repos.toml`, when `scripts/bench-ingest.mjs` runs locally (macOS or Linux, never CI), then each is cloned at its exact commit without running its code and recovered through the production showcase ingest. One JSON result records, per repository, wall time, max RSS and peak footprint as measurements, and files, nodes, edges, label counts, endpoint auth states, kept receipts and receipt omissions as counts, with the Cartograph commit and platform. An unreadable store is reported as unknown, never zero. `--compare` prints measurement deltas and count differences fails when the two results cover different repositories, and otherwise fails only when counts differ for a repository with the same url, commit and path between two runs the script built from one clean Cartograph commit (a result records whether its tree was uncommitted or changed during the build, and whether its binary was supplied with `--bin`). No threshold gates a run and no numeric ceiling changes (#1228).
- **AC-0526** Given a recovered node or edge, when it is packed for holding until publication, then its props are kept as the exact compact JSON that publication writes (`serde_json::to_string`, key-sorted), and unpacking returns the identical fact: nested key order, array order, numbers (every finite `f64` to the exact bit, as parsing is correctly rounded), escapes and provenance are unchanged, and its complete-fact digest is the same. Props nested deeper than `serde_json` parses (127 arrays or objects) are refused when packed, so every packed fact unpacks. Packing is a memory representation only and changes no fact, digest or stored row (#1518, #1525, #725).
- **AC-0527** Given packed recovered facts and a recovery stage that reads only some node labels, edits them in place and may append nodes, when the stage runs through the fact store over just those labels, then the resulting facts and their order are identical to running the stage over the whole node list; the stage receives the selected nodes as a fixed-length slice plus a separate list for new nodes, so it cannot remove selected nodes, and a stage whose facts cannot be packed leaves the store unchanged (#1518, #725).
- **AC-0528** Given a stored graph, when its snapshot is read packed and a patch of packed upserts is applied conditionally on that snapshot, then the snapshot decodes to the facts the unpacked read returns, the stored rows equal those an unpacked patch writes, and a stale packed snapshot is refused with the graph unchanged (#1523, #725).
- **AC-0529** Given recovered facts published through packed facts, when a stored fact differs from its new occurrence only in JSON spelling of an equal value (`-0.0` and `0.0`), then it counts as unchanged and its stored row is kept; every float keeps its exact value, so a one-ulp difference is a change; colliding nodes and merged edges are counted as before with the last occurrence winning, and republishing the same facts changes no row (#1523, #725).
- **AC-0530** Given packed recovered facts and a recovery stage that selects nodes or edges by identity (id, label, endpoints) rather than by label alone, when the stage runs through the fact store over just that selection, then the resulting facts and their order are identical to running it over the whole lists; an edited edge keeps its slot even when its endpoints change, and a stage whose facts cannot be packed leaves the store unchanged (#1521, #725).
- **AC-0531** Given recovery facts held as plain lists or packed, when the same identity-selected decodes, in-place edits and appends run through the shared fact interface, then both leave identical facts in the same order; and the referential closure over that interface, which decodes only edges with an endpoint no node defines, mints exactly the placeholders of the whole-list closure, with the same order, cited first references and boundary judgements (#1528, #725).
- **AC-0536** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when service-worker runtime marking runs through the shared fact interface, then with no worker registration no fact is decoded or edited; otherwise only File nodes are decoded for their paths, `DEFINED_IN` edges are read by identity, and only the marked File, Symbol and Component nodes are decoded and edited, leaving facts identical to marking the plain lists. A refused fact surfaces as an extraction error (#1536, #725).
- **AC-0537** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when GraphQL test-request completion runs through the shared fact interface, then with no pending request no fact is decoded or edited; otherwise only Endpoint nodes are decoded, and the resolved `REQUESTS` edges and Gap nodes and edges are appended in the same order, leaving facts identical to completing the plain lists. A refused fact surfaces as an extraction error (#1541, #725).
- **AC-0538** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when Vue Router route completion runs through the shared fact interface, then it reads no fact and appends the same Screen nodes, `RENDERS` edges and gaps after the facts already recovered, so both backings end identical. A refused fact surfaces as an extraction error (#1543, #725).
- **AC-0539** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when Vue Router navigation completion runs through the shared fact interface, then only Screen nodes are decoded, only those from Vue Router are targets, and the same `NAVIGATES_TO` edges and gaps are appended, so both backings end identical. A refused fact surfaces as an extraction error (#1543, #725).
- **AC-0540** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when Next.js app-page and route completion run through the shared fact interface, then node ids are read by key, only File nodes are decoded for app pages, and only an already-held Endpoint referenced by a route site is decoded, its matching reconciled in place when it differs; the same Screens, Endpoints, Gaps and edges are appended, so both backings end identical. A refused fact surfaces as an extraction error (#1548, #725).
- **AC-0541** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when pending-call and Pulumi relationship completion run through the shared fact interface, then guessed call targets are reconciled against known symbols, only the targets of calls that need an instance method are decoded, and the same call links, Gaps and resolvable Pulumi relationships (with recomputed content hashes) are appended, so both backings end identical. A refused fact surfaces as an extraction error (#1551, #725).
- **AC-0542** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when Next.js pages-router completion runs through the shared fact interface, then only File nodes are decoded, each page under a `pages/` directory (excluding `_app`/`_document`-style files and `pages/api`) becomes a Screen at its route, and a RENDERS edge to the page's default-export component is appended when one is known, so both backings end identical (#1562, #725).
- **AC-0543** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when guessed-target reconciliation runs through the shared fact interface, then only `file:` and `sym:` edges whose guessed target (extensionless `.ts`, directory index or NodeNext sibling) is proven by the known file or symbol set are decoded and rewritten in their slots, while unproven, `mod:` and already-known targets stay unchanged, so both backings end identical (#1566, #725).
- **AC-0544** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed and a loaded tsconfig/workspace/bundler resolution index, when bare-import resolution runs through the shared fact interface, then only a File's `mod:` IMPORTS are decoded, a specifier the index proves is rewritten to the real file with `resolved_via` and the deciding config cited as evidence, and every other edge stays unchanged, so both backings end identical (#1566, #725).
- **AC-0545** Given recovered TypeScript facts held as plain lists or packed, when the directory walk closes over its endpoints through the shared fact interface, then only edges with an endpoint no node defines are decoded, and the same placeholders are appended in the same order: a repository asset the import names is a Confirmed internal boundary, a bare package is classified against the resolution index, and an unresolved relative import stays a Gap, so both backings end identical (#1566, #725).
- **AC-0546** Given a TypeScript directory walk, when each file's facts merge, then they are packed once, the incremental cache keeps the packed facts rather than a decoded copy, directory completion runs over the packed store, and the walk decodes its output once. A re-ingest under a new commit that reuses an unchanged file and reparses a changed one yields the same nodes and edges as a fresh walk, with no stale commit, and merging packed stores keeps every fact in order (#1571, #725).
- **AC-0547** Given recovered facts held packed, when placeholders that several closures minted for one id are reconciled, then only nodes whose id occurs more than once are decoded, and the store keeps the list form's survivor in the first duplicate's slot and drops the rest (a Gap wins, disagreeing proofs become a Gap, agreeing proofs keep the first, a parsed node leaves the group untouched). Packed facts can also be moved out in order without decoding, and the TypeScript walk can return its facts packed: the same facts in the same order as the decoded walk, with the extraction's own lists left empty (#1574, #725).
- **AC-0548** Given a repository ingest in the app, when recovery runs after the TypeScript walk, then the facts stay packed: other adapters' facts are appended packed, each later pass decodes only the labels it reads (Resource enrichment, Endpoint stitching, test drift, environment links), found ADRs match governed targets by id alone, declared API docs and trace enrichment decode the facts only when they can change anything, closure and placeholder reconciliation run over the packed store, and the published graph is unchanged (#1574, #725).
- **AC-0549** Given a repository ingest in the app, when recovered facts are published, then they reach the graph packed: recovery returns its packed store, receipt bindings decode only the nodes and edges a receipt names (every occurrence of a named edge included), coverage decodes only the nodes it counts, and publication merges and stores packed facts, decoding only edges that repeat a key. The published graph, bindings and coverage equal the decoded path's (#1574, #725).
- **AC-0550** Given a repository that declares API docs and recovered facts held packed, when contract facts are extracted, then only Endpoint nodes are decoded and every other recovered fact is matched by id alone, and the facts equal those extracted from every recovered node: a recovered doc File suppresses the emitted one, and non-Endpoint nodes are never matched as routes (#1574, #725).
- **AC-0593** Given a completed ingest of one source registration at one revision, then the graph content hash recorded for it is shown beside that source in **Workspace** system contents, read from ingest history without reading the graph. Given a spec export, then the bundle states under `graph_basis` the content hash of the whole base graph and each source registration with its revision, computed by the same definition as the recorded hash. Curation decisions and overlay facts do not change either hash, and no equality is claimed across installations for `local/` registrations.
- **Security:** —
- **Performance:** Delta re-ingest scales with change size, not repo size. Per-file extraction scales with worker count (#236), including captured-file extraction on the normal recovery path (#482).

### US-0015 — Security view of the spec
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** auth edges and IAM grants projected into a security view **so that** unauthenticated endpoints and over-broad policies surface as findings.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0041** Given an endpoint with an explicit negative auth fact, when projected, then it is listed as a cited security finding mapped to US-0015/AC-0041; missing auth evidence alone never asserts unauthenticated status. The finding states what was recovered, not that the endpoint is unauthenticated (amended for #1820, AC-0656).
- **AC-0042** Given an IAM `GRANTS` edge with a wildcard action or literal wildcard resource scope, when analyzed, then a confidence-preserving finding lists the exact actions and resource scope and maps to US-0015/AC-0042.
- **AC-0380** Given a recovered Flask endpoint, when its authentication is derived, then `props.auth` is `required` (a `flask_login` `login_required`/`fresh_login_required` or bare `jwt_required()` guard, Confirmed; or a repository decorator whose followed responses include 401/403, InferredStrong), `none` (no decorator below the route and every request hook and `wsgi_app` assignment provably non-blocking, InferredStrong, with the non-repository-call assumption recorded), or `unknown` with sorted reasons, each request hook that may block cited in `blocking_hooks` by its recovered Symbol and registration span, with `blocking_hooks_omitted` counting any past the evidence bound (only the hook's own return value, including the generator a `yield`ing hook returns, ends a request: a called repository function blocks only by raising, aborting or being wrapped by a decorator); a decorator above the route registration is listed as ineffective, a decorated hook or a name shadowed by an enclosing scope is never proof, and the security finding takes the auth fact's own provenance.
- **AC-0383** Given Flask request hooks and endpoints, when authentication is derived, then a hook that may block reaches an endpoint only when both may be the same application: a function-local `Flask()` (its scope's only binding) is identified by its construction, a module-level application by its definition when that assignment is the module's only binding of the name and no module assigns it through an import, and a parameter of a top-level undecorated function by the arguments at every call of its name. Any other receiver, a function name used other than as a callee, a call that omits or spreads the argument, a function with no call, and an unnamed Blueprint registration keep the hook on every endpoint it may reach. A `none` fact past an out-of-scope hook records `request_hooks_reach_only_their_application`.
- **AC-0387** Given a Flask route decorator below the route, when authentication is derived, then an import-proven, unrebound `flask_cors.cross_origin(…)` or a `.limit(…)` / `.shared_limit(…)` on a module-level flask-limiter `Limiter()` (its module's sole binding of the name, never assigned through an imported module, resolved across imports) is non-authenticating when every argument is a literal, a container of literals, or a `lambda` calling only unbound `hasattr`/`getattr`/`isinstance`: it neither guards nor makes the state `unknown`, and a resulting `none` fact lists `non_auth_decorators` and records `rate_limit_and_cors_decorators_do_not_authenticate`. Any other argument, receiver or lookalike stays `unclassified_decorator`.
- **AC-0395** Given a Flask route decorated with a repository decorator, when authentication is derived, then a wrapper, or a check it returns the result of (`return check(…)` or `x = check(…); … return x`), that calls Flask's `abort` with a literal 401 or 403 in its own scope makes the route `required` with basis `decorator_aborts_401_or_403`, citing each abort call; a returned 401/403 is recorded beside it. A computed or other status, an `abort` not imported from `flask`, one rebound in the wrapper, the enclosing decorator or at module level, `flask.abort` replaced anywhere in the repository, and one only inside a nested function or lambda leave the decorator not classified. Aborts are never endpoint responses (#1166).
- **AC-0446** Given a Flask route decorated with a repository decorator that is not a guard, or with a `@factory(…)` call whose arguments are literals and whose factory returns an instance of a class in its own file, when authentication is derived, then the decorator is listed in `non_auth_decorators` (the route `none` when nothing else blocks, recording `non_repository_calls_in_repository_decorators_do_not_abort` and `werkzeug_errors_keep_their_status`) only when its request-time wrapper (the inner function a bare decorator, or the class's `__call__(self, f)`, returns, decorated with nothing but `functools.wraps(f)`) with the view parameter and wrapper name not rebound, returns only the wrapped call or `None`, raises only werkzeug HTTP errors other than 401/403 with literal arguments, never aborts (under any alias), calls an authentication library or a denying name such as `unauthorized` or `verify_jwt_in_request`, yields or awaits, and calls only methods of its own undecorated, base-less class (no dunder but `__init__`/`__call__`, attributes assigned only in `__init__` from literals or its literal-defaulted parameters, followed at most four deep) and repository functions proven not to abort, raise other errors, call an authentication library or be decorated; `from package import module` is read as a submodule only when the package binds no such name; a decorator, factory or class name written elsewhere, a replaced `werkzeug.exceptions` or `functools.wraps`, or any unproven step keeps the factory `unclassified_decorator` and the bare decorator `decorator_not_classified` (#1173).
- **AC-0448** Given a Flask `before_request` / `before_app_request` hook whose every value-return is a two-element `(body, status)` pair with a literal 429 or 5xx status other than 511 (a maintenance or rate response), when authentication is derived, then the hook does not block: undecorated routes it reaches stay `none`, with the assumption `request_hook_statuses_do_not_authenticate` recorded. A 401, 403, 407, 511 (Network Authentication Required), 404, 3xx or 2xx status, a three-element pair (headers), a non-literal status, or any other value-return still makes the hook blocking (#1317).
- **AC-0599** Given a spec export, then `endpoint-permissions.md` lists every recovered endpoint in a framework for which permission sites are recovered, in one of three places: with its recovered permission sites; under "none recovered", only when the adapter's reading was complete and the handler path was read to its end; or as partial, when the path meets an unresolved hop, has no handler, reaches a walk bound or the guard state is unknown. Endpoints in a framework with no permission-site recovery are listed under a coverage note, in neither table, and the document names the frameworks it covers. The document never states that an endpoint is unauthenticated or unprotected. Each row carries tier, confidence and span for the endpoint and each site; `verified-only` omits a row resting on an InferredWeak site and counts it, `best-effort` annotates it; every assertion resolves in the fact index; the same graph yields the same bytes.
- **AC-0656** Given an endpoint whose auth fact is negative (a Flask route with guard state `none`), when the spec is exported, then `security.md` titles the finding "No authentication check recovered: {endpoint}" with Type `no_auth_check_recovered`, states once that a check outside the read source is not visible to the finding, and never contains the word "unauthenticated"; `endpoint-permissions.md` lists the same route under "none recovered"; the finding's stored category, identity hash, tier, confidence and provenance are unchanged.
- **AC-0660** Given a spec export, then `external.md` lists, by one boundary predicate (ADR-0063, SPEC-13): as outside, each literal HTTP origin that an outbound call or observed HTTP client names and no binding citation in the graph covers (a call the binding pass kept without recording its outcome is unclassified), with its sites, spans, the flows that reach it and the data entities read or written on those flows; as unclassified with a reason, each target whose host is not known from source, each SDK or auth-provider client, and each published channel with neither a recovered subscriber nor a backing resource; and it counts, without listing as targets, calls that binding evidence places in a registered repository. A call resolved into another registered repository, a same-origin call, an in-process channel, browser storage and an imported package are not listed. A flow that breaks before the outbound call is not listed as reaching the target and stays partial (R-INT-4). A row carries the tier and confidence of the fact holding its literal and never a higher one; `verified-only` omits InferredWeak rows with a count (R-INT-5); the same graph gives the same bytes.
- **Security:** This story *is* the security projection.
- **Performance:** —
- **Trace:** M9 · `iac`, `spec`, `ui`, `adapters-lang-python` · — · T-0041..0042,T-0380,T-0599,T-0656,T-0660

### US-0016 — Recover WebExtension systems end to end
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** Cartograph to recover a browser extension's entry points, message topology, persisted data, permissions, capabilities, and flows **so that** a real TypeScript WebExtension such as Image Trail compiles into a useful official specification instead of a file-only graph.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- _(Issue #99 drafted these as AC-0055..0059 before that range was assigned; renumbered here.)_
- **AC-0071** Given a Manifest V2/V3 browser extension, when it is ingested, then manifest-declared service workers/background scripts, content scripts, extension pages, toolbar actions, commands, permissions, host permissions, and externally connectable boundaries become deterministic provenance-tagged topology facts (Extension/ExtensionContext/Command nodes with DECLARES/ENTRY edges) and exact-scope GRANTS security facts; declared entry files bind to their `.ts` sources when the built `.js` is absent, a missing entry is an explicit Gap, and the ingest summary reports the WebExtension layer separately.
- **AC-0072** Given import-proven or global Chrome runtime messaging plus explicit message definitions and handler registrations, when the repository is ingested, then deterministic channels and PUBLISHES/SUBSCRIBES edges connect extension contexts; unresolved or dynamic message identities remain explicit Gaps.
- **AC-0073** Given explicit IndexedDB schema/store declarations and repository operations, when ingested, then DataEntity nodes and READS/WRITES relations produce a cited data model.
- **AC-0074** Given recovered WebExtension entry points, message flows, data entities, found ADRs, and permission facts, when the official spec is compiled, then the artifact set contains useful cited assertions and the verified-only output is deterministic across repeat ingest of the same commit.
- **AC-0075** Given an ingest completes with partial or unsupported coverage, when the engineer reviews the result, then the recovery workspace presents artifact readiness, coverage diagnostics, explicit unsupported-pattern guidance, Atlas/Flow/Spec navigation, and evidence context instead of presenting a file graph as a complete analysis.
- **Security:** All new facts are T0 Deterministic/Confirmed or explicit Gap; target code stays read-only; manifest host and API permissions retain their exact scopes.
- **Performance:** Re-ingest uses the existing content-hash cache and determinism guarantees.

### US-0017 — Runtime-loadable adapter plugins
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** to install a gated adapter plugin while Cartograph is running **so that** an unsupported language or event system becomes a recovered layer instead of a permanent finding.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0068** Given a WASM adapter artifact in a discovery directory (project-local `.cartograph/adapters/` beating user-level on id conflict), when Cartograph loads it, then it activates only after a durable conformance-gate job passes SPI contract tests, the generator-supplied golden corpus (expected facts with exact evidence spans), and a double-run determinism check; a failed or ungated adapter stays in the proposed state under the standard propose/accept curation semantics.
- **AC-0069** Given facts emitted by a plugin adapter, when inspected, then their provenance carries `extractor_id@version` plus the plugin artifact's content hash, and re-ingesting the same commit with the same adapter set yields an identical whole-graph content hash (the US-0014 determinism invariant extends to plugin facts).
- **AC-0070** Given a plugin at extraction time, when it attempts network access, a filesystem write, or exceeds its fuel/memory bounds, then the call fails closed with an explicit finding and no partial facts join the graph (amended for #1810, ADR-0062: an exceeded fuel, memory or table bound refuses that file per AC-0658; the other cases fail the run); source access is read-only and host-mediated, and no LLM is ever invoked inside a T0 plugin.
- **AC-0093** Given a Preflight unsupported finding for an uncovered language, when the engineer follows its request-adapter action and a generated artifact lands in a discovery directory, passes the conformance gate, and is enabled for the project, then the next scan reports the language covered by the plugin (the originating unsupported finding closes), extraction routes the files the plugin's golden corpus claims through the plugin with host-pinned facts, and compiled-in adapters always win a contested extension.
- **AC-0594** Given a plugin adapter returns facts, then the host builds every fact's provenance itself: T0 Deterministic and Confirmed, the pinned extractor id, and a content hash computed by the host. A fact's own `prov` contributes evidence spans only, and only when it states Deterministic and Confirmed, writes no other provenance field, and every span names the mediated repository, commit and file with a byte range inside that file. Any other fact fails the file's extraction with a malformed-fact error naming it, and no fact from that file joins the graph (R-INT-2).
- **AC-0658** Given a gated plugin whose extraction of one claimed file fails with a malformed fact, a guest error or wasm trap, or an exceeded fuel, memory or table bound, then that file is refused for that plugin, no fact from that call joins the graph, the ingest continues, and the same tree gives the same graph and refusals on every run; the plugin's coverage row records exact counts by class (`malformed_fact`, `guest_error`, `bound_exceeded`), the refused paths sorted and bounded with an omitted count, and no guest error text; refused files are outside the plugin's file count and the inventory is limited; `coverage.md`, `coverage.json` and the Workspace coverage view list them as not covered by that plugin; the wall-clock deadline, a stack overflow, instantiation and a failure without a trap code still fail the ingest; an inventory with no refusal keeps its bytes and older stored inventories still read (ADR-0062).
- **Security:** AI-authored code runs sandboxed (no network, no writes, bounded resources) per ADR-0017; fail closed.
- **Performance:** Plugin extraction may trail native adapters; first-class languages stay compiled in.
- **Trace:** post-M10 · `adapters-*`, `ingest`, `app`, UI · — · T-0068..0070, T-0093, T-0594, T-0658

### US-0018 — In-app Help, single-sourced with the wiki
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** help inside the app — a native Help menu, an in-app Help view, and contextual per-surface topics — **so that** guidance is reachable without leaving the work, and never drifts from the published wiki.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0090** Given the native app menu, when it renders, then a Help submenu offers Cartograph Help (opens the in-app Help view), User guide — wiki (opens the wiki in the system browser, never the webview), Report an issue (the tracker's new-issue page, system browser), and About Cartograph with the app version.
- **AC-0091** Given the in-app Help view, when opened — from the menu, the command palette, or the `?`/F1 shortcut — then it renders a keyboard-navigable topic TOC (concepts plus every surface) and the selected topic's content entirely from markdown bundled at build time, offline, with no network dependency.
- **AC-0092** Given any surface, when its contextual help entry (the header's Help action) is used, then the Help view opens on that surface's topic; topics are authored once under `docs/help/`; the PR gate validates that source without reading the wiki, and a post-merge workflow mirrors main's topics to the wiki so the two cannot drift.
- **AC-0222** Given the desktop app on macOS, Linux, or Windows, when it opens an external link (the Help submenu's wiki and new-issue pages), then the complete URL — every query parameter after an `&` included — reaches the system browser through the platform's URL handler (`open`, `xdg-open`, or the Windows URL protocol handler, never `explorer`), and on an OS with no known launcher nothing is spawned and the refusal is logged; and on Windows neither that launcher nor the auth ladder's `gh auth token` step (AC-0001, ADR-0009) flashes a console window.
- **Security:** External links open via the system browser; help content ships in the bundle (no runtime fetch).
- **Performance:** Topics render from pre-bundled strings; no I/O on open.
