### US-0019 — Toolchain recovered as cited facts
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** the system's toolchain — frameworks, build tools, package managers, compiler configs, CI, containers — recovered from its config files as cited graph facts **so that** the spec states what the system is built *with*, not just what it does.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0096** Given a tree containing recognized config files (`package.json`, `tsconfig.json`/`jsconfig.json`, lockfiles, `Dockerfile`/`docker-compose.*`, `.github/workflows/*`, language manifests such as `Cargo.toml`/`pyproject.toml`/`go.mod`/`pom.xml`/`build.gradle`), when it is ingested, then each detected tool becomes a Confirmed T0 `Tool` node (`tool:{repo}@{name}`) whose allowlisted resolved settings are props, with `DEFINED_IN` edges to the config `File` node(s) that prove it and provenance citing the exact declaring span; Preflight's framework chips quote the same detection registry as the graph (single source, never disagreeing), and the spec export gains a toolchain artifact fed by the same nodes.
- **AC-0097** Given env files (`.env`, `.env.*`, `*.env` and `env.example`-style templates) or secret-shaped strings in any config, when toolchain facts are extracted, then env files contribute key names only (values are never read into facts), any stored string that looks secret-shaped is dropped rather than stored, and configs authored in code (`vite.config.ts`, `webpack.config.js`, …) are detected by presence only — never evaluated — with an explicit `config-behind-code` Unsupported finding naming the limitation. An observed resource identified by an env key is linked to each env file declaring that key, and the topology map draws that link to the file named by its path, citing no span. Redaction fails closed.
- **AC-0598** Given a tree with GitHub Actions workflows or Dockerfiles, when it is ingested, then each workflow yields its triggers, its jobs with their declared position, `needs` relations and dependency depth, and each job's steps in declared order with their `uses` reference or `run` command; each Dockerfile yields its stages with base image, the relations between stages, its ordered instructions and its final stage. Every fact is T0 Confirmed with the exact byte span it states, and the same tree yields the same facts. Matrices, expressions and reusable workflows are recorded as declared and never evaluated. A secret reference is stored as its name only, `with:` values and `env:` maps are not stored, and command text passes redaction. A file that cannot be read exactly yields no facts and is listed in coverage as present and not read, as are GitLab CI, Jenkins, CircleCI and Azure Pipelines files; a `needs` naming a missing job is an explicit Gap.
- **AC-0650** Given a graph with recovered pipeline facts, when a spec is exported, then `pipelines.md` lists each pipeline with its triggers, its jobs in dependency order and each job's steps in declared order, each row with tier, confidence and source span and resolving in the fact index; a job whose needs do not resolve or form a cycle is stated as not ordered and never placed; `verified-only` leaves out InferredWeak rows and states how many, `best-effort` includes them marked; files present and not read are listed with their recorded reason; the reader's remaining limits are stated in every export; the same graph yields the same bytes. `Containerfile` and `*.dockerfile` names are read, and `RUN --mount=…,from=<stage>` is a stage relation.
- **AC-0661** Given a repository with Compose files, when it is ingested, then each file that parses and has a `services` mapping yields one `Environment` identified by its path, named only where the file states a top-level `name`, and one `Workload` per service with its image, ports, environment key names (never values), env files, dependencies and profiles, each with a span (ADR-0070, SPEC-14); a construct the reader does not evaluate, an unparseable file, and a dependency on an undeclared service yield Gaps; an environment file whose environment is carried only by its name yields a Gap and no `Environment`; Kubernetes manifests, kustomize and Helm files are recorded in coverage as present and not read. Given a spec export, then `environments.md` lists the environments, their workloads, each workload name declared in more than one environment with the properties that differ and both spans, and the Gaps; no path is shown as an environment name; the same commit gives the same facts and bytes.
- **AC-0662** Given a repository with Kubernetes manifests and kustomization files, when it is ingested, then each `Deployment`, `StatefulSet`, `DaemonSet` and `Job` document yields a `Workload` with its containers (image, ports, environment key names, referenced ConfigMap and Secret names; never a value or any `data`), each kustomization file yields an `Environment` named only where it states a `namespace`, with `DECLARES` to every such workload its `resources` chain reaches inside the repository and `PATCHES` for each patch-file document that matches one, stating only what the patch writes (ADR-0070, SPEC-14); remote or missing entries, unmatched and inline patches, JSON patches, cycles and unevaluated transformers yield Gaps naming them; other manifest kinds and Helm files are recorded in coverage as present and not read. Given a spec export, then `environments.md` shows a workload reached by several environments once per environment with that environment's patch and image overrides, lists workloads no environment declares, and merges nothing; the same commit gives the same facts and bytes.
- **Security:** AC-0097 is the security surface: fail-closed redaction (AC-0009 standard); no config value is stored unless allowlisted and non-secret-shaped.
- **Performance:** Only name-matched config files are read; one sorted deterministic walk shared with Preflight's skip set.
- **Trace:** post-M10 · `ingest`, `core-graph`, `spec`, `app`, UI · ADR-0058, ADR-0060, ADR-0070 · T-0096..0097,T-0598,T-0650,T-0661,T-0662

### US-0020 — Query one evidence-bound context through shared transports
- **Actor:** Human developer or development agent
- **As a** context consumer **I want** bounded, revision-bound reads of recovered facts **so that** the app and agent integrations can cite the same project evidence.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0101** Given a recovered graph, when a snapshot is built or paged, then reordered facts yield the same snapshot identity, any property change invalidates it, duplicate identities fail explicitly, and stale or mismatched cursors are rejected.
- **AC-0102** Given item and serialized-response byte budgets, when a query returns a page, then the entire response fits both budgets and exposes continuation explicitly; invalid budgets or a next fact that cannot fit fail rather than silently skipping it.
- **AC-0103** Given valid, absent, malformed, or ceiling-violating provenance, when a fact is returned, then only validated provenance is authoritative, invalid provenance is explicitly Gap, raw provenance is removed from properties, and inferred facts retain their confidence ceiling.
- **AC-0104** Given a label or neighborhood selection, when queried, then exact labels and the bounded undirected neighborhood select deterministically; absent domain facts return no invented concepts and unknown anchors fail explicitly.
- **AC-0105** Given the app context-query command, when invoked, then graph copying and query work run off the calling thread, read nodes and edges in one database snapshot even across multiple app processes, return the recovered-graph view, and preserve graph contents without model or network calls.
- **AC-0137** Given multiple database connections, when full or label-filtered graph snapshots are read through GraphStore, then nodes and edges belong to one SQLite revision; selections/order and malformed-unselected-property behavior are preserved, and paired counts are coherent without materializing facts.
- **AC-0138** Given legacy Atlas, spec, flow/topology, findings, metrics or semantic-preview graph reads, when another process commits between collection reads, then the application uses the shared coherent contract and performs downstream computations after the read transaction ends, preserving existing output/filter policy.
- **AC-0139** Given a node or edge property read failure, when a graph projection fails and its source is repaired, then the same reader can observe a later valid revision; tests exercise real spec/Atlas adapters without timing races, and no claim is made of atomic ingest or cross-store publication.
- **AC-0232** Given a coherent snapshot and a query by exact identifier, name or text under a declared, versioned matching policy, when deterministic discovery runs, then it returns bounded candidate handles with label, source identity, match reason, counts, coverage and omissions; same-name hits in different repos stay separate candidates and no candidate becomes a binding or a relationship.
- **AC-0233** Given a discovery continuation cursor, when the snapshot, normalized query or matching policy version differs from the one the cursor was issued for, then the continuation is rejected and no page mixes identities; an object that cannot fit the response budget fails explicitly instead of being skipped.
- **AC-0302** Given a graph store with the exact supported private generation schema, every committed insert/update/delete on nodes, edges, source-node/source-edge bindings or coverage inventories advances its database-local revision atomically through guarded row triggers, including writes from an already-open older connection. Rollback restores revision and publication state together; signed-counter exhaustion is a typed refusal without partial changes. An instance/revision token identifies storage mutation order, not content identity, recovery completion, or a transaction spanning other stores (#744, ADR-0047).
- **AC-0303** Given a legacy compatible store, additive initialization preserves facts, associations, coverage and instance identity; partial, future, foreign or oversized private schema metadata is refused before repair/body copies. Version/reset decisions occur under the write transaction; destructive reset including an empty reset advances revision atomically, and failure preserves prior state. A revisioned bounded read observes its revision and rows in one read transaction; oversized/malformed instance metadata is refused before String allocation (#744, ADR-0047).
- **AC-0460** Given an exact recovered node ID, when the context query selects `node { id }`, then it returns only that node, never neighbors or edges including self-loops; an unknown ID fails explicitly. Existing kind/label filters and response budgets apply, reference membership agrees with paged results, and cursors cannot cross node IDs or other scopes. Qualified queries preserve coverage identity. Existing neighborhood bounds and historical specialist schemas remain unchanged (#1350).
- **Security:** Read-only core API; no SQL, arbitrary filesystem paths, model execution, or transport-specific privilege in requests.
- **Performance:** At most 500 facts, 1 MiB serialized response and three hops; large-graph snapshot caching/latency is a later measured gate, not guaranteed by output bounds.
- **Trace:** H1 (SPEC-01) · `context-hub`, `core-graph`, `app` · — · T-0101..0105,T-0137..0139,T-0232..0233,T-0302..0303,T-0460 · ADR-0047

### US-0021 — Inspect and curate a persistent business-domain context
- **Actor:** Human developer
- **As a** developer **I want** domains linking features, rules, designs, decisions, tests and gaps **so that** I can understand behavior and intended architecture in one project context.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Ready
- **AC-0106** Given the pinned external benchmark and declared configuration, when recovered, then features and business rules cite source evidence and report measured coverage against an independently reviewed expected set, with unsupported paths explicit.
- **AC-0107** Given documented intent, inferred interpretations and future designs, when viewed beside implementation facts, then their meanings and producing tiers remain distinct and a missing document is described as unknown intent.
- **AC-0108** Given an accepted proposal, when context, UI and exports are read, then the same reviewed projection includes it without upgrading its tier; source changes mark affected proposals stale until reconciled.
- **AC-0652** Given the base graph, the reviewed staged proposals with their basis assessments and the Workbench assertion decisions, when the curated projection is computed, then every accepted proposal gets exactly one state, the first that applies of `unverified`, `already-covered`, `unresolvable`, `stale`, `conflicted` and `eligible`, with a reason; only `eligible` records are curated items and every other accepted record is listed; pending and rejected proposals are neither; tier and confidence are carried as produced; an assertion decision that matches no current assertion is listed as stale; the curated revision is the same for the same inputs in any order, changes when a decision, a state, a reason, the graph hash or the policy version changes, and does not change with a review note, a timestamp or a review counter. No surface reads the projection yet.
- **AC-0653** Given reviewed staged proposals, when a spec is exported, then the bundle carries the curated revision and counts beside the graph basis and includes `curated.md`, which lists each eligible accepted proposal with its record identity, proposed edge, Gap, tier and confidence as produced, review status, source support and state, and every other accepted proposal with its state and reason; `verified-only` contains no item and states how many it left out, `best-effort` includes them marked InferredWeak; an item cites its Gap and endpoints through the fact index where the export admits them; accepting a proposal changes only `curated.md` and the curated basis, never the graph basis or another artifact; a projection that cannot be computed leaves the export intact and is stated with its reason; the artifact read alone equals the bundle's; work packets refuse the document.
- **AC-0654** Given a complete MCP read session, when the curated documents are read, then `curated` serves the same bytes and content identity as the export for the same inputs and mode and `curated-json` serves the same projection as data with the DR-4 fields; the read basis carries the curated revision or the reason it was not computed, and the basis identity changes with it; `h3_curated_available` is true only when the projection was computed for that session; acquisition is stale when a review or a Workbench rejection changes while it runs; no review is gathered before the session's bounded graph read succeeds; an agent read never compiles every document, so a Workbench decision that matches no stored fact leaves the projection not computed with that reason and fails no other read; a `verified-only` session gets no item, only the count; notes, timestamps, and pending or rejected records are not served; a scoped session reports the read as unavailable.
- **AC-0655** Given the Spec Workbench, when the documents are loaded, then a curated section shows the view, the curated revision of the loaded bundle, the counts, each curated item with its record identity, proposed edge, Gap, tier and confidence as produced and marked InferredWeak, review status, source support, basis and state, every other accepted record grouped by state with its reason, and the Workbench decisions that match nothing; the section and the bundle come from one computation, so they carry one revision; `verified-only` shows no item and the count left out; a projection that was not computed is stated with its reason; the section reloads after a review and after a change of export mode without polling; proposal history and the review dialog state an accepted record's state and reason.
- **AC-0659** Given a spec export or a single-artifact read from the desktop, then its work runs off the UI thread and holds no graph or decision lock after the snapshot read; the basis assessments of one export share one graph copy and identity, each still checked against that copy's store revision (a moved store is read again, and an assessment at another revision is stale); a Workbench decision that no stored fact carries costs one extra compile per store revision, graph content hash and set of such decisions, settled in memory only and never for an MCP read; the curated revision, counts and `curated.md` are the same as without sharing or settling.
- **AC-0109** Given multiple named project contexts, when the app restarts or changes project, then each domain's evidence, review history and task references remain scoped to its project and revision.
- **Security:** Agents propose only; human acceptance cannot mint confirmed facts.
- **Performance:** Domain queries expose scope and uncovered portions rather than claiming a complete inventory from partial extraction.
- **Trace:** H2–H3 (SPEC-01, SPEC-12) · `context-hub`, `agents`, `spec`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0058, ADR-0061 · T-0106..0109,T-0652..0655,T-0659

### US-0024 — Recover source-backed rule evidence with real lexical owners
- **Actor:** Developer investigating business behavior
- **As a** developer **I want** cited callable owners and guarded-exit evidence **so that** business rules can be recovered without guessed dispatch or stronger predicates than the code establishes.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0119** Given TypeScript object methods and property-bound callbacks, when extracted, then each has exact T0 provenance, a real scope-qualified symbol and DEFINED_IN edge; same-named members of different objects and objects nested inside classes remain distinct.
- **AC-0120** Given nested or shadowed callable declarations, when their calls are extracted, then the nearest actual callable owns the calls and direct-call targets require lexical binding proof; unrelated file-wide name matches and unknown member dispatch do not become confirmed links; nested Nest decorators require their actual unshadowed imported bindings.
- **AC-0121** Given unchanged source and existing unambiguous top-level/class callables, when re-extracted, then identities and content hashes are deterministic and existing identities remain stable; anonymous route symbol and endpoint ownership continue to agree, and nested Nest controllers bind HANDLES to the actual qualified decorated-method symbol.
- **AC-0122** Given supported guarded returns/throws, when recovered, then source conditions, branch polarity, owner, local effect and exact evidence are retained without treating return strings as rejection or an ancestor-condition list as a complete execution predicate.
- **AC-0123** Given unresolved calls, mutation, loops or exception control flow, when rule evidence is recovered, then unsupported behavioral interpretation and dependencies remain explicit gaps rather than omitted prerequisites or invented semantics; parser-recovered exits or controlling branches yield an explicit unsupported_rule_syntax omission Gap rather than Confirmed rule evidence or a silently dropped throw.

- **AC-0125** Given recovered rule evidence, when the inventory, shared context and exports are inspected, then owner, conditions, local effect, dependencies, confidence and incomplete interpretation are consistent; computed callable keys disclose omitted source names and unresolved runtime keys; the pinned benchmark records only fully supported matches.
- **Security:** No model or source execution; no new raw predicate/return text stored before the redaction contract is implemented.

### US-0025 — Resume review of immutable agent proposals
- **Actor:** Human developer
- **As a** developer **I want** agent results staged durably and reviewed by identity **so that** I can resume after restart and know the exact evidence and interpretation I accepted.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0126** Given a bounded host task and broker result, when staged, then complete proposal content, provenance, task/run identity, a graph snapshot read coherently under AC-0105 and a versioned basis manifest determine an immutable identity; validation rejects unsupported candidate/citation changes, repeated identical staging is idempotent, task source fields persist only as fingerprints, and bounded pre-write annotation validation rejects complete nonempty whitespace-normalized supplied text or contiguous 48-scalar excerpts without echoing source in diagnostics or changing accepted content.
- **AC-0127** Given a staged proposal, when reviewed by ID and expected review revision, then the host atomically records the decision without changing proposal content; unknown IDs and stale revisions fail, and both pending and reviewed records survive restart.
- **AC-0128** Given single or class escalation, when a result completes, then it is durably staged before reported success; failed persistence is a failed result and cancellation preserves already completed staged instances.
- **AC-0129** Given staged history after restart, when the review surface opens, then pending and reviewed results can be retrieved with bounded explicit pagination and reviewed by ID; accepted status states the record's curated state and its reason where the projection gives one (amended for #1746, ADR-0061; it formerly stated that shared-context reconciliation is pending).
- **AC-0130** Given current working-tree evidence assembly, when its task is staged or accepted, then fingerprints bind the exact supplied text while the record explicitly retains unverified source binding and pending reconciliation; a commit-shaped citation, unchanged snapshot or human acceptance cannot certify freshness.
- **AC-0131** Given staging and human review, when recovered graph/context/export state and legacy decisions are compared, then recovered facts and producing tiers remain unchanged and legacy caller-body decisions are not activated as staged proposals; full H3 projection remains an explicit later gate.
- **Security:** No raw task-source archive, caller-body review authority, source writes or model execution from staging/review.
- **Performance:** Staged records have a 128 KiB payload cap; history reads have bounded pages and explicit continuation.
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite (SPEC-01/SPEC-03) · `agents`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0126..0131

### US-0026 — Retain exact source captures for recovery evidence
- **Actor:** Recovery engine and developer inspecting evidence
- **As a** developer **I want** bounded immutable source captures **so that** later evidence can refer to the bytes actually retained without trusting a mutable checkout or a commit-shaped label.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0132** Given selected raw source buffers and a host-owned repository identity, when a capture is built, then a versioned canonical manifest binds kind, membership, paths, lengths and digests; reordered selection is identical while changed bytes, repository or kind are distinct, without serializing raw bytes in metadata or diagnostics.
- **AC-0133** Given selected local paths, when working-tree capture runs, then rooted regular-file reads reject symlinks, escaping paths, special files and exceeded limits; the acquired immutable buffer survives later file mutation without claiming an atomic filesystem or Git snapshot.
- **AC-0134** Given a full local Git commit and selected paths, when Git-object capture runs, then exact tree/blob bytes are retained independently of checkout conversion and HEAD movement; unsupported entries and missing objects fail without hooks, filters, shell commands or network fallback.
- **AC-0135** Given a capture and a bounded local store, when persisted and reopened, then manifests and byte objects publish atomically, identical persistence is idempotent, corrupted or mismatched identity fails closed, capacity errors preserve prior data, and strict nonempty bounded span reads validate the complete file reference before slicing the same buffer; restart retains the effective caller span cap, duplicate persistence can only tighten it without changing content identity, and missing or invalid stored policy fails closed.
- **AC-0136** Given a retained captured buffer, when the real TS parser consumes it and its span is read after source mutation and store restart, then the parser and reader see the captured bytes while legacy EvidenceRef and staged identities remain unchanged; this fixture does not certify production ingestion or complete H3.
- **Security:** Local selected-file acquisition only; no target writes, repository execution, model calls or network; private raw-byte retention is separate from graph/proposal/export payloads and does not authorize egress.
- **Performance:** Hard acquisition, path, manifest, span and logical-store limits from SPEC-04; failures return explicitly without silently incomplete capture.
- **Trace:** H3 (SPEC-01), #387 prerequisite of #385 · `source-capture`, `spec` · — · T-0132..0136


### US-0027 — Keep registered sources distinct and resolve evidence on the host
- **Actor:** Engineer and context consumer
- **As a** engineer **I want** lasting source identities and exact host-owned source lookup **so that** similarly named projects never share recovered ownership or display another project's code.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0140** Given separate local roots or linked worktrees, when registered and reopened through independent connections, then the same canonical root retains its opaque identity and separate roots remain distinct even with equal basenames or Git metadata; missing/moved/replaced roots follow the explicit logical-registration semantics in SPEC-05 without a physical-continuity claim.
- **AC-0141** Given normalized managed origins including same-basename local mirrors, when a clone is added or replaced, then the host reserves separate owned destinations before cloning, cleans only its own failed attempt, rejects unowned or substituted destinations, and preserves the existing authentication/no-partial-clone contract.
- **AC-0142** Given registered sources, when preflight/gate re-scan, direct ingest, manifest-local or clone intake runs, then adapters, caches, findings, reconciliation and metrics use one registered repository key; re-ingesting one source preserves another's facts and root-specific plugin permissions do not transfer.
- **AC-0143** Given a selected evidence reference, when the UI or agent task reader requests source, then the host resolves the exact registered repository and preserves bounded rooted reads; unknown, unavailable or legacy-unmapped references return unavailable without a first-repository or caller-root fallback and without claiming revision freshness.
- **AC-0144** Given a registered repository and revision label, when Repo facts and system display are produced, then canonical fact properties/hash omit operational roots and mutable display metadata, while an operational DTO supplies human labels and equal names remain disambiguated by exact repository identity; repeated recovery for a retained registration is stable without claiming cross-installation graph equality.
- **AC-0145** Given a new source-bound ingest job or a legacy path-only job, when retry is requested, then the new job resolves its registered source and the legacy job requires explicit fresh ingestion before any retry mutation; original job kind/history remains unchanged.
- **AC-0146** Given legacy graph/findings and durable history, when identity migration or later graph/job cleanup runs, then disposable legacy graph ownership is rebuilt and generated findings retire once, while registered sources and historical jobs, metrics, decisions and staged proposal JSON/IDs/reviews remain unchanged.
- **AC-0147** Given concurrent managed-source operations or an owning process that exits, when guards are acquired, then shared readers exclude replacement, exclusive guards last through parser/enrichment use and publication, contention fails without lock cycles, and OS handle closure releases ownership without making an interrupted source ready; guards are acquired before graph/cache locks and nested work reuses them. ADR snapshot validation and its fact patch share one SQLite write transaction, rejecting stale input without writes and rolling back failed patches.
- **Security:** Host lookup never accepts webview overwrite roots; source locks and registry identities grant no egress, target-code edits, source-verification flags or tier upgrades. Existing path/artifact consent stays root-specific.
- **Performance:** Bounded identities/origins and short SQLite registration transactions; try-only source locking never holds a database transaction across clone or extraction. Whole-graph/ADR snapshot costs remain explicit.
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite #392 · `app`, `ingest`, `core-graph`, `ui` · — · T-0140..0147


### US-0028 — Inspect the retained primary source that produced a rule
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** source observations bound to the bytes their parser consumed **so that** later edits cannot silently change the evidence I inspect.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0148** Given registered TS/JS sources, when the participating production lane runs, then bounded exact enumeration that never follows, opens or resolves a symlink and captured buffers feed the real parser without participating cache reuse or live-read fallback, preserving explicit incomplete input coverage. A non-ignored, non-excluded symlink is skipped as the ordinary walk skips it (ADR-0030), so for UTF-8 trees both lanes select identical files; recovery completes, and coverage names the skipped symlinks as one `symlink-not-followed` limitation (count and first path) instead of refusing the whole tree. A non-UTF-8 name still refuses the tree, and a refusal names its fixed reason (#1229).
- **AC-0149** Given direct lexical rules, their owners and GOVERNS edges, when parsed from a CapturedFile, then versioned immutable metadata-only receipts bind the complete emitted fact, producer/grammar, source/capture and all original ranges; eval, placeholders and enrichment cannot acquire this binding by matching spans.
- **AC-0150** Given current facts and proposed receipts, when repository reconciliation publishes, then facts and exact current receipt associations commit atomically, mismatched final facts lose participation, ordinary mutations invalidate associations, and identical facts from different captures retain distinct receipt identities.
- **AC-0151** Given direct, retry, clone or manifest recovery, when participating facts are published, then captures and receipts are already retained under the exact registered source, same-named roots stay isolated and a persistence/capacity failure publishes no participating binding.
- **AC-0152** Given a typed fact, expected receipt and stored range index, when captured inspection runs after source edits/restart, then coherent host checks return the original strict UTF-8 bytes or explicit stale/unavailable status without arbitrary offsets, caller roots or working-tree fallback.
- **AC-0153** Given retained source, when capacity or forgetting is handled, then private bounded stores and source guards preserve immutable receipts/reviews, a refreshed inventory preview authorizes deletion of only that source's unshared bytes, and graph/job cleanup never deletes retained historical source. On Unix, revalidation walks the retained canonical application path without following replaced symlink components or producing a new resolved-path string; original directory identity must still match.
- **AC-0154** Given the evidence and retention UI, when the user changes selection, inspects captured/current source or confirms forgetting, then the UI preserves exact fact/receipt/source identity, labels captured primary source with incomplete input coverage, rejects stale async results and previews affected references before deletion.
- **AC-0155** Given legacy graph/proposals and unrelated producers, when primary-source recovery and inspection run, then legacy EvidenceRef/staging bytes, producing tier/confidence and review state remain unchanged, raw source stays absent from graph/context/export/proposal payloads and H2/H3 verification is not inferred.
- **AC-0280** Given a captured TS/JS file, when it is parsed, then every directly emitted callable declaration also receives a provenance-only primary receipt for its exact span, so code that owns no rule is citable; rule receipts are admitted first (their own budget behavior is AC-0292) and declarations only use the remaining extraction budget, minted under a per-file bound and admitted greedily in manifest order, with each omitted declaration counted under its file path and never silently dropped (#691).
- **AC-0292** Given a captured TS/JS repository whose rule receipts exceed the extraction receipt budget, or a retained receipt store without room for a recovery's receipts, when it is recovered, then the recovery completes: receipts are admitted greedily in deterministic manifest order, every rule fact without a receipt stays in the graph and is counted under its file path, store omissions are counted the same way, and coverage names each kind under its own limiting capability (`ts-rule-receipts`, `receipt-store-capacity`) as a tool limitation. Integrity failures still reject the recovery, already-stored receipts are never removed, and bindings cite only receipts the store actually retained (#722).
- **AC-0282** Given a captured TS/JS file, when it is parsed, then each top-level non-callable variable, each first-level property of its object-literal initializer, each method decorator and the header of each callable larger than the 8 KiB inspection range also gets its own fact and a provenance-only primary receipt for its exact span; existing Symbol spans and identities are unchanged, quoted keys are never persisted, a header that alone exceeds the range is flagged as a named gap, and a section or header too large for one range is flagged as a named gap with oversized sections described one level deeper, and the coverage inventory names declarations left without a receipt as a limitation (#693, ADR-0039).
- **AC-0283** Given a captured TS/JS callable larger than the 8 KiB inspection range, when it is parsed, then each non-callable `const`/`let`/`var` declarator directly in its body also gets a citable Variable fact scoped to that callable (with its object-literal sections, bounded as in AC-0282); nested blocks, nested callables and callables within the range are unchanged, and these locals are minted after the file's own declarations so a tight budget omits only locals, which are counted (#699, ADR-0039).
- **AC-0299** Given a newly recovered TypeScript fact, when its primary-source receipt is minted, then it uses contract v3 (`ts-primary-v3:`). Each range is stored only as role, index and byte span, and its evidence and captured-file binding are derived from the receipt's single file, repo key and commit. Decoding expands v3 to the same ranges as the equivalent v2 receipt, so resolver, inspection, MCP and history behave unchanged. Exactly one wire encoding is valid per receipt: a range form that doesn't match its version, an unknown or duplicated field, an explicit `null` commit, a range count over the limit, or an expansion over the per-receipt byte bound are all rejected. v1 and v2 receipts keep decoding with unchanged ids (#725, ADR-0046).
- **AC-0301** Given a retained source the user confirms forgetting, when its capture bytes are removed, then in one transaction its receipts pinned by a saved investigation or a staged proposal keep their metadata, marked capture-forgotten and bound to the tombstone of the forget that first marked them, and every other receipt of that source is reclaimed. A tombstone records the source, the forgotten captures, the time and the reclaimed and pinned counts, and a reclaim ledger maps each reclaimed receipt id to it. An exact receipt id then resolves as present, capture-forgotten, reclaimed or unknown, never by source or path. The ledger is bounded by the existing receipt ceiling: the oldest entries are dropped first and then resolve as unknown. Tombstones are kept only while a ledger row or a marked receipt references them, and their recorded capture ids share one store-wide byte pool bounded by the receipt store's byte ceiling; when it is full a forget is still recorded, with its capture ids marked truncated. Recapturing identical bytes makes a capture-forgotten receipt present again. If the reclaim fails after capture deletion, the next confirmed forget completes it. The receipt store migrates from v1 to v2 in one transaction, a failure leaves v1 intact, and fresh and migrated stores have identical schemas (#725, ADR-0046 decision 3). Saved work packets also pin the receipts they cite: a packet that cites a source cannot be saved while that source is being forgotten, and the store refuses any packet body saved without its receipt index, so an older app instance still holding the store open cannot write unpinned packets (#749).
- **AC-0314** Given a recovered fact whose primary receipt was omitted because a declaration, rule or store-capacity receipt bound was reached, when the repository is published, then the graph store records a positive per-fact omission record bound to the complete fact digest the omitted receipt was emitted for (kept only while it equals the published fact's digest, and never for a fact that has a retained receipt binding; a later binding from any repository also takes precedence over an existing record when the fact is selected) alongside a per-repository omission index, migrating exact v1 association schemas to v2 in one transaction and invalidating omission rows whenever a fact is rewritten or deleted. Each fact in a bounded source selection carries its omission outcome, read in the same transaction and against the same copied graph revision as its association state, with the observed store revision returned and an expected-revision variant that refuses a changed basis (omission row events advance the revision even when no fact changes): `Omitted` with its repository key and omission kind when its digest in that revision matches a recorded omission row, `NotOmitted` only when every covered repository has a complete omission index and no row matches, and `Unrecorded` otherwise; the repository index is recorded as `incomplete` when omissions exceed the 262,144-record bound or when a producer only has bare per-file counts without fact keys, and `NotOmitted` never means a receipt exists, which only a current source binding establishes (#725).
- **AC-0319** Given a recovery of a source that completes and publishes its graph, when no other recovery of that source holds the source's retention guard, then every receipt of that source not pinned (by a saved investigation, a staged proposal, a saved work packet or a prepared task) and not bound by the current graph is reclaimed into the ledger under a tombstone with cause `superseded`, and resolves as superseded (unavailable, never forgotten). Pinned and capture-forgotten rows are untouched and capture bytes are kept. A busy source is skipped and settles on its next publish. A task prepared before or during the reclaim keeps its receipts. Existing tombstones migrate with cause `forgotten` (#725, ADR-0046 decision 5).
- **AC-0373** Given receipts persisted to the receipt store, when a v3 receipt is stored, then its per-file header is stored once per distinct header and source and the receipt as a compact body, every read rebuilds byte-identical canonical receipt bytes and id or fails closed (missing, foreign or contradictory header or body), the store budget charges each retained header once plus every body, forget and supersession remove a header only with its last referencing row, legacy rows stay readable unchanged, and an exact v3 store migrates to v4 in one transaction (#725, ADR-0046 decision 7).
- **AC-0497** Given a receipt store shared by several retained sources, when one source's recovery persists its receipts, then each receipt that would take the recovery's retained rows past that source's share, a ceiling proportional to its largest retained capture's bytes among all sources' (never below a sixteenth of the store, never above it, by count and by bytes with each header charged once), becomes a named omission instead of failing; already-stored identical rows stay retained and count against the share, and the store-wide bound still applies (#725).
- **AC-0503** Given a TypeScript directory recovery, when its receipts are held before persistence, then each file's identity is kept once and shared by every receipt minted for that file, each range keeps only its role, index and byte span, and the full receipt is rebuilt on demand with byte-identical canonical JSON and id. The extraction and per-file declaration budgets measure this retained memory, charging each shared header once, only when the first receipt under it is admitted; a refused receipt charges nothing. The ceilings are unchanged, so the same budget now holds more receipts, and omissions are still named by fact. The host checks, persists and binds the compact form directly, serializing one receipt at a time instead of holding the whole batch's JSON (#725, ADR-0046 decision 7).
- **Security:** Private local retention only; no target writes, implicit network, model disclosure, arbitrary-root reads or confidence upgrades. Deletion is exact-source and preview-bound.
- **Performance:** SPEC-04 capture limits, bounded enumeration and receipts, selected-object span reads and try-only locks; participating TS cache bypass is explicit.
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite #395 · `adapters-lang-ts`, `source-capture`, `core-graph`, `ingest`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0039, ADR-0046 · T-0148..0155,T-0280,T-0282,T-0283,T-0299,T-0301,T-0314,T-0319,T-0373,T-0497


### US-0029 — Keep job ownership accurate across concurrent processes and retries
- **Actor:** Engineer running recovery and agent investigations
- **As a** engineer **I want** each live job attempt to keep exclusive execution ownership **so that** another startup or retry cannot steal it or accept stale worker updates.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0156** Given a new or legacy job store, when execution metadata initializes and rows are returned, then a private versioned namespace and recorded new-job attempts coexist with unchanged historical fields, while ownerless legacy rows remain explicitly unknown and require fresh operations rather than same-ID adoption or retry.
- **AC-0157** Given participating job workers, when execution is claimed or cloned into blocking work, then a host-owned persistent try-only OS guard excludes another claim until the last worker holder exits, without a database mutex spanning lock acquisition or a substituted lock granting ownership; before SQL claim, its file and rooted directory entries are synced, Windows handles must report NTFS and permit real write-access directory flushes, and unsupported/query/sync failures release ownership with the job unchanged.
- **AC-0158** Given an execution handle, when progress, checks, finish or fail occur, then the exact store/job/attempt/owner and allowed status fence every update; same-attempt cancellation remains unchanged and missing, interrupted or superseded ownership stops work distinctly.
- **AC-0159** Given another startup or confirmed owner exit, when recovery runs, then busy live attempts remain untouched and only the exact observed still-running recorded candidates whose locks were acquired become interrupted, without overwriting concurrent terminal transitions or reporting unchanged rows.
- **AC-0160** Given cancellation, cleanup or supported retry, when transitions race, then guarded transactions preserve the winning status, live cancelled workers exclude retry, source/kind admission precedes mutation and one retry atomically claims the next attempt without stale writes or an unowned queued interval.
- **AC-0161** Given plugin, ingest, add and single/class escalation workers, when progress, publication, cancellation or async waiter loss occurs, then all production lifecycle writes use the retained execution, blocking closures preserve ownership, and completed proposals remain staged and reviewable after cancellation under AC-0128, including an already-started model result that completes after job cleanup; missing history still stops subsequent work and lifecycle writes.
- **AC-0162** Given the Jobs surface, when legacy-unknown work or a busy/rejected retry is shown, then historical state is not presented as proven live or retryable, fresh recovery guidance is explicit and action failures remain visible without inventing a successful transition.
- **AC-0163** Given history and independent source/evidence contracts, when ownership migration, retry, recovery or job cleanup runs, then existing job kinds/fields and staged proposal IDs/content/reviews remain intact, tracking grants no tier/source freshness upgrade and source guards retain their separate scope.
- **Security:** Private host-generated ownership only; no caller tokens, force takeover, target writes, added egress or implicit model replay.
- **Performance:** Short SQLite transitions, owned plans and try-only OS reservations; no mutex spans long work or blocking lock acquisition.
- **Trace:** Durable runtime prerequisite #393 (SPEC-07) · `app`, `ui` · — · T-0156..0163

### US-0030 — Inspect cited local definitions behind rule conditions
- **Actor:** Engineer or agent investigating business-rule evidence
- **As a** engineer **I want** rule conditions linked to supported local initializer structure and its gaps **so that** I can inspect source reasoning without mistaking it for established runtime or business meaning.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0164** Given stored rule evidence, when v1 or v2 is read or newly produced, then v1 retains its original wire meaning and bytes, v2 requires bounded local-definition evidence, and null, malformed or unsupported version combinations fail explicitly without rewriting historical content.
- **AC-0165** Given a condition dependency, when a local definition is recovered, then the declaration is a scope-proven directly initialized simple const in the same callable with structured before-use proof; shadowing, TDZ, mutation, unsupported scope crossings and non-admitted bindings retain explicit gaps.
- **AC-0166** Given an admitted initializer, when its expression is captured, then a cited flat arena preserves known operator/operand order, grouping, short-circuit and member syntax without substitution or evaluation, while runtime values and unsupported forms remain explicitly unestablished.
- **AC-0167** Given source expressions or excessive analysis, when local definitions are emitted, then every stored display/literal uses the source-aware sanitizer and all definition/node/dependency/depth/byte limits fail visibly without leaking withheld text or claiming truncated expressions complete.
- **AC-0168** Given nested definition evidence and revision changes, when source traversal or ordinary cache retargeting runs, then every declaration, use, initializer, expression, dependency and redaction reference is visited in the declared order and the complete fact hash reflects the actual revision.
- **AC-0169** Given captured parsing and historical receipts, when v2 definitions are produced or current facts change, then new versioned receipts bind the complete fact and every original range, v1 identities remain unchanged and readable, and oversized, stale, missing or mismatched inventories cannot acquire a partial or substituted current association.
- **AC-0170** Given shared context and the source rule inventory, when definitions are shown, then both surfaces retain the same structured evidence and citations, safely render stored expressions, disclose v1 evidence absence and unresolved value/business meaning, and preserve provenance filtering and the original condition.
- **AC-0171** Given the frozen Vendure oracle/input, when the local-definition increment is assessed, then repeated source-only runs and an independent source audit record separate revision/hash/score artifacts, preserve the eight-rule denominator and 41-case obligations, and distinguish definition improvement from complete rules or producer input closure.
- **Security:** T0 is model-free; no target writes, raw-source rereads in shared projections, secret-bearing fallbacks, tier upgrades or historical proposal rewrites.
- **Performance:** Bounded flat arenas and traversal; 16 definitions, 64 expression nodes and 128 initializer dependencies per rule, depth 8 and definition-chain depth 4, with existing payload/receipt caps retained.
- **Trace:** H2 prerequisite #398 (SPEC-08) · adapters-lang-ts, core-graph, spec, context-hub, app, ui · — · T-0164..0171

### US-0031 — Retain the exact source basis of agent investigations
- **Actor:** Engineer reviewing agent evidence
- **As a** engineer **I want** agent tasks and proposals to retain their actual source selections **so that** I can distinguish captured parser input, unverified evidence and later changes without altering what was reviewed.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0172** Given a supported gap task, when evidence is planned and acquired, then typed supporting facts and original citation occurrences follow deterministic candidate order under separate lookahead, attempted-read, supplied-item and captured-validation-byte limits, with explicit omissions and unread tails; unreadable legacy candidates do not consume supplied slots, and unused-tail failures cannot abort a completed prefix.
- **AC-0173** Given graph facts and current source associations, when a task is prepared, then one coherent read selects the bounded window, sorted per-source/access-kind guards are acquired outside store mutexes and one authoritative recheck rejects a changed visited basis; equal facts with different receipts remain distinct, and missing, invalid and absent bindings never collapse into one state.
- **AC-0174** Given a participating citation, when its evidence is acquired, then exact source/fact/digest/receipt/inventory/range checks return original retained strict UTF-8 bytes after checkout mutation or disappearance; corrupt, forgotten, unsupported or over-budget captured reads fail without live fallback, while valid absence remains explicitly working-tree unverified. A working-tree span longer than the per-span cap, or one that would pass the combined evidence cap, is omitted as unreadable rather than failing the whole task (#1635).
- **AC-0175** Given a prepared task and result, when staged, then immutable v2 identity binds graph and selected associations, every supplied evidence origin/text fingerprint and all candidate memberships/summary fingerprints; literal v1 bytes/IDs/reviews remain unchanged, malformed version/shape/bounds fail and raw task text remains transient under the existing replay policy.
- **AC-0176** Given preview or single/class execution, when evidence origins or receipts change, then the same prepared path changes the exact redacted payload and consent identity even for equal span text, preserving per-tier consent, broker limits and one-instance cloud grants without provider or source-write authority from retention.
- **AC-0177** Given a selected staged proposal, when current-basis assessment is requested, then separate graph, association, retained-availability and unverified dimensions report the observed state or operational failure without rewriting history, persisting a blanket fresh flag or certifying legacy references; source retention previews count actual v2 receipt references.
- **AC-0178** Given reingest, forgetting, cancellation or cleanup during a model call, when a result completes, then valid retained execution stages the original copied task basis, releases acquisition guards before the call and preserves completed results; later assessment reports changes without altering graph, provenance, exports or legacy history.
- **AC-0179** Given preview and staged-history UI, when mixed evidence, selection limits or a changed/unavailable basis is shown, then the user can distinguish each origin and assessment dimension, late responses cannot replace another proposal selection and an accepted result is never shown as fresh or as a curated item on the strength of review alone: its curated state comes from the projection (amended for #1746, ADR-0061).
- **Security:** Source acquisition is host-qualified and local; no arbitrary roots, target writes, captured-to-live fallback, extra egress, raw task archive or confidence upgrade.
- **Performance:** 64 request lookahead/attempt cap plus one slot key, 8 candidates/12 supplied spans, 8 KiB per span/48 KiB combined, 128 MiB captured validation work, 64 KiB source basis and 128 KiB staged record; graph/manifest costs remain separate.
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite #401 (SPEC-09) · agents, core-graph, app, ui · — · T-0172..0179


### US-0032 — Investigate recovered context with durable specialists
- **Actor:** Developer
- **As a** developer **I want** named specialists to query recovered context, inspect original evidence and retain cited findings **so that** I can investigate business behavior, design evidence and gaps from the app and revisit the exact basis of an answer.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0180** Given a scoped question and a selected specialist, when an investigation starts, then the host binds the exact versioned Domain analyst or Evidence auditor definition, provider, normalized scope and limit profile to a distinct investigation contract; all findings remain T3/InferredWeak and no synthetic edge, target write or confirmed fact is created. New @3 defaults use compact recovered context and explain that empty initial input requires discovery through host-executed query/read actions; original @1/@2 definitions retain their prompts and cumulative-input behavior without changing saved task meaning.
- **AC-0181** Given concurrent identical starts or a lost start acknowledgment, when the same origin/request nonce is submitted, then one transaction returns the same durable task and initial Job; changed material conflicts, execution attachment is atomic, and history survives job cleanup. Disposable regression fixtures expose only static start-stage and numeric SQLite-code diagnostics on failure, with scoped per-thread capture and unchanged production errors, timeout and retry behavior.
- **AC-0182** Given a prepared frozen graph and cumulative selected-fact ledger, when a specialist queries or acquires additional evidence, then bounded queries use the original authorized snapshot and one coherent union observation rejects graph or previously selected association changes without rebinding inputs or substituting a current revision. Page budgets include evidence inventories; a fitting ordered prefix preserves its continuation cursor, only returned facts enter the ledger, and an individually oversized fact fails without being skipped.
- **AC-0183** Given an admitted typed fact and original receipt role/index, when evidence is read, then retained strict original bytes and nested v2 occurrences remain available after checkout deletion under exact receipt validation; missing, forgotten or invalid participating evidence never falls back, legacy absence remains unverified, and repeated attempts/full-file validation consume cumulative limits.
- **AC-0184** Given a provider action, when the coordinator processes it, then only one complete strict query_context, read_evidence or finish value is admitted, tool actions are journaled before execution, invented references and raw-source replay from any supplied item fail, and invalid output causes no hidden repair or additional model call except the bounded source-replay correction below. Ordinary analyst/auditor finish refusals retain a fixed, content-free validation class in the terminal error and event; an ordinary read of an unadmitted fact has its own fixed diagnostic. For one ordinary source-replay refusal only, the rejected finish is discarded and a fixed generic correction prompt may authorize one new finish-only invocation within existing limits; the refusal and subsequent invocation are durably ordered, and cancellation, replay, metadata, prior-finding and other refusal protections remain unchanged. Legacy terminal strings remain readable and staged completion refusal/retry rules are unchanged (#937).
- **AC-0185** Given a local or cloud investigation call, when its bounded provider API executes, then request/response entity-body acquisition, generation reservations, actual stop conditions, verified TLS, no redirects/retries and the remaining task deadline are enforced before action decoding; measured usage and unknown outcomes remain distinct from reservations or estimates and legacy completion remains compatible. Bounded transport @2 requests Ollama JSON actions with optional thinking disabled, binds that version into consent and new provider records, and leaves older unknown transport identities unchanged. An inactive optional response-schema field is bound before firewall preview/consent, admitted for bounded Ollama (extended by AC-0342, AC-0567 and AC-0568 to OpenAI-compatible, hosted OpenAI and Anthropic transports), counted within existing input/request bounds and emitted as object-valued format. Absent-schema bytes remain unchanged; malformed schema text, schemas containing JSON escapes or text changed by secret redaction, and unsupported transports are refused without changing schema semantics. No investigation selects a schema (#1309). - **AC-0560** Given the desktop app, when any model call is made, then a `SENT` entry with the request is appended to the local plain-text log `logs/model-debug.log` in the app profile as the request leaves, and a matching `RECEIVED` entry with the duration and the response or error when it finishes, so an in-flight call is visible as a `SENT` without a `RECEIVED`. Bounded investigation calls record the exact wire request and response bodies, single-shot completions record the redacted payload and the completion text, and embedding calls record the input texts and a vector-count summary instead of the vector values. The `RECEIVED` entry states the outcome the caller sees, including envelope refusals after a successful HTTP response and empty completions. Request headers, including API keys, are never recorded. Recording never blocks or delays a model call. Every recorded body and queued entry is charged to one fixed 8 MiB process pool before anything is copied: a fixed per-entry charge plus at most 1 MiB of decoded text per body and a short truncation note, whatever the input bytes. An entry the pool cannot cover is dropped without copying, and the count is written with the next entry. One writer thread formats a single entry at a time into at most 4 MiB, clipping with a stated note, and decodes message and output text by streaming without building a JSON tree; its scratch is that cap plus one decoded string no longer than the bounded body. Small call metadata (endpoint or provider id, evidence span headers, error text) is held outside the pool for the duration of the call. Cancellation and the deadline are checked after the request is recorded, so a call cancelled at that moment sends nothing. The log rotates at 25 MB to a single `model-debug.previous.log`, and nothing is sent off the machine. **Help → Debug Viewer** opens the log in the plain-text editor of the OS (TextEdit on macOS, Notepad on Windows, the default handler on Linux). This is an owner request before v1.0.0 (#1624).
- **Trace:** H4 · `llm`, `app` · — · T-0560

It also points to Help → Debug Viewer for each request and reply. Cancellation and pending consent take precedence over the last event. The line is derived only from recorded events and usage, with no simulated progress or model reasoning, and disappears once the task finishes. Each activity row is labelled with its stage: Waiting for model, Model replied, Proposed by model · accepted, Running, Executed or Not executed. This makes clear whether a model-proposed action was only proposed, is running, or actually ran. This is an owner request before v1.0.0, made while watching the v0.60.0 pilot (#1631).

- **AC-0566** New semantic audit contracts are advisory: valid non-pass verdicts and typed not-completed outcomes retain the deterministically admitted candidate, without retry or extra invocation. Cancellation, task deadlines, privacy and ownership remain blocking. Historical contracts retain their blocking semantics. Saved detail and findings views label model feedback advisory, never verified. Analyst@20 requires cited support for qualifiers in findings and limitations; values are described by how cited code obtains them rather than by unestablished states. Historical terminal analyst@20 records accept only explicitly pinned previous prompt definitions with unchanged identity, operations and trust metadata; live tasks still require the current definition. Analyst@20 serializes stable instructions and disclosure before growing coverage/context/citations, with volatile fields last; this changes ordering and the role fingerprint only, preserving all JSON values, source bytes, limits and historical role wires. Analyst@20 instructs the model to paraphrase cited source rather than copy complete excerpts into authored prose. Its versioned evidence-boundary guidance requires limitations to name the searched scope and missing evidence, negative implementation assertions to have exact scoped support, and workflow answers to explain the acquired steps rather than only exception handling. An authenticated analyst@20 source-replaying finish spends the shared once-only correction allowance and receives fixed paraphrase feedback. With the allowance spent, replay is terminal with the content-free `source_replay` reason. Both paths retain measured response usage; no rejected prose or findings are persisted, and reopen preserves the result. Historical roles and privacy thresholds are unchanged. Given a new analyst@20 choice invocation, the prompt permits acquisition and qualifies terminal-wrapper syntax only when finishing. While pending obligations have offered acquisition actions, the response schema offers those actions without premature finish branches. Once every obligation has authenticated acquisition retained in the current ledger, only finish branches are offered; attempted but unavailable obligations retain exploration. Once every obligation has a completed attempt, insufficient-evidence finishing is offered; supported finishing additionally requires an acquired citation and restricts citation IDs to the acquired set. Empty-action blocked/deferred states retain the explicit historical fallback without satisfying obligations, and FinishOnly behavior remains unchanged. The same exact-role selector is used during input construction and validation, with citation-enum bytes included in existing reservation and workspace bounds. Typed-feedback reservation uses a contract-specific encoded bound covering every source action and unmet obligation, capped by the unchanged policy ceiling; nested escaping and projection scratch remain accounted. When an eligible finish cites known unacquired target IDs or receipt IDs whose matching source targets are all pending and offered, the next model-visible correction maps each mistaken target to its obligation and exact offered acquisition action, survives durable reopen, and is bound to the authenticated refusal and current choices. Query admission and exact source acquisition remain distinct; the mapping grants neither evidence nor citation authority. Historical @18/@19 prompt and record bytes remain unchanged. Foreign or mixed IDs, forged mappings, stale invocation cutoffs and duplicate publication fail closed; cancellation and the existing shared once-only correction allowance remain unchanged (#1650). Historical blocking semantic-audit contract (preserved on reopen): a structurally admitted candidate remains unpublished pending a model audit against the exact acquired spans. Every finding, overall limitation and finding-local limitation is addressed by a typed verdict bound to the candidate and ledger. Contradictions require an acquired witness; unsupported scope does not manufacture one. Honest disclosed partial answers may pass; indeterminate and rejection are terminal non-pass. Each new semantic audit reserves 16,384 output tokens from the original task budget before dispatch. The auditor retains the task request timeout. Each review emits the subject before its assessment, and each witness-bearing assessment emits the verdict before the citation ID. A separately configured reasoning auditor can enable thinking. Only answer content is admitted, and the response byte cap covers the entire body. Confirmed owner loss after audit dispatch terminates with unknown outcome, retains the single audit charge and unpublished candidate, and never replays the invocation; terminal reopen preserves that state. Audit settlement binds the exact saved invocation and candidate, stores a typed immutable receipt, and publishes only an uncancelled pass before the original deadline. Rejected, indeterminate, cancelled and late responses retain measured usage without publishing; duplicate settlement is refused and reopen rederives the decision. Provider failures and unadmitted replies terminate without a verdict receipt or result, retain the reserved audit charge and terminal clock sample, and leave unreported token totals unknown. An analyst invocation stopped without an admitted response retains all debits and terminal elapsed time, marks aggregate reported tokens unknown, and reopens terminal even after earlier measured calls. Deadline expiry between invocation commit and dispatch records a fixed timeout without dispatch. Before an analyst@20 finish can become an audit candidate, deterministic authored-field checks refuse schema placeholders, bare single-token colon labels and punctuation-only fields in titles, statements and limitations through fixed schema-v3 prose feedback. The original text is neither dropped nor echoed. This uses the existing shared once-only correction allowance; exhausted correction terminates without audit or publication. Terminal prose refusal reports incomplete or placeholder text without echoing it, rather than claiming missing source obligations. Ordinary prose mentioning code identifiers or balanced JSON remains allowed; historical roles and numeric ceilings are unchanged. A separately configured auditor uses its own local bounded provider, with no silent fallback to the analyst. Its provider profile and optional reported model digest are bound into the immutable audit invocation and the receipt's invocation hash; reopen validates this identity independently of the analyst. A configured auditor requesting the same model is refused before audit debit even on a different endpoint. When both reported model digests are available, equal digests are also refused. The analyst reported digest is retained with the auditor identity; dispatch, begin and durable replay enforce the same separation rule. A reported digest is metadata, not proof of executed weights. The single audit debit, original deadline and prepared request timeout remain unchanged; auditor transport failure and timeout retain the charge and reopen terminal. Historical same-provider audit records remain readable without granting a new dispatch. Production dispatch remains disabled. No audit repair is enabled until Phase B. A limitation directly denying an identifiable acquired path or symbol uses the shared correction allowance and names the conflicting evidence IDs. Candidate-evidence-auditor@3 retains @2 evidence scope: it supplies parent citation IDs per finding and finding limitation, labels overall scope, and judges local evidence boundaries separately from investigation-wide acquisition claims. Unproven material qualifiers receive indeterminate. The single audit invocation retains the existing bounds and typed reply format. The opt-in `domain-analyst@26` inherits @25 and additionally treats whole trimmed authored titles, statements and limitations matching `title`, `statement`, `findings`, `type` or `finish` (ASCII case-insensitive) as schema placeholders through the existing correction allowance. `Limitations`, `limitation` and sentences mentioning those words remain prose. Historical @20–@25 admission and saved-result reopen bytes remain unchanged, including an @25 limitation equal to `statement`; adopting @26 into frozen acceptance or a release requires a separate decision.
- **Security:** Refused finding prose is not retained as correction feedback. Typed mapping is validated against the saved contract and choices; source-target IDs never become admitted citation handles.
- **Performance:** Feedback and every escaped enclosing payload must fit existing policy bounds; no budget or numeric limit is raised. Failure to prove capacity refuses publication.
- **Trace:** H4 · `agents`, `app` · — · T-0566

- **AC-0567** Given a hosted OpenAI investigation call, when `OpenAiProvider` runs on the bounded path, then it is a declared Cloud provider. The egress firewall requires consent bound to the exact payload, and release builds can reach only the pinned `https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions` endpoint.
  - **Request:** the consented system and user messages, `max_completion_tokens` from the reserved output limit, `store: false` and `stream: false`. The API key travels as a sensitive bearer header and is never logged. No tools or thinking template are sent.
  - **Schema:** a bounded response schema travels as `json_schema` with `strict: true`. It uses the AC-0568 projection plus these checks:
    - every object is closed and lists every property as required;
    - every `const` and `enum` schema carries its explicit scalar `type`, and a lone object or array `const` travels as a closed, fully required schema of typed literals with the array's exact length (strict mode cannot pin array positions, so each item admits any listed element). A structured literal with sibling keywords is refused. The live API rejects untyped and structured literals with HTTP 400, as #598 development showed;
    - `allOf`, `$ref` siblings and non-root definitions are refused;
    - the published limits (10 nesting levels, 5000 properties, 1000 enum values, 120000 schema string bytes) are enforced in one memoized pass before send, and each definition is counted once.
  - **Root wrapper:** a root that is not a plain object travels wrapped as the single required property `response`, with definitions kept at the root. Only the model's exact inner bytes reach host validation against the complete original schema. A wrapped reply in any other shape is Invalid.
  - **Responses:** envelope admission, refusal, stop and usage handling follow AC-0342 under the AC-0185 transport rules.
  - **Refusals:** a schema that strict mode cannot represent is refused as Unsupported before connecting. The unbounded completion path is unsupported.
  - **Model:** the model id is the caller's reviewed choice and is bound into provider identity (#1709).
- **Trace:** H4 · `llm` · — · T-0567
- **AC-0568** Given a hosted Anthropic investigation call with a bounded response schema, when the Messages request is built, then the schema travels as `output_config.format` (`json_schema`) in a wire-only projection. The host still validates the reply against the complete original schema.
  - **Dropped from the wire copy only:** constraints the API rejects, namely string and numeric bounds, `multipleOf`, `maxItems`, `uniqueItems`, and `minItems` above 1.
  - **Rewritten:** `oneOf` becomes `anyOf`, and structured enum members become exact branches. The API compiles only scalar `const`, so a structured literal is sent as the closed object schema that admits exactly it: `additionalProperties: false`, every key required, and scalar leaves as `const`. An array cannot be length-bounded on the wire, so an empty one admits only `null` items and a non-empty one admits items drawn from its own elements; the host enforces the exact array. Same-shaped structured choices in one `anyOf` merge into a single closed schema whose varying scalar leaves become an `enum`, which keeps large menus within the API grammar limit. Any combination this admits beyond the original choices is rejected by host validation. An array schema with `maxItems: 0` travels with `null` items, which keeps its unreachable item grammar out of the compile; the complete schema is projected and reference-checked first, so this never hides an unsupported or missing reference, and the OpenAI strict projection keeps such items unchanged.
  - **Constrained:** `additionalProperties` may only be `false`. Every `$ref` must name an existing root `$defs` or `definitions` entry, and reference cycles are refused.
  - **Inlined:** the API rejects `$defs` alongside `anyOf`, so the wire copy carries no definitions. Each local reference is replaced by its projected definition, and unreferenced definitions are not sent. The expansion is bounded by a fixed node budget. The OpenAI strict projection keeps root definitions and references.
  - **Refused as Unsupported before sending:** any other keyword, an external, missing or recursive reference, and a conflicting merge. Also refused: a `$ref` with sibling keywords, definitions below the root, and an inlined copy that exceeds the node budget.
  - **Unchanged:** absent-schema request bytes.
  - **Model pin:** the Opus lane is pinned to `claude-opus-5-5` (#1709).
- **Trace:** H4 · `llm` · — · T-0568
- **AC-0569** Given a frozen captured-analyst definition whose provider is a hosted identity pinned to a production endpoint (`anthropic:` Haiku/Opus lane at the Messages API, or `openai:<model>` at Chat Completions), when the owner runs the manual acceptance harness, then execution requires `CARTOGRAPH_ACCEPTANCE_CLOUD_AUTHORIZE` equal to that exact provider id and `CARTOGRAPH_ACCEPTANCE_CLOUD_STEP_CAP` from 1 to 64. Both are checked before the one-attempt marker is consumed.
  - **Provider:** built from the owner-run process environment key (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY`), which is never logged. It must reproduce the frozen descriptor exactly; the staged request records Cloud mode.
  - **Consent grant:** T3 cloud consent is granted through the settings API only when T3 is currently local.
  - **Approvals:** each pending step is approved through the app's own consent path, with its exact step, revision and payload-hash check. Past the cap the next step is declined, which cancels the run.
  - **Consent log:** an exclusive append-only `analyst-consent-log.jsonl` durably records the grant, each approval (step, revision, payload hash, payload bytes, redaction count, provider id), the cap decline and the revert, each before it takes effect.
  - **Revert:** on completion, error or a partial grant, T3 reverts to local, which revokes the consent, and its prior enabled flag is restored.
  - **Entry dispatch:** the manual analyst entry reads the local-only override variables (`CARTOGRAPH_ACCEPTANCE_PROTOCOL`, `_THINKING`, `_URL`, `_MODEL`) only for a Local definition. A hosted definition goes straight to this gate without them.
  - **Frozen run limits:** an optional captured-definition pair of active seconds and request timeout uses the existing configured desktop start route. Reopen must match both saved values; omission preserves historical definition and manifest bytes. Preparation and execute=false reopen remain provider-free. Product defaults are unchanged.
  - **Unchanged:** Local definitions and their behavior. Unpinned, unavailable or mismatched hosted identities are refused (#1709, owner decision 2026-11-07).

- **AC-0570** Given a desktop next-prompt investigation on the expanded profile whose configured provider is the pinned hosted Opus lane (Cloud `anthropic:claude-opus-5-5`), when the task starts, then the owner-approved output allowance is frozen into its effective policy: 16,384 generated tokens per invocation and a total reservation of 64 × 16,384, because Opus 5.5 always runs adaptive thinking whose tokens count toward `max_tokens` (#1709).
  - **Scope:** every other provider keeps the static 2,048-token allowance unless the separate explicit local policy in AC-0571 applies; MCP-origin tasks and the v1 profile stay static. Every other limit is unchanged.
  - **Recovery:** a saved desktop task recovers the allowance only as these exact values, composed with any saved time override, and only when the saved task is a next-prompt task whose saved provider is Cloud `anthropic:claude-opus-5-5`; the exact values on any other saved task, and any other generation value, are refused, and MCP recovery stays static.
  - **Source admission and recovery:** source-read reservations and settlement use the authenticated frozen allowance, including calls beyond the static generation budget. Historical reservation bytes remain readable; altered or mismatched saved allowances are rejected.
  - **Transport:** the bounded call admits exactly 16,384 output tokens only through the investigation constructor and the saved step-consent record's decoder; ordinary limit decoding, the general completion ceiling, the response and output-text byte caps and the request timeout are unchanged.
