- **AC-0506** Given an ingest that has committed the deterministic base, when the knowledge phase runs in the background afterwards, then a single worker (later ingests coalesced into one follow-up pass) embeds that base's units through the local provider only while T2 is enabled and the pinned embedding model is installed, and republishes the knowledge overlay for the same base revision with a recipe recording whether the provider phase ran and its embedding namespace; T2 switched off (AC-0588), no installed provider, a failing provider and a base past the bounded snapshot read are each a named omission that never fails the ingest; an overlay is never published for a base that moved during the phase; and the base graph is unchanged (#1441).
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445) · `semantic` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0499
- **AC-0501** Given a base graph snapshot and a query, when hybrid retrieval runs, then nodes whose id, name, path or `METHOD path` equals the query come first as anchors, followed by their one-hop neighbours, and only then knowledge units ranked by SQLite FTS5 and, when unit vectors and a local provider are present, fused by reciprocal rank with vector similarity (the query is embedded only by the model that embedded the units; any other model is refused); ties break by node id, before any per-leg row limit, so pinned vectors give identical results; with no provider the lexical leg alone answers; only a local provider receives unit or query text; graph hits that fill the limit return without ranking or a provider call; every hit cites its base node (and anchoring edge) and carries the T2 tier; nothing is written to the base (#1442).
- **Security:** Indexes only the redacted unit text; the query is redacted before it reaches the provider; query terms are quoted, never FTS5 syntax.
- **Performance:** In-memory index over at most the 16,384 knowledge units; queries above 512 bytes are refused; at most 200 hits and 200 candidates per ranked leg.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445) · `semantic` · ADR-0051 · T-0501
- **AC-0502** Given provenance on any fact, when an evidence basis is recorded, then it is a field separate from tier and confidence: `source`, `observation` or `artifact`, where artifact evidence cites the artifact's `sha256:` content digest, a reader-named kind and a byte offset or relative member path, and malformed artifact evidence fails provenance validation; facts without a basis keep their exact wire form and read as `source`, or `observation` for T1; recording a basis never changes tier, confidence or content hash, so the confidence ceiling and verified-only exports keep their meaning; no artifact reader ships (#1444).
- **Security:** Member paths are relative with no empty, `.` or `..` segments, backslash, NUL or colon in the first segment (drive or scheme), at most 4,096 bytes.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445) · `core-prov` · ADR-0051 · T-0502
- **AC-0505** Given a current knowledge overlay, when a caller requests a bounded Capability selection, then the read validates every row and the unchanged full-overlay identity while retaining only Capability nodes within the caller's selected-count and stored-JSON-byte bounds; overflow returns a typed limit error without partial facts and still detects corrupt unselected rows. Row-size admission precedes decode and identity verification streams the existing canonical encoding. Absent/Stale semantics and base data remain unchanged. These logical selection bounds do not claim decoded heap or process RSS bounds; host session admission and artifact wiring remain separate (#1456).
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445, #1456) · `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0498, T-0505
- **AC-0533** Given a current knowledge overlay, when a caller requests the combined Capability and Actor read with count and byte caps, then Capability and Actor nodes are retained in key order under one combined count and stored-byte charge, and past either cap the read returns the selection limit, never a partial set. Every row is identity- and shape-checked as for the AC-0505 Capability read, so corruption in any row takes precedence over a selection overflow; the Capability read is unchanged, both reads share the same logical workspace bound, and absent or stale overlays read as `read` does (#1534).
- **Security:** Verification is the AC-0505 streaming check over every row; widening the selected labels does not skip or relax it.
- **Performance:** One scan; the selection charge and workspace bound are the caller's existing caps. No new numeric limit.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1534) · `core-graph` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0533
- **AC-0509** Given bounded Capability selection, a storage-owned checked helper accounts retained decoded facts and citations, returned metadata, metadata construction and fixed single-row scratch using the reader's existing limits. Zero selection retains row-verification scratch; stale revision strings use the stored basis cap. Arithmetic overflow refuses a bound. Base/coverage, host copies, compiler/output and RSS remain outside this logical currency (#1469).
- **Security:** Pure accounting helper; no provider, database write or admission-policy change.
- **Performance:** Uses fixed-size templates and checked arithmetic without scanning facts.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445, #1456, #1469) · `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0498, T-0505, T-0509
- **AC-0514** Given the knowledge phase's bounded base snapshot, when the phase publishes the overlay, then every deterministic knowledge producer that has landed (the AC-0500 GraphQL Capability rule, then the AC-0516 BusinessFlow producer traced within the overlay's own fact and byte admission caps) derives its facts from that same snapshot outside the graph lock and they are published with the phase, with or without a provider; a producer that fails as a whole is reported by its named omission while the others still publish; the overlay recipe names each producer rule and version so a rule change changes identity; the report counts derived facts and per-fact oversized omissions; a derived set the overlay refuses as a whole is published as the phase alone under the named omission `knowledge-derived-rejected`, marked in the recipe, so producer output never fails the phase or the ingest; a base that moved is still never published over and the base is unchanged (#1443).
- **Security:** Pure deterministic producers over the local snapshot; no provider sees producer input or output.
- **Performance:** Producers run once per phase over the already bounded snapshot; BusinessFlow tracing takes flow, cumulative-hop and serialized-flow-byte limits numerically aligned with the overlay's 200,000 and 256 MiB caps; these are independent trace budgets, not overlay fact or byte accounting, so overlay admission still applies to the whole derived set, with exact rejection rather than truncation.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1445) · `app`, `spec` · ADR-0051 · T-0514

- **AC-0500** Given existing import-proven NestJS GraphQL Endpoint, HANDLES and Method facts, when the Capability rule groups operations by exact API and resolver ownership, then it emits Semantic/InferredWeak candidates with a versioned Rule method and every supporting base citation, leaves the input snapshot unchanged, and produces stable identities and ordering under input permutations; missing, ambiguous, Gap or foreign ownership yields no candidate; groups exceeding the existing overlay per-fact JSON limit are omitted with an oversized-group count while ordinary groups survive, including exact-boundary and escaping cases (#1451).
- **Security:** Derived candidates preserve cited deterministic support and do not modify the base or call a provider.
- **Performance:** Existing per-fact JSON limits reject oversized groups; aggregate publication admission remains separate.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445, #1451) · `spec` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0500

- **AC-0519** Given otherwise-valid deterministic Endpoint, HANDLES or Method support whose provenance names an explicit artifact or observation evidence basis, when the Capability rule checks support, then that support is refused and no candidate forms, because the derived candidate's own provenance resolves to a source basis; support with no basis or an explicit source basis is unchanged, and the rule version moves to 2 so the changed eligibility changes candidate identity, Rule provenance and the knowledge recipe (#1489).
- **Security:** Derived facts can no longer relabel artifact or runtime evidence as source evidence; the existing source-backed contract is enforced, not widened.
- **Performance:** One allocation-free field match per support; no new limits.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445, #1489) · `spec` · ADR-0051 · T-0519

- **AC-0517** GraphQL Endpoint permission observations require exactly one stable import-bound Allow site whose nonempty ordered arguments are direct members of one stable named Permission import. Endpoint.permission_site records import-bound syntax, the actual exported/local/module bindings, original member arguments and its own Confirmed deterministic provenance citing the exact Allow decorator, import specifiers and module strings. Query/Mutation Endpoint and HANDLES provenance remain unchanged. Legacy permissions is retained only for these proven observations; unbound lookalikes, type-only/default/namespace or ambiguous/reassigned/shadowed bindings, mixed/computed arguments and multiple Allow sites produce neither permission field, preserving no partial list.
- **Security:** This establishes source annotation and binding syntax only, without evaluating imported implementations, runtime OR/AND semantics, effective permission checks, authentication, roles or principals. Missing annotation never means anonymous; Owner remains a token.
- **Performance:** Uses existing lexical bindings and source spans with no dependency reads, providers, new numeric limits or global binder changes.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1451) · `adapters-lang-ts` · ADR-0051 · T-0517
- **AC-0507** Given bounded graph and coverage reads, the caller can capture their store revision, nodes, edges and coverage inventories in one SQLite read transaction. Concurrent publications cannot mix revisions, including coverage-only changes. Existing graph and coverage limits and integrity checks apply without partial output; invalid generation metadata fails closed. Legacy read APIs keep their behavior. The returned revision describes the captured observation, not continued currency (#1461).
- **Security:** The revisioned covered read validates stored metadata and inventories; it performs no provider call or write.
- **Performance:** Existing caller-supplied graph and coverage admission limits apply before rows are materialized.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445, #1461) · `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0498, T-0507
- **AC-0504** Given a base snapshot and borrowed derived Capability candidates with their complete `BaseRef` sets, when the compiler produces the official `user-stories` artifact, then each valid candidate is a unique, non-shadowing Semantic/InferredWeak Capability with Rule provenance, and every cited base node/edge resolves uniquely with valid provenance; malformed candidates or support, invalid support provenance, missing/ambiguous references, duplicate candidates and shadowing return a named input error without an artifact. Each included derived story retains its candidate provenance, every exact support reference and an association to that base fact with its original provenance; a candidate hidden by its own provenance, any Gap support, or support hidden by export mode or rejection suppresses the whole story and contributes once to a content-free omission count (#1540). Hostile candidate/support text renders inertly, and complete content, associations and assertions share the output-byte budget. Empty candidate input preserves the legacy artifact bytes and identity. Overlay currency and input admission remain caller responsibilities; this compiler adds no producer or overlay mutation. The explicit-security entrypoint preserves caller security-projection row/byte admission and returns a closed derivation limit without an artifact on refusal; the legacy API remains compatible (#1455, #1495).
- **Security:** Candidate and support text is rendered as inert serialized data; the compiler makes no provider calls or graph/overlay writes.
- **Performance:** The complete artifact content and assertions share the selected compiler output-byte limit; candidate selection, count/byte admission, and overlay currency remain caller responsibilities.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445, #1455, #1495, #1540) · `spec` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0504
- **AC-0510** Complete MCP read sessions capture one owned graph snapshot, coverage inventory and store revision from a coherent read, reject observed revision drift before session publication, and include the captured revision in their immutable basis. Repeated same-content publications advance the basis without changing recovered content identities; existing selected artifact bytes remain unchanged (#1471).
- **Security:** Recheck after registration and rejection observations; no provider call, overlay capture or write is introduced.
- **Performance:** Existing limits remain unchanged; checked reservation includes revision owners, token reread, protocol metadata and complete basis-digest workspace.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445, #1471) · `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0510

- **AC-0516** Given a base revision and caller-supplied existing trace limits, the pure BusinessFlow producer recomputes bounded traces for all current tracer triggers, including server Endpoints, and emits one node-only Semantic/InferredWeak rule candidate per nonempty supported flow. Each cites the exact trigger, stored hop edges and participant nodes used for display or Gap reasons. Missing, ambiguous or invalid support omits the whole candidate explicitly; trace or cumulative projection admission failure returns a producer-local typed omission with a stable source-free code and no partial report, so other producers may still publish. Ordered hops, trace status, score, depth limitation and Gap details are preserved, and content-bound versioned identity is deterministic. Empty and over-per-fact-cap flows are counted omissions; aggregate publication remains separate.
- **Security:** Existing provenance ceilings and valid source evidence are required for every cited fact; no base mutation, new edge vocabulary, provider execution, ingestion or automatic publication.
- **Performance:** A single trace call applies existing flow, hop and byte limits cumulatively across all triggers. The supplied trace byte limit is also a separate nonrefundable projection allowance charged for borrowed support-provenance JSON before decode and final candidate JSON before retention, including invalid and oversized work; this conservative currency differs from final output size. Existing per-fact cap remains separate. Callers admit input, indexes and typed workspace; tracer scans can still repeat across triggers, worst-case O(F(N+E)) traversal plus index sorting for F admitted triggers, N nodes and E edges. This is not a wall-time or RSS guarantee.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443) · `spec`, `flowtracer`, `core-graph` · ADR-0051 · T-0516
- **AC-0508** Given admitted base graph and selected OverlayFact byte/count bounds, when the Capability compiler workspace is calculated, then checked named components cover lookup/visibility/candidate indexes, Story/support vectors, current typed provenance, and derived rendering scratch; validation owners remain charged during shared compilation, existing security/output accounting is composed once, and arithmetic overflow refuses admission. Real shared-support compilation and security projection fixtures check concrete owners. Retained base/selection, caller candidate vectors and storage/session scratch remain explicit host responsibilities (#1466).
- **Security:** No execution, provenance change or admission bypass; this helper does not establish host admission.
- **Performance:** Logical ownership currency only, excluding allocator capacity and RSS; no numeric policy changes.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445, #1451, #1455, #1466) · `spec` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0498, T-0500, T-0504, T-0508
- **AC-0512** Complete read sessions capture an immutable Capability selection and Absent, Stale or verified Current overlay identity against their coherent base revision. Stale or absent overlays supply no selected facts; malformed storage fails closed, and corruption remains a storage failure even after selection overflow. Existing sessions retain their captured facts and basis across republish; base queries and official artifact compilation retain their existing behavior in this preparatory slice.
- **Security:** Existing storage identity, citation and provenance checks apply before capture; stale revision metadata must have a valid graph-instance shape and a generation in the nonnegative SQLite i64 domain. Graph and overlay locks are released before acquisition rechecks. No provider execution or base write.
- **Performance:** Existing limits remain unchanged. Checked logical reservations compose storage row/basis scratch, selected JSON and typed containers, retained metadata, protocol and digest owners; these bounds do not guarantee RSS.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1494) · `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0512
- **AC-0513** Complete-session official user-stories compilation consumes the session's captured Capability facts and exact base citations, preserving the candidate and supporting assertions' original provenance. Verified-only export excludes weak candidates; rejecting a candidate or filtering any support suppresses the whole derived story, with an explicit support-filter omission. Absent or stale selection preserves base-only artifact content; other artifacts and base queries retain their behavior. Produced text pages bind the immutable session basis and artifact content identity, and old handles remain unchanged after overlay republish. Invalid candidates fail closed; already-admitted traced flows participate in shared ADR derivation, preserving the legacy ADR output-byte refusal. Original trace and compiler limits return no partial artifact.
- **Security:** Only the official user-stories route consumes the selection; derived prose remains literal JSON text, and provenance is never promoted. Caller security row and byte limits retain the existing typed refusal with no partial artifact. This read-side acceptance does not establish provider execution or automatic-ingest acceptance; no base write occurs.
- **Performance:** Existing limits remain unchanged. Checked logical reservation composes the compiler-owned validation/render workspace and host-owned borrowed candidate vector with the retained session, without duplicating the base or final output; output and page admission retain exact boundaries, without an RSS guarantee.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1497) · `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0513
- **AC-0511** Given a deterministic `DataEntity` emitted by a supported ORM extractor, when the ORM domain-entity rule derives overlay facts, then it emits a Semantic/InferredWeak `DomainEntity` candidate and a `MAPS_TO` edge only when the exact supported extractor provenance, nonempty evidence, supported storage kind, matching explicit/default table and store, and nonempty mapped fields are structurally valid; the candidate identity is derived from the backing DataEntity ID, and its source name is display text only. Both facts cite that exact DataEntity, retain its evidence, carry versioned Rule provenance, and remain outside the base graph. Duplicate IDs, ambiguous or malformed ORM facts, unsupported storage, missing or invalid table/store/field metadata, and a pair exceeding the overlay fact-size limit produce no pair; oversized pairs are counted. The JPA path is exercised through the real Java extractor; the SQLAlchemy test uses extractor-shaped Python facts and does not claim an end-to-end Python extractor or Vendure result (#1443).
- **Security:** No provider call or base-graph write; reads only the supplied snapshot. ORM support requires an implicit or explicit source evidence basis; explicit Artifact or Observation support is refused rather than relabelled as source evidence.
- **Performance:** Indexes base-node IDs, then visits unique nodes; each retained fact must fit the existing overlay per-fact byte cap. Aggregate publication admission remains separate.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443) · `spec` (ORM DomainEntity rule and Java extractor fixture) · ADR-0051 · T-0511
- **AC-0520** Given the knowledge phase's bounded base snapshot, when the phase publishes the overlay, then the AC-0511 ORM DomainEntity rule runs after the Capability and BusinessFlow producers over that same snapshot, and its weak DomainEntity candidates and `MAPS_TO` mappings are published with the phase, with or without a provider. Its per-fact oversized omissions count in the report; the recipe names the rule and version within the name bound; a rerun over the same base republishes the identical set; the base is unchanged (#1443, #1507).
- **Security:** The rule is pure and deterministic over the local snapshot and makes no provider call. Its derived overlay facts are not embedded, because the provider phase embeds only base knowledge units, and those already include the base `DataEntity` it reads. Provenance stays weak as the producer emits it.
- **Performance:** One pass over the already bounded snapshot; the combined derived set is still admitted, or refused whole, by the existing overlay caps under `knowledge-derived-rejected`. No new numeric limit.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443) · `app` · ADR-0051 · T-0520
- **AC-0521** Given admitted graph rows and JSON values, when the official selected compiler's logical workspace is bounded, then security findings count as typed generated rows with a proven value bound: one fixed per-finding schema, measured through the real projection, plus the admitted source values. This replaces one value per serialized output byte. Every action, scope and evidence entry costs exactly as many output values as its smallest accepted source form, so concentrated single-row, escaped and many-minimal inputs stay within the bound. Byte charges, escaping and the remaining compiler roles are unchanged (#1509).
- **Security:** Accounting only. Projection output, identity and the configured policy limits are unchanged. The derived reservation floor is intentionally lower, so a reservation offer that was refused before can now be admitted.
- **Performance:** At 4 MiB / 5,000 rows / 100k values the selected-compiler logical peak falls from about 18.98 GB to about 9.51 GB, and the Capability compiler peak from about 20.04 GB to about 10.57 GB. These are logical accounting currency, not RSS. Policy limits are unchanged.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1509) · `spec` · ADR-0037 · T-0521
- **AC-0522** Given admitted graph bytes, rows and JSON values, when the official selected compiler's logical workspace is bounded, then typed `from_value` decodes of provenance, omissions and source-rule payloads are charged by an explicit ownership recurrence instead of one value role per input byte. Each string payload is credited to the one JSON string token it comes from, and each collection slot to the one array item it holds. Enums and nested structs stay inside the parent slot that is already charged. Fixed fallback strings and omission classification (its token slots, lowercase expansion and prefix) carry their own explicit charges, including cached and per-candidate omission `Arc` counters. For provenance and omissions, a changed field, variant or field type fails compilation next to the element lists. The source-rule closure (GuardedExitEvidence and its nested types) remains source-audited against the reviewed core-graph element list. Serde attribute changes are a manual-review residual in both (#1509).
- **Security:** Accounting only. Decoding, classification, projection output and the configured policy limits are unchanged. The derived reservation floor is intentionally lower, so a reservation offer that was refused before can now be admitted.
- **Performance:** At 4 MiB / 5,000 rows / 100k values the selected-compiler logical peak falls from about 9.51 GB to about 3.95 GB: source rules about 5.77 → 0.72 GB, registers about 5.00 → 2.31 GB. These are logical accounting currency, not RSS. Policy limits are unchanged.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1509) · `spec`, `core-graph` · ADR-0037 · T-0522
- **AC-0523** Given admitted graph bytes, when the official selected compiler's registers workspace is bounded, then the `supports_listed` token scratch and its lookup clone are charged by the most subject-id parts (five), not one fragment slot per input byte. The token is cleared at every space, so between clears it keeps at most the last fragment of each part. Node, edge and hop subjects are built only through `node_subject` and `link_subject`, whose fixed array shares that constant. The listed-subject probe keeps the historical flattened space split, including empty, leading, trailing, consecutive-space and Unicode names (#1509).
- **Security:** Accounting only. Subject ids, listed-subject matching and the configured policy limits are unchanged. Direct construction of a subject id outside the two helpers still needs reviewer scrutiny. The derived reservation floor is intentionally lower.
- **Performance:** At 4 MiB / 5,000 rows / 100k values the registers workspace falls from about 2.31 GB to about 0.63 GB, and the selected-compiler logical peak from about 3.95 GB to about 2.36 GB. These are logical accounting currency, not RSS. Policy limits are unchanged.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1509) · `spec` · ADR-0037 · T-0523
- **AC-0524** Given ordinary (unbounded) spec compilation, when the bundle builds the US-TM, gap register and rule-evidence artifacts, then each Markdown artifact and its JSON index come from one producer pass with both outputs selected, and every one of the six artifacts is byte-identical to its single-artifact selected compilation, including grouped rule Markdown past the flat limit. On the REA graph (310k nodes) this halves the gap-register pair and cuts the rule-evidence pair by about 40% in a release build (#1478).
- **Security:** Output-equivalent. Artifact bytes, assertions and the selected/bounded path are unchanged; the index artifact keeps no assertions, as single-index production already did.
- **Performance:** On the REA graph (310k nodes, release) the gap-register pair falls from about 15 s to 7 s and the rule-evidence pair from about 7 s to 4 s; whole export about 20 s.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1478) · `spec` · ADR-0037 · T-0524
- **AC-0525** Given recovered Capability facts, when the base user-stories artifact is rendered, then each story's name and id and every inline-provenance cell (summary, method, evidence, extractor, content hash) are written as literal Markdown text: HTML is entity-escaped, Markdown punctuation, backticks and GFM strikethrough `~` are backslash-escaped, and newlines are shown as ` ⏎ `, so fences, headings, code spans, emphasis, strikethrough and HTML stay inert. Bare-URL autolinks are not suppressed. The built-in artifact viewer decodes every CommonMark backslash escape and these three entities, so it shows the exact recovered text. Structured assertions keep the exact stored text and provenance, output-byte admission counts the escaped bytes at the exact boundary and one byte short, and this intentionally changes base user-stories bytes and content ids (#1453).
- **Security:** Presentation only, not a redaction guarantee; stored text is shown, not removed. Derived-story sidecar text stays inert JSON as before.
- **Performance:** Per-character escaping on story text only.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1453) · `spec`, `work-packets` fixtures · ADR-0037 · T-0525

- **AC-0515** Given a unique GraphQL Endpoint with structurally valid AC-0517 import-bound permission syntax, matching legacy permissions and consistent deterministic source provenance, the pure Actor producer emits one endpoint-scoped Semantic/InferredWeak caller-set candidate. It preserves the exact ordered permission alternatives, including duplicates, under any_of and records the named versioned assumption “Allow(...) admits callers holding any listed permission”. Identity binds the rule, assumption, endpoint and exact site; its evidence is the original site evidence and its BaseRef cites the Endpoint. Absent, Public, malformed, mismatched, ambiguous or unproved observations abstain; oversized candidates are counted under the existing overlay per-fact cap.
- **Security:** The exported Allow name selects an explicit weak convention, without repository/path/commit/module whitelists or effective runtime authorization, authenticated user, role, principal or anonymous claims; Confirmed syntax is never promoted. Owner remains a token. Compiler-visible assumption rendering is required in a separate slice before user-facing adoption.
- **Performance:** Deterministic node-key lookup and existing per-fact JSON admission only; callers admit the input and aggregate publication separately. No providers, ingestion, new numeric policy or RSS guarantee.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443) · `spec`, `core-graph` · ADR-0051 · T-0515

- **AC-0518** Given valid Capability and canonical Actor sidecars, the combined official user-stories compiler renders one artifact preserving Capability-only bytes when Actor input is empty. Each Actor shows exact ordered alternatives, the complete named/versioned weak assumption, Endpoint citation and site evidence together. Full canonical producer validation rejects malformed, duplicate, shadowed or missing/ambiguous support atomically; each valid Capability or Actor filtered by its own provenance or supporting facts is counted once, with a content-free omission explanation rather than a claim that nothing was recovered (#1540). Hostile data stays escaped and content plus assertions share existing output admission. Validation returns borrowed stories before rendering, dropping lookup/projection indexes while retaining every support vector; the combined logical peak composes these disjoint stages without changing the existing input/output limits (#1555). Renderer reference slots remain additive while current per-item Capability/Actor scratch is composed by maximum across the sequential assertion loops; legacy public bounds retain their existing totals (#1557). The combined fixed user-stories route retains common projection, ADR/security, flow, output and base-row roles while reserving only its selected renderer indexes; generic compiler bounds remain unchanged.
- **Security:** Assumed caller sets are Semantic/InferredWeak conventions, not identified users, roles, principals or effective runtime authorization. No producer semantics, providers, host hookup or graph writes change.
- **Performance:** Checked Actor-only workspace accounts canonical verification and retained/render roles separately; callers compose it with Capability/compiler admission and retain responsibility for input and currency. No numeric policy or RSS guarantee.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1488, #1540, #1555, #1557) · `spec` · ADR-0051 · T-0518

- **AC-0532** Given the knowledge phase's bounded base snapshot, when the phase publishes the overlay, then the AC-0515 permission Actor rule runs after the Capability, BusinessFlow and DomainEntity producers over that same snapshot, and its weak caller-set candidates, each citing exactly its base `Endpoint`, are published with the phase, with or without a provider. Its per-fact oversized omissions count in the report; the recipe names the rule and version within the name bound; if the overlay refuses the combined set, none of the derived facts are published and the recipe says `knowledge-derived-rejected`; a rerun over the same base republishes the identical set; the base is unchanged (#1530). A test-only published-category census counts Capability, BusinessFlow, DomainEntity and Actor nodes after successful full-set publication, excludes edges, and reports zero when derived publication is refused.
- **Security:** The rule is pure and deterministic over the local snapshot and makes no provider call; the candidates keep their weak provenance and named assumption, which do not attest effective runtime authorization. Derived facts are not embedded.
- **Performance:** One node-key pass over the already bounded snapshot; the combined derived set is still admitted, or refused whole, by the existing overlay caps. No new numeric limit.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1530) · `app` · ADR-0051 · T-0532

- **AC-0534** Given canonical Actor and Capability sidecars plus caller-admitted flows and explicit security limits, the spec-only combined user-stories entrypoint uses the existing shared dispatcher: empty Actor input preserves the Capability flow/security artifact bytes and identity, combined output retains flow-sensitive ADR admission, restrictive security admission returns Limited without an artifact, and an invalid Actor refuses the whole compilation.
- **Security:** Caller input, currency and compiler/Actor workspace admission remain required; no host route, provider, overlay selection or graph write is connected by this slice.
- **Performance:** Thin wrapper over existing validation and rendering; no budget, numeric policy or workspace formula changes.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1532) · `spec` · ADR-0051 · T-0534

- **AC-0535** Given a current knowledge overlay holding canonical Actor and Capability facts, when an MCP read session captures the overlay and compiles the official `user-stories` artifact, then the session reads Capabilities and Actors together through the bounded combined overlay read under one shared count and byte charge, and compiles them through the admitted combined compiler with the session's flows and security limits. Each Actor story keeps its ordered assumption alternatives and Endpoint citation. Under VerifiedOnly the weak Actor is omitted and counted, never claimed absent. A canonically invalid captured Actor returns a storage error without an artifact. The capture's byte bound admits exactly the serialized base graph, and the logical Actor workspace is added to the session's capability compile reservation (#1539, #1443).
- **Security:** Assumed caller sets stay Semantic/InferredWeak conventions; no provider call, graph write or new route is added.
- **Performance:** The existing capture and compile reservations are extended by the retained Actor slots and the logical Actor workspace bound; no new numeric policy or RSS guarantee.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1539) · `app`, `core-graph`, `spec` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0535
- **AC-0583** Given a recovered base larger than the interactive read default (50,000 rows or 32 MiB), when the knowledge phase runs after ingest, then it reads the base under the desktop investigation ceiling (10,000,000 rows, 4 GiB) and publishes its overlay, so the deterministic knowledge nodes exist for a repository of real size; a base past that ceiling is still the named `knowledge-base-bounds` omission. Reads that reserve memory in advance (MCP sessions and MCP-started tasks) keep the interactive default (ADR-0052 amendment, #1771, #1602).
- **Security:** Local read only; no provider call is added and the overlay caps are unchanged.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1771) · `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0052 · T-0583
- **AC-0588** Given the knowledge phase has run after an ingest, when it skipped or left out work, then the app shows a notice naming each reason with its code: the base past the read ceiling (`knowledge-base-bounds`), T2 off (`semantic-tier-disabled`), no installed or a failing embedding provider, derived nodes refused as a set, a producer that yielded nothing, units past the embedding cap and oversized candidates; a phase that failed outright shows its error; the outcome of the last phase is kept so a window opened afterwards still shows it; and a phase that did everything shows nothing. With T2 off the phase still runs: no provider is probed and nothing is embedded, and the deterministic producers (Capability, BusinessFlow, DomainEntity, Actor) publish as before. A desktop context query on a base larger than the interactive read default (50,000 rows or 32 MiB) reads under the desktop investigation ceiling and answers; a base past that ceiling returns the bounds error. MCP reads keep the interactive default (ADR-0052 amendment, #1782).
- **Security:** Local reads only. With T2 off no provider is contacted; the notice carries reason codes and counts, never source text.
- **Performance:** A desktop context query copies the whole base per page, so its cost grows with the graph; the knowledge phase now runs after every ingest, on the existing single coalesced worker.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1782) · `app`, `ui` · ADR-0051, ADR-0052 · T-0588

### US-0047 — Have an agent draft a section of a catalogue document
- **Actor:** Engineer or product owner
- **As a** reader of the recovered documentation **I want** an agent role to draft one section of a catalogue document from cited facts, for me to accept, reject or return **so that** the documents hold written content where no rule reaches, without any uncited or unreviewed statement appearing in them.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Draft
- **AC-0663** Given document catalogue version 1.0 and an ingested system, when the export is compiled, then `product-specification.md` (D1) is emitted with every catalogue section in order, each P section and the frame of each P+A section taken from the existing projection or naming the exported artifact that holds it, each A section and each P+A section without an eligible draft marked as not drafted, each H section a labelled blank, and a header carrying the catalogue version, graph content hash, curated revision and export mode; a section not in the section table cannot be addressed, and every existing artifact is byte-identical except the fact index, which names the document among the artifacts citing each fact its frames print.
- **AC-0664** Given a drafting run with its admitted citation set, when the role finishes, then a draft is accepted only if it targets the requested address, holds 1 to 64 claims of at most 2,048 bytes each with 1 to 16 citation ids, and every cited id is in the admitted set; a claim with no citation, an id outside the set, an exceeded bound or a statement failing the existing replay and privacy checks refuses the whole finish with a closed reason code and stores nothing.
- **AC-0665** Given an admitted draft, when it is stored and reviewed, then its identity covers its address, claims, cited fact hashes, role definition, producing task and graph content hash; storing it again is idempotent; a review is an atomic compare-and-set recording accepted, rejected or returned; a returned draft admits exactly one successor for the same address, which cannot itself be returned; a stale review revision fails; and the draft, its review and its citations reopen unchanged after a restart.
- **AC-0666** Given accepted drafts, when the curated projection is computed, then each has exactly one state (superseded, unresolvable, stale, conflicted or eligible) with a reason, a draft is stale exactly when a fact it cites has a different content hash than the draft recorded, the curated revision covers drafts and proposals under one identity that any review changes, and the base graph content hash is unchanged.
- **AC-0667** Given one eligible accepted draft, when the export is compiled, then a `best-effort` export prints its claims in the addressed section, each with its citation ids, T3 / InferredWeak, the author role and the review status; a `verified-only` export of the same state prints no claim and reports the number omitted for that section; every cited id resolves in the fact index or the export fails; no projected statement is replaced; and an MCP read of the artifact returns the export's bytes for the same inputs and mode.
- **AC-0668** Given a request to draft one address, when `section-writer@1` runs, then it runs inside the existing investigation limits, consent and cancellation, its citation set is the facts of the section's frame and declared scope by citation id, a valid finish stages a draft and a refused or insufficient-evidence finish stages none, and no other role's definition, prompt fingerprint or saved history changes.
- **AC-0669** Given the Spec Workbench, when a catalogue document is opened, then each section shows its kind, its claims with their annotations and openable citations, the state and reason of any listed draft, and the draft and review actions; accepting, rejecting or returning updates the view from the next curated read.
- **AC-0670** Given an expected set for one SimpleLogin capability set written from the repository before any drafting run and never part of an ingested source, when the accepted drafts for those D1 sections are scored claim by claim by a reviewer who did not produce them, then each claim is recorded as supported, unsupported or missing against its cited sources, and the result is committed whatever it is.
- **Security:** Agents propose only; acceptance never upgrades a tier or writes a fact (R-INT-1, R-INT-3). Egress consent and redaction per step are unchanged. No write to the ingested target.
- **Performance:** A drafting run is bounded by the existing call, byte and time limits; a draft is at most 64 claims and 256 KiB.
- **Trace:** W13 (#1748) · `spec`, `agents`, `context-hub`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0064, ADR-0061, ADR-0058 · T-0663..0670

### US-0048 — Keep authored sections current as the code changes
- **Actor:** Engineer or product owner
- **As a** reader of the recovered documentation **I want** an accepted section to be marked stale, with the changed facts named, when a re-ingest changes what it rests on, and to request a reviewed re-draft **so that** the documents never silently describe code that no longer exists.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Draft
- **AC-0671** Given an accepted draft and a graph in which facts it cites have changed or are gone, when the curated projection is computed, then the draft is reported with the sorted citation ids whose content hash differs from the recorded one and the sorted ids that resolve to no fact, in the curated Workbench read and in `curated.md`; the state rules are unchanged, and the same graph, drafts and reviews always give the same lists.
- **AC-0672** Given two snapshots of a fixture that differ in one handler and accepted drafts for several sections, when the document is compiled against the second snapshot, then exactly the drafts that cite a changed fact are marked stale and every other draft is unmarked, identically on a second run; a best-effort document prints a stale draft in place under a stale mark naming the changed ids and marks each claim that cites one, does not print an unresolvable draft and states how many of its cited ids are missing (`curated.md` names them, so every id the document prints resolves in the fact index), and lists the stale and unresolvable sections in its header; a verified-only document prints no drafted claim and states the same list.
- **AC-0673** Given the Spec Workbench with a stale or unresolvable accepted draft, when the document view is shown, then the draft shows the changed and missing citation ids under its state as the host reported them, each changed id openable as evidence and each missing id shown as text because no fact remains to open, marks each claim that cites a changed id, and offers a re-draft for the same address; a draft in any other state shows neither list nor the offer.
- **AC-0674** Given an accepted draft that is stale or unresolvable, when a re-draft is requested, then an ordinary drafting run is started for the same address and the draft it stages names the accepted draft as replaced; the replaced draft stays accepted and in place until the successor is accepted and is then superseded and kept; rejecting the successor changes nothing else and allows another re-draft; a draft has at most one successor that is not rejected, and a request for a draft in any other state is refused with a closed reason.
- **AC-0675** Given a draft that replaces another, when its change is read, then the host returns the claims added, removed and kept and the cited facts added, removed and changed, computed from the two stored drafts alone, and the Workbench shows them beside the successor before review.
- **Security:** Stale marking is a set comparison with no model call. A re-draft is an ordinary drafting run under the existing consent, redaction and limits; acceptance never upgrades a tier or writes a fact (R-INT-1, R-INT-3). No write to the ingested target and no edit of a target document (NG1).
- **Performance:** The comparison is linear in the cited facts of the accepted drafts, inside the existing curated read bounds.
- **Trace:** W15 (#1749) · `context-hub`, `spec`, `agents`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0045, ADR-0064, ADR-0061 · T-0671..0675

### US-0049 — Read the target's existing documentation as evidence
- **Actor:** Engineer or product owner
- **As a** reader of the recovered documentation **I want** the READMEs, design notes and runbooks of a source read as documents and sections, linked to the facts they name exactly, with broken references listed **so that** I can see what the existing documentation refers to, where it no longer matches the code, and cite it as stated intent.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Draft
- **AC-0676** Given a registered source with Markdown and plain-text files, when it is ingested, then each accepted file is one `Document` fact and each of its sections one `DocSection` fact with its heading, level and byte span, Deterministic and Confirmed, linked by `HAS_SECTION`; headings inside fenced code are not sections; the file, file-count and section bounds hold; and a second ingest of the same commit yields the same facts and the same graph content hash.
- **AC-0677** Given documentation files of which some are too large, not UTF-8 or past the file limit, when the coverage inventory is built, then it counts the files found, read and skipped by closed reason, the sections and the files read to the section bound; a skipped or partly read file makes the inventory limited; the coverage document prints the counts; no count is stated as completeness; and a repository with no documentation file keeps the inventory it had.
- **AC-0678** Given a section whose inline code spans and link targets name a fact id, a repository-relative file path, a route as method and path, or a name that exactly one fact of a named kind carries, when references are resolved, then each is linked by one `MENTIONS` edge citing the reference span and naming the rule; a reference that matches no rule or more than one fact is not linked and makes no finding; and text inside fenced code is never a reference.
- **AC-0679** Given a README that names one route that is served and one that is not, when the source is ingested, then the first is linked at T0 and the second is in the drift register citing the reference span and stating what it was compared with; a route served only with other methods cites those endpoints; a relative link to a file that does not exist is listed with the path looked for; and with no recovered endpoint in the system no route entry is made.
- **AC-0680** Given documents that contradict or restate recovered facts, when they are ingested, then no other fact's tier, confidence, content or content hash changes; the graph and every export hold a section's heading, span and hash and never its text; and a title or heading holding a secret is stored redacted.
- **AC-0681** Given ingested documents with exact references, when the export is compiled, then `documentation-evidence.md` lists each document, its sections and the facts each section mentions with rule and citation ids that resolve in the fact index, and counts the links by rule and the drift entries by kind, in both export modes; and the citation set of a section draft includes the `DocSection` facts that mention a fact in the set, so a documented-intent claim can cite one.
- **Security:** A document is evidence of stated intent only: it never promotes or overwrites a fact (R-INT-1). Section text is read through the redacting source read and is never stored in the graph; headings are redacted before storage. No write to the ingested target (NG1). No model call.
- **Performance:** One pass per file; at most 2,000 files of at most 1 MiB per repository and 512 sections per file; reference resolution is by indexed lookup.
- **Trace:** W16 (#1750) · `ingest`, `spec`, `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0065, ADR-0036, ADR-0058 · T-0676..0681

### Decision #1692 — authenticated analyst diagnostics (US-0032 / AC-0287)

Analyst @22 uses text-free replay locations and a separate
capacity-excluded obligation projection. The foundation provides typed field
paths and one-based sentence ranges, including matches spanning multiple fields.
It preserves the existing joined-prose detector, thresholds and metadata rules;
no rejected model/source text is included in diagnostic output. Capacity-excluded
IDs are collected at the exact hash filter before menu truncation, separately
from other blocked obligations. Exclusion never establishes an attempt,
acquisition or satisfaction.

Analyst @20/@21 prompts and canonical menu bytes remain unchanged. Analyst @22
has a distinct prompt and receipt family, invocation-bound authentication of saved
diagnostics, host workspace charges and missing-history/tamper rejection. The
correction allowance is unchanged. Capacity exclusions are recomputed at the
authenticated ledger cutoff and clear on a new ledger revision; they remain
unresolved, unattempted and unacquired. A clean development result and semantic
review remain separate from release acceptance.

For the @22 witness, admission establishes locator correctness from the
transient refused finish. Reopen can prove only integrity and binding after text
is discarded: an independent immutable response-metadata digest must bind the
response hash, finish hash, refusal ID and step. Missing or mismatched @22
witnesses fail closed; older roles omit the field entirely.
If diagnostic location disagrees with the replay detector, the finish stays
refused and uses generic schema-3 replay feedback, preserving the existing
correction allowance. Sensitive-output, size and invalid-action errors still
propagate; diagnostic fallback never admits a result.

### Decision #1697 — evidence-bound analyst claims (US-0032 / AC-0287)

Analyst @23 (prompt generation 13) checks literal enumeration claims against
its cited captured spans before admitting a finish. Unbound claims and spans
that are not literal enumerations are not applicable; bound false or
unverifiable claims receive text-free sentence locations through the existing
single correction turn and authenticated witness. Unsupported subset or
exception language is unverifiable, not interpreted semantically. Bound quantified
claims outside the grammar are refused with typed feedback, consuming the single
correction turn; this intentional over-refusal also applies to true claims whose
phrasing falls outside the supported grammar.

Default/configured/registered assertions require configuration evidence binding
the named identifier. Titles may resolve a subject only against identifiers in
the same finding's statement or citations: normalized words must uniquely match
a contiguous CamelCase prefix. No synonyms or stemming apply; unresolved or
ambiguous subjects are not applicable. Configuration subject binding is limited
to identifiers with multiple normalized words. Single-word identifiers (for
example, `Redis`) do not bind a subject, even when present in cited source; such
claims are not applicable. This is a known scope limit, not verified configuration.
Limitations are exempt. Default-prefixed
source paths and identifiers may establish the corresponding default identity,
but unrelated co-occurrence does not establish configuration.

Historical @20–@22 behavior and canonical bytes, privacy detectors, limits and
correction allowance remain unchanged. Refused prose is never stored. Golden
regressions bind the r3 findings to the actual captured source span; these local
checks do not establish real-provider or release acceptance.

### Decision #1701 — exact captured reservation and combined corrections (US-0032 / AC-0287)

Analyst @24 uses receipt-bound original and production-redacted serialized source
sizes when available, retaining the 6x fallback for historical captures. No source
read precedes the existing charge/access gate. After a charged digest-verified
read, both actual sizes must fit their reserved values before context installation.
Schema-6 feedback reports all applicable correction locations up to the existing
byte ceiling, with deterministic deduplication and an explicit omitted count.
The single correction allowance, detectors, privacy rules and numeric limits stay
unchanged. Historical @20–@23 bytes and reopen behavior remain unchanged.
Joint r3/r4 capacity measurements and real-model acceptance are separate gates.
See [implementation design](design/analyst24-reservation-feedback.md).

### Decision #1704 / issue #1705 — partial findings after correction (US-0032 / AC-0287)

Analyst @25 may withhold current-finish finding-local unverified enumeration or
configuration claims after the existing single correction. Every other refusal
class stays fatal. Re-derived unmet obligations or no surviving findings fail.
A text-free bound witness and explicit partial disposition survive sealing,
settlement, reopen and export; the result is never silently Supported. Historical
@20–@24 remain unchanged. See [implementation specification](design/1705-withheld-findings.md).

### Decision #1707 — bounded refusal diagnostic sidecars (US-0032 / AC-0287)

New refusal diagnostics use a separate immutable, independently witness-bound
sidecar capped at 4096 complete serialized bytes (ADR-0055). Closed reason codes,
source positions and token classes explain the existing verdict without raw
prose or identifier strings. Overflow records explicit omissions or a capacity
marker. Final withholding describes the current authored proposal. Historical
absence remains readable; deletion or rehashed substitution of a newly bound
sidecar is rejected. Diagnostics must preserve whole-run dispositions,
withholding, model bytes, later capacity and provider counts. Only new records of analyst roles 22–25 receive diagnostics. Earlier roles
remain unchanged; future roles adopt the witness at their own version bump.

### Issue #1729 — bounded enumeration predicates (US-0032 / AC-0287)

In progress; the new grammar is selected only by opt-in analyst @27. Historical
roles through @26 retain their existing verdict and diagnostic behavior. This
change is stacked on #1723 and must merge after it; adoption remains separate.
Given a cited literal transition table, when a finding uses the complete forms
`X is terminal` or `X is terminal and has no outgoing transitions`, the new
grammar accepts exactly an empty X row and rejects a nonempty row with a
text-free literal counterexample. Given `X is the only state that lists Y` or
`X is the sole state that lists Y`, X must contain every named target and no
other row may contain any named target. This does not constrain other targets
in X. Explicit and/or lists use the existing target-list grammar. Additional
prose and unknown subjects are not accepted as these predicates. Original
positions, historical false-universal results, privacy checks and diagnostic
schemas remain unchanged. T-0287 covers synthetic contrasts and diagnostics.
